# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## UI guidance

Apply these rules to every UI change:

1. **No horizontal scrolling.** If a row's contents don't fit, either (a) shrink elements in that row to fit (`min-width: 0`, `flex-shrink: 1`, narrower padding/font on small viewports via media queries), or (b) wrap to a new row and resize the new row's contents to fit. Never solve overflow with `overflow-x: auto/scroll`.
   - **Lane step strips (`.lane-chips`) are a BAR GRID** (this replaced the old single-row scrolling timeline — the prior carve-out is gone). Each `.lane-chips` is a CSS grid `repeat(_BAR_SUBCELLS, 1fr)` where **1 row = 1 bar** = `_BAR_SUBCELLS` (32) sub-cells, fitting the strip width with **no horizontal scroll**. A step spans `round(stepLengthFactor × 32)` cells (one whole note = a full bar/row); a step crossing a bar line **splits into continuation segments** (`.cont-start` head + `.cont-mid`/`.cont-end` clones, marked `.cont-segment`) via `_barGridPlan`. Continuation segments are visual-only (no handlers, excluded from index-based selectors by `:not(.cont-segment)`). The old horizontal sweeping playhead cursor (`_followChipInLane`/`_positionCursorsAtTick`) is disabled — playback shows via the per-chip `.active-loop` highlight. Triplets/odd meters: 32 isn't divisible by 3, so triplet steps round to the nearest cell — they're now MARKED (a ³ badge + amber accent + a bracket tick per group of 3, via `_isTripletStep`/`.triplet`) rather than made exact (an exact grid would need 96 cells). The grid playhead is DONE: a line sweeps each `.lane-chips` strip per bar row, driven by a rAF loop off the audio clock (`_startBarPlayhead`/`_barPlayheadFrame` in 07, positioned from `_intrinsicLoopSec` + the fixed grid geometry).

2. **Audio playback must be smooth and immediate.** Every user-triggered sound (cell press, wrap audition, sequence/loop playback, REST tap, sample preview, recording playback, etc.) should fire with no perceptible lag, no distortion, and no fluctuation. When changing audio code, prefer firing the sound *before* DOM work, keep Tone's `lookAhead` minimal (currently 25 ms, set in `js/bloops/02-wraps.js` — NOT in bloops.html), and don't add unconditional time cushions to interactive triggers — guards for cold-start (suspended `AudioContext`) are fine but should not penalize the warm path. If a change risks audio glitches (under-runs, stagger between voices, dropouts), test it in the browser before declaring done.

3. **Keep the audio signal-flow diagram current.** `README.md` (section *"Bloops — audio signal flow"*) holds an ASCII diagram of how notes route from each source through the two entry buses (`globalSendTap` vs. the per-lane bus) to the shared master chain and speakers. Whenever you change audio routing — `playNote()` destination resolution, `globalSendTap`, `getLaneBus()`, the FX send/return wiring, or the master chain order — **update that diagram in the same change** so it never drifts from the code. Prefer one global routing rule over per-mode volume/FX hacks; if modes sound inconsistent, fix it at the bus/master level (and re-draw the diagram) rather than scaling individual call sites.

4. **New UI elements must match the styling of nearby components — reuse, don't reinvent.** Any control, button, readout, badge, or panel you add should be visually indistinguishable in style from the elements around it: reuse the existing class (`.ambient-ctrl`, `.ambient-seg`/`.ambient-seg-row`, `.ambient-select`, `.ambient-step-btn`/`.ambient-step-inp`, `.ambient-hint`, `.ambient-mod-target`, `.ambient-euclid-*`, `.btn`/`.btn-small`, etc.) rather than writing a new one-off. If a genuinely new component is needed, style it from the same design tokens (colors, radii, padding, font sizes) as its neighbors and match their light/dark behavior — never ship raw/default-styled or mismatched chrome. When unsure which class fits, look at the sibling controls in the same panel/card and copy their markup shape. A new element that looks bolted-on is a bug, not a detail.

## Record learnings here (avoid repeat debug churn)

When a bug or behavior takes more than a couple of attempts to figure out — especially anything non-obvious about this codebase (CSS that overrides JS, event/lifecycle ordering, audio scheduling/lookahead, save/load quirks, the Bloom engine, view-mode rules) — **add a short note to the "Gotchas / hard-won learnings" list below in the same change**, so the next task doesn't rediscover it. Keep each entry one or two lines: the symptom and the fix/cause. Check this list before deep-debugging a UI/audio/state issue.

### Gotchas / hard-won learnings
- **FX-SPAWNED notes (pitch echo) must NOT enter the rolling capture (`E.cap`) — anything in cap gets BAKED into Write/Hold freezes as material.** The capture-sink tee runs for EVERY playNote, including notes an FX spawns mid-emit; captured echo events froze into loops and replayed forever regardless of the FX's on/off ("removed pitch echo, suddenly hearing it"), with fresh spawns doubling on top each rewrite. Rule: notes generated BY an effect carry a marker param (`_pecho`) and are (1) excluded from `arr.push` in `_ambCapSink` (the cancel-tag still runs) and (2) skipped by `_ambReplayFrozen` (the sink regenerates them live from the dry replayed notes — which is also why pitch echo works on frozen/locked loops at all). Any NEW note-spawning FX must follow the same two-place pattern. Diagnose suspected baking by scanning `E.cap[key]`/`E.freeze[key].events` for the marker param.
- **"Seed" was OVERLOADED and is now split in the UI — but the DATA MODEL keys are unchanged (label≠key, per the naming rule).** Three unrelated concepts all said "seed": (A) the area RNG seed `cfg.seed` (`_ambSeed`/`_ambSeededRand`); (B) a layer's note-material source (the Generate|Author|Grid system — `seedmode`, `seedEdit`, `seedPv`, `seedroll`, `seedgrid`, `_ambSeedPreview/Promote/Render`); (C) generation strategy (`rhythmSeed` fill/euclid, `pitchSeed` random/grounded — already labeled "Rhythm"/"Pitch", internal-only). A and B were BOTH user-facing as "Seed" → the "what is Regenerate regenerating?" confusion. Fixed by LABELS only (keys kept for save-compat): (A) "Regenerate" + "#<seed>" → **"🎲 New take"** + **"Take <base36 id>"** (`E.seedId` readout renders `cfg.seed.toString(36)`; framed as a "take" of the area = same arrangement, different random realization); (B) the layer **`['grp','Seed']` group → `['grp','Source']`** (9 schema entries + the `c[1]==='Source'` kit-exemption check in the extras renderer) and `_ambSeedModeHtml`'s inner label "Seed" → **"Mode"**. So: UI "Source" = `seedmode`/`seedEdit`/`seedPv` keys; UI "Variation" = `cfg.seed`. When you add/read these, search the KEY (`seed*`), not the label. If you add a new layer type, its Source group is `['grp','Source']`; the `groupsOpen` persisted key is now "Source" (open-state only, cosmetic).
- **NEVER swap the live Tone context with `Tone.setContext()` — in Tone 14.9.17 it updates `Tone.getContext()` but NOT the `Tone.context` property, and this codebase uses both idioms.** The old weak-device fallback (persisted `bloopsAudioLatency`, writer long removed) did exactly that, splitting the app across TWO live AudioContexts with clocks ~0.5 s apart: fast grid taps were released before their wrong-clock start (freed while still Scheduled = silent — measured 7/15 silent) and all audio exited a ~186 ms output pipeline ("presses laggy, 1 in 5 silent" — machine-persistent, invisible on fresh profiles). The reader is removed and 01-core-state purges the fossil key on load. If a latency mode is ever needed again, set the hint BEFORE Tone loads (or replace every `Tone.context` read) — never swap mid-boot. Diagnose suspected splits by comparing `Tone.context.rawContext.currentTime` vs `Tone.getDestination().output.context.rawContext.currentTime` — any delta = two contexts. Repro trick: clone the user's Chrome profile localStorage (`~/Library/Application Support/Google/Chrome/Default/Local Storage`) into a puppeteer `userDataDir` — field bugs that "never reproduce headless" are often persisted-state bugs.
- **The euclid Pattern grid can load rhythm PRESETS (`_EUC_PRESETS` from 09, the same list the grid "Generate Sequence" dialog offers) — euclidean presets drive the knobs, clave presets store an override.** `_ambEuclidPresetOptions`/`_ambApplyEuclidPreset` (17) group `_EUC_PRESETS` by `grp` (1 World / 2 Rock / 3 African / 4 Clave) into a `<select>` in the Pattern control. A preset with `{k,n,rot}` sets Steps=n, Pulses=k, Rotate=rot and CLEARS the override (the generated euclidean pattern IS the preset, so multi-voice spread still works); a preset with a `pat` string (non-euclidean clave/bell timeline) sets Steps and stores `euclidPattern=[row]` on voice 0. `_EUC_PRESETS` is a top-level `const` in 09 (loaded before 17) so it's visible cross-file — guarded with `typeof` anyway. To fit presets up to n=24 the euclid **Steps max was raised 16→32 and Rotate 15→31** (schema rows + ALL emitter clamps `Math.min(16→32, …steps)` in `_ambEmitEuclidCore`/`_ambEmitArpEuclid`/the readout/`_ambEuclidGridDims`) — harness-safe (defaults are ≤8, so `Math.min(32,8)===Math.min(16,8)`). The grid also shows a **Bars readout**: `_ambEuclidBarInfo` — bar count = **Phrase** (`bars`); when Unit-Synced it appends "· plays in N bars" (Lock-to time-scales the phrase's DURATION, a separate axis from its bar COUNT). If you add a preset kind or change the step cap, update `_ambApplyEuclidPreset` AND keep the four `Math.min(32,…)` step clamps in lock-step.
- **Euclid layers (bass, euclid Beat, euclid Arp) carry an editable step grid spanning the WHOLE PHRASE — `inst.euclidPattern` (per-voice rows) OVERRIDES the generated euclidean pattern, with PER-BAR editing.** All euclid emitters resolve their per-slot pattern through `_ambEuclidPat(inst, pulses, steps, rotate, V, v, salt)` (NOT `euclideanPattern`/`_ambEuclidVoicePat` directly) — it returns `inst.euclidPattern[v]` when that row's length is a nonzero **multiple of `steps`** (one bar-block per Phrase bar, or a single-bar override), else the generated (steps-long) pattern. **Emitters index `pat[(bar*steps+slot) % pat.length]`** so a steps-long pattern REPEATS every bar (the default → byte-identical to before, harness green, ZERO added rng draws) and a phrase-long override plays a different block per bar. The grid (`_ambEuclidCellsHtml`/`_ambWireEuclidGrid`, schema token `euclidgrid`, shown in euclid mode via `_ambBeatGenVis`/`_ambArpEuclidVis`) renders V voice-rows of **`bars`×`steps`** cells (bar groups w/ `.barstart` dividers + `data-bar` badges); each cell's `data-ei` is its ABSOLUTE index `bar*steps+slot`. A cell tap SNAPSHOTS the whole phrase (generated repeats via the same modulo) into a `bars*steps`-length override then toggles. **Steps** change + Regen CLEAR the override (`_ambEuclidRegen` nulls it); Pulses/Rotate also clear; **Phrase (`bars`) and Voices KEEP** it (grid re-renders, editing re-snapshots to the new length). The bars readout re-renders on Timing edits via `_ambRefreshEuclidGrids` (hooked into `_ambSyncLayerUnits`, which Lock-to calls) + direct `bars`/`rate` listeners. Normalized in the one chokepoint (`_ambNormalizeEuclidPattern`) — keeps any non-empty 0/1 rows (length checked at read time). Raising Phrase LENGTHENS the grid. If you add a 4th euclid call site, route it through `_ambEuclidPat` AND use the `(bar*steps+slot) % pat.length` indexing, and keep `_ambEuclidGridDims` (now returns `bars` too) in lock-step with the emitter.
- **A BUS CHOOSES WHERE IT ENTERS THE MASTER CHAIN, NOT WHICH EFFECTS IT USES — the chain is serial and SHARED, so per-effect switches are not physically possible.** `masterBus → DC → Warmth → Vinyl → Compressor → Glue → Volume → Limiter`: by the time the signal reaches Warmth, every bus is already summed into one signal, so no bus can take Warmth, skip Vinyl and rejoin for the Glue without its own duplicate chain. `bus.entry` is therefore an entry POINT — `full` (masterBus) / `postwarmth` (vinylWobble) / `postvinyl` (masterCompressor) / `direct` (masterVolume) — and each option names what it keeps and what it loses. The old `direct: true` boolean still reads as `entry:'direct'`. Measured with Vinyl on (its age lowpass at 5500 Hz): a bus at `postvinyl` keeps **2.7x more high-frequency energy** than the same signal at `full`. NOTE the vinyl CRACKLE bed is additive into the compressor and is global — skipping Vinyl skips the wobble and the age lowpass, not the crackle. **TEST TRAPS:** `globalFx.vinylOn === true` is a STRICT boolean, so setting it to `1` silently leaves vinyl off and every measurement flat (cost a full round of confusing spectra); `routeBloomBus` is a no-op on a bus that does not exist yet, so call `getBloomBus(id)` first; and a raw AudioBufferSourceNode needs `Tone.connect(src, toneNode)`, not `src.connect(...)`.
- **BLOOM MIX BUSES (`layer.bus` + `cfg.buses[id]`) group layers and are the ONLY way Bloom reaches the shared FX returns.** Bloom always summed into one `bloomMasterGain` and, per the signal-flow diagram, bypassed the grid FX sends entirely — so reverb/delay/chorus were unreachable from a Bloom layer. Now each layer names a bus ('a'|'b'|'c'|'d', absent = 'a'), each bus is a Gain in 03-audio-bus-fx (`getBloomBus`/`routeBloomBus`/`setBloomBusSend`, exposed on window), and a bus decides two independent things: `direct` skips the master Warmth/Glue/compressor by landing on `masterVolume` — but NEVER the limiter, since bypassing that is not a creative choice — and per-FX send gains feed `fxSendBus[name]`, the same parallel returns lane playback uses. `routeBloomBus` calls `disconnect()`, which drops the sends too, so it RE-ATTACHES them; forget that and a bus silently loses its FX the moment its routing changes. Bus 'a' with no settings is left completely alone (`_ambBusApply` returns early when `cfg.buses[id]` is absent) — that is what keeps the golden render bit-identical BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by luck; verified 82/82 plus mod parity after the change. The per-layer chain resolves its destination at BUILD time via `_E.busNode(L)`, so changing a layer's bus must tear down and rebuild that layer's mod chain (`_ambKeyOfLayer` → `_ambTeardownMod` → `_ambSyncMods`). **TEST TRAP:** `document.querySelector('[id$="-mixbus"]')` finds the hidden PRIMARY bed card first — scope by `[id^="mix-bloom-<type>-"]` or you will drive the wrong layer and conclude the control is broken.
- **The core-strip reverb-send bus (worklet output 16) is GLOBAL — one summed send, feeding ONE reverb — and must be (re)claimed by the engine that's generating.** Symptom: "turn a layer's Reverb send up, hear no change" on lane/Shape Bloom under core strips: `_ambEnsureReverb` used to connect the bus to the MASTER engine's reverb only (often never built), and only at reverb creation — a pre-worklet claim failed silently and never retried because the reverb caches. Now `connectSend` (03b) routes through a persistent native gain (output 16 itself is never disconnected — the keep-pull sink hangs off it) and `_ambBuildMod`'s core path re-claims the bus on every strip build, so the last engine to build strips owns it. Diagnose send-path deadness with analysers on worklet output 16 (send sum) vs the engine's reverb output: send hot + reverb silent = orphaned bus, both silent = the strip isn't sending.
- **Key-grouped chips MUST render inside a subgrid container — a flex `.key-group-container` silently shrinks them to text width.** Steps sharing a `keyContext` get re-parented into `<div.key-group-container>`; inside a flex box their `grid-column: span N` is ignored, so grouped quarter-note chords drew ~2 cells while playing (and cursoring) a true 1/4 — an audible-vs-visual divergence that ONLY hits key-grouped steps (injected test steps without keyContext looked fine, which hid it). The old `.grid-mode` gate on the subgrid rule died when the bar grid landed, dropping every container into the flex fallback. The rule is now unconditional; the container's column span is clamped to the row (over-span forces implicit columns and shrinks the whole strip) with `grid-row: span ceil(total/32)` for multi-bar groups. Diagnose suspected width bugs by comparing a step WITH vs WITHOUT `keyContext`.
- **NEVER REINTRODUCE synth body-pooling without passing the oscillator-leak test** (the `VOICE_POOL_ENABLED` kill switch and the pool itself were REMOVED in 2026-07 — 04 now builds a FRESH synth per note and the deferred build queue absorbs the construction cost; this entry survives as the reason not to bring pooling back) — pooled retriggers leak RUNNING native OscillatorNodes (1-2/note), and THAT was the dense-project cut-out.** Tone's retrigger bookkeeping cannot reliably stop the previous note's native oscillator when a synth body is reused (native source stops can't be cancelled; restarts orphan running nodes; stopping at pool-release makes it worse). A started-never-stopped OscillatorNode is GC-protected and renders forever: ~25 leaked/s on an all-FM project drowned the render thread in ~12 s (audio clock < 1× wall clock = the device plays gaps = glitch → silence; Stop→Play recovers because voices get disposed). Diagnose node leaks with puppeteer `page.queryObjects(OscillatorNode.prototype)` after `window.gc()` — flat counts good, linear growth = leak. Fresh-synth-per-note + `safeDisposeSynth` leaks zero; construction cost is handled by the deferred voice-build queue, NOT pooling. (This is now simply how it works — there is no flag and no pool.)
- **The dense-project cut-out was RENDER CAPACITY, machine-dependent — the voice budget now ADAPTS to the measured audio clock; don't re-diagnose silence as engine bugs before reading `[bloom-health]`.** On the user's machine the audio clock ran at 0.86× wall clock (device playing gaps = glitch→silence, meters still flashing) with only ~19 plain FM voices — below the static 24-unit cap — and voice tails hit steady-state concurrency ~12 s after Play (bed ≈ 12.3 s lifetime), so it always died "around 12 seconds". The Bloom health watchdog (`_ambHealthStart`, 17) measures ctRate every second with signal probes bracketing the master chain, and feeds `_setVoiceBudget` (04): ctRate <0.95 → shed most-decayed tails to 70% of current cost (floor 8); >0.985×3 s → recover +1 (session-persistent). Console prints `[bloom-health] render behind … voice budget → N`. Diagnostic buffer: `window.__bloomHealthLog`. If a future silence report shows healthy ctRate AND healthy signal probes, THEN hunt routing/NaN — the probes name the dead stage.
- **NEVER connect a modulator signal into a biquad filter param, and never run continuous ramps on one — schedule stepped/glide control-rate curves instead.** Measured in Chrome (audio-clock rate at 24 voices): connected LFO→`filter.frequency` = 0.82× real time, continuous linearRamps = 0.96, stepped/5ms-glide points = 1.00 (free). A signal input (or an always-active ramp) forces per-SAMPLE biquad coefficient recompute; `automationRate='k-rate'` is silently ignored by Blink. So the Design mod-matrix applies cutoff/reso as ONE pre-summed scheduled curve (filter-env + all mods, sampled every 45 ms with 5 ms glides — `_sdBuildModRig`'s `scheduleCurve`, env math shared via `_sdFilterEnvShape`), and per-voice deterministic LFOs for SEQUENCED notes are scheduled `Tone.Signal` automation, not `Tone.LFO` nodes (2 LFO nodes × 24 voices alone = 0.57× real time). Pitch/amp/pan mod stays as connected signals (measured free). Held presses (dur>30 s) keep true LFOs/connected topology — one live voice is affordable. The VOICE CAP is a DSP **budget**, not a headcount: `_voiceDspCost` weights design voices (filter +1, matrix +0.5, sub/ring +0.5, unison +0.15/voice) so plain projects keep the old count exactly while design-heavy stacks settle at a render-thread-safe concurrency.
- **Scheduled-ahead voices BUILD via a deferred time-sliced queue inside playNote — new cancel/stop paths must purge it, and emit-tag globals are captured per entry.** playNote defers construction of any voice with ≥0.25 s lead (all Bloom notes; interactive presses build synchronously) into `_voiceBuildQueue`, drained ~10 ms per 16 ms pump, earliest `at` first (`_vqPump`). This is what actually stops dense-project cut-outs: even POOLED voices cost ~2-3 ms each to wire+trigger, so a 20-voice chord/burst built synchronously in one tick starved Tone's scheduler no matter how cheap each voice got. Notes still sound at their exact `at`; only construction moves. THREE invariants: (1) any new cancel/stop/fade path that sweeps `_pendingVoices` must also `_vqPurge`/`_vqClear` the queue or cancelled notes still build and sound; (2) voice `_ak` tagging is temporal (reads `window._ambEmitKey`/`_ambEmitAt`/`_ambCaptureSink` at build) so entries capture those globals at enqueue and `_vqBuild` restores them around the build — any new emit-time side-channel global read inside `_playNoteNow` needs the same treatment; (3) the capture-sink TEE runs at emit time (before the defer gate) — keep it there or Freeze/Shape-It capture breaks. Urgent entries (<0.3 s to onset) get a BOUNDED 30 ms slice — never unlimited, so a backlog degrades to slightly-late notes instead of a scheduler-starving burst. Kill switch: `VOICE_BUILD_QUEUE_ENABLED`.
- **[SUPERSEDED — synth-body pooling was REMOVED in 2026-07; there is no voice pool. Kept for the history only. Verified: no `_poolKey`, no acquire/release, and 04's own comment reads "Synth-body pooling is permanently gone… NEVER reintroduce without passing an oscillator-leak test". If you are reasoning about cold-start cost, there is no pool to be cold — every note builds a FRESH synth and the deferred build queue absorbs the cost.]** Design patches must POOL their synth bodies, and pool release keys must match acquisition keys. Two dense-project cut-out causes fixed together: (1) `_sdOscNeedsFreshVoice` used to force a fresh voice for any fm/am patch with a finite harmonicity — but EVERY Design patch carries one (default 3), so Design FM/AM built a full synth (~7ms) per note and a bed+motif+riff stack starved the scheduler → silence. FM/AM timbre is now set per acquisition on the pooled body, and unison designs pool under a signature key (`basic:sine:u5:s30` — FatOscillator rebuilds when `count` CHANGES, so configs can't share a bucket); only ring-mod still needs a fresh voice. (2) playNote released pooled synths under the raw `type` while basics acquire under `'basic:'+type` → released synths parked in a dead bucket and basics never actually reused. Release sites now use the `_poolKey` stamped at acquisition — never hand-derive a pool key.
- **Arp direction resolves `entry.dir || layer.dir` — and normalize STAMPS every series entry with its own dir at creation, so a layer-level Direction control is silently outvoted unless it PROPAGATES to the entries.** Symptom: "Direction select does nothing" — `L.dir` was written but every entry already carried `dir:'up'` baked in by the normalize migration (17-ambient ~1607). The `arpdir` change handler now writes `L.dir` AND `L.steps[*].dir` (+ `_ambResetArp` so the new sweep starts clean). General rule: any layer-level control whose emit-side read is `entryValue || layerValue` must either propagate to entries on change, or the entry stamping must stop — check which side normalize backfills before wiring the control. (Same pattern risk: entry `passes`/`unit`/`count`.) Arp dir modes live in THREE places that must stay in lock-step: `_DIRS` in normalize (~1594 — an unknown dir is reset to 'up' on every getCfg!), `_ambArpIndexFor`/`_ambArpPassLen` (the sweep math + pass length), and `_ambArpDirHtml` (the select options). **Synced-arp note rate = `arpRes` Resolution** (`_ambArpResIvSec`): a fixed division ('16' default → every Direction plays at the same speed) vs 'fit' (legacy: cycle squeezed into the locked unit → pass length drives the rate). It's a STRING field normalized in the arp block (NOT in defaults — the numeric backfill trap), applies ONLY when `unit.mode==='sync'` (Free = Unit ms × Rate, harness-pinned), and the effective interval is read in THREE sites that must agree: `_ambEmitArpWindow` (emitter) + both `_ambLayerNextBoundary`/`_ambLayerAudibleBoundary` arp branches (queue/stop boundaries).
- **The EUCLID arp anchors phase in `E.runPhase[key]`; the SERIES arp in `E.arpState[key]` — don't conflate them.** Both are `type==='arp'`, but the euclid arp (`arp.euclid`) is windowed/phase-anchored like Bass/Beat-euclid (`_ambEmitArpEuclid` reads/writes `E.runPhase[key].lastAt`), while the series arp uses `E.arpState[key]`. This bit the Bar-Lock UNLOCK: `_ambResumePhaseAt` (thaw handler) reset only `arpState` for arps, so a thawed euclid arp's `runPhase.lastAt` stayed stale → live gen resumed at `now` and doubled the still-playing captured loop from unlock until the next boundary. Fix: discriminate by `L.euclid` and re-anchor `runPhase[key].lastAt = tA`, CREATING `{startAt:tA,lastAt:tA}` when the locked run left it absent (else the emit rebuilds it with `lastAt=null` → `tFrom=now` → the `hh.at<tFrom` per-hit guard no longer holds the line at the boundary). Any new per-arp phase/state code must branch on `L.euclid` for the store.
- **Bar Lock loop length = the manual `cfg.bars` (a hard override), NOT the LCM.** Early Bar Lock auto-sized the loop to `_ambAreaLoopBars` (LCM of capturable layers) and HID the Bars picker — so Bars did nothing while locked. Now `_ambBarLockSync` captures over `cfg.bars` and `_ambAreaDurSec` = bars×plays (locked or not). `_ambAreaLoopBars` survives only as (a) the "nothing capturable → stay live" guard and (b) the advisory **`◈ N-bar unit`** readout (`.ambient-orch-unit`) beside Bars — grey when `bars % unit === 0`, amber `.warn ⚠` otherwise (a non-multiple cuts a layer mid-cycle at the loop seam = the user's informed choice). If you touch the loop-length math, keep the LCM as advisory only; don't re-hide Bars.
- **Wrap-ensemble = a wrap with PER-NOTE TONES, voiced by Bloom; the LAYER MODE supplies the shape.** Wraps always gave Bloom only PITCH (`intervals`+`root`); the wrap-ensemble adds per-degree TONE. `_ambPublishWrap` captures `wrap.tones[]` (parallel to `intervals`) from the wrap's chord notes that carry `toneOverride`+`sound` (set in the Seed wrap step-editor — already existed). `_ambDegreeToneAt(deg, src)` returns a wrap's per-degree tone (null otherwise). Two delivery mechanisms: (1) SINGLE-note emits read a side-channel — `_ambDegreeFreq` stamps `_ambLastDegTone` on every pick, and bass/run/motif/pedal/texture do `if (_ambLastDegTone) params.type = _ambLastDegTone` right after the pick (works only because the pick immediately precedes the param build, no intervening pick). (2) MULTI-pick emits read INLINE (the side-channel would be stale): bed `_ambPickVoicing` chaos path builds `{f,t}` pairs, sorts by freq, and hangs a parallel `freqs._tones` on the returned array which `_ambEmitBed` applies per voice (= the **Stack** shape; structured Chords/Monk modes use chord FORMS not wrap degrees, so they don't carry tones); arp series uses `_ambDegreeToneAt(d, notes)` with the interval index `d`; arp euclid stores `tone` on each precomputed hit at build (its `deg` isn't in scope at play). **The shape is the layer**: single-note layers → Run (a melodic line, each note its own timbre), bed chord → Stack. **Gated/harness-safe:** every site applies the tone ONLY when non-null, so a non-wrap source (or a wrap with no per-note tones) is byte-identical to before — the note stream (and the invariant harness, which doesn't hash tone) is unchanged. NOT covered: the bed STRUCTURED modes (form-based, not degree-based).
- **A WRAP is stored as PITCH CLASSES (mod-12, deduped), root = the LOWEST pc NUMBER (not the played bass); differing note counts across layers come from per-layer VOICING, not the wrap.** `_ambPublishWrap` folds each freq → midi → `pc mod 12` and dedupes, so two "notes" an octave apart (same pc) collapse to one interval at publish — the only place a genuine chord loses a note. A genuinely-distinct 3-pc chord stays 3 intervals verbatim through `_ambScaleIntervals` (`type==='wrap'` → `w.intervals`). What LOOKS like the wrap dropping notes is the layer voicer: euclid arp `euclidVoices` default 1 plays only the root; bed/drone play `density` voices; `maxPitches` caps the arp. **BED CHAOS-VOICER BUG (fixed):** `_ambPickVoicing` biased 75% toward "chord tones" computed as `[0,2,4,6].filter(d<N)` — right for a 7-note SCALE (root/3rd/5th/7th) but WRONG for a wrap/chord POOL where every note is a chord tone; for a 3-note wrap it collapsed to `[0,2]`, so the middle note appeared in ~10% of voices / absent from ~64% of chords ("only 2 of the 3, octave-spread"). Fix: `chordPool = notes.type==='wrap'|'chord'|'prog'` → `chordDegrees = intervals.map((_,i)=>i)` (all notes are chord tones); scale sources keep `[0,2,4,6]` AND the RNG-draw count is unchanged either way → harness byte-identical (no harness bed uses a wrap/chord source; verified E 10%→34%, balanced ~⅓ each).
- **The euclid BASS (synth render in `_ambEmitEuclid`) walks its SOURCE by scale-degree, and at the default Pitch var 0 it never moves — so a SCALE source plays a monotone root, and a CHORD/wrap source now ARPEGGIATES.** `walkDeg` starts at 0 (root) each cycle and only advanced when `pitchVar` fired → a chord source used to sit on the root ("the chord doesn't distribute"). Fix: `chordPool = srcType==='chord'|'wrap'` → the chord tone is `arpDeg % N` where `arpDeg` is a DETERMINISTIC ORDINAL counting euclidean-pattern hits from the cycle start (root→3rd→5th→…, base octave), and SKIP the pVar walk for chord pools; scale/prog sources keep the melodic proximity walk. **CRITICAL — the ordinal must advance BEFORE the `tFrom` gate (per euclidean hit), NOT per PLAYED note.** The euclid emitter re-emits a cycle progressively across ticks (each tick advances `tFrom`), so a per-played-hit counter (`walkDeg++` after playNote) restarts at the root every tick → the chord distributes for the first bar then collapses to root ("changes stop after a few bars"). PITCH-only (same onsets, same RNG draws) + no harness bass uses a chord/wrap source → harness byte-identical (verified C-maj distributes across ALL bars, write on+off; scale unchanged). NOTE: prog is deliberately EXCLUDED from chordPool (prog-bass is harness-pinned). The Bed had the analogous bug (chord-tone assumption `[0,2,4,6]` vs a chord pool where every note is a chord tone).
- **Melodic-euclid STEP EDIT: a per-step override modal (`inst.stepFx["i"] = {note,tone,vel,len,rat,prob,pan}`, sparse, absent-by-default → harness byte-identical).** A "✎ Step" toggle on the euclid Pattern (`_ambEuclidCellsHtml` meta row, non-kit only, state in `inst._stepEdit`) flips a cell press from toggle-on/off to opening `_ambBassStepModal` — a Grid-style editor (Note = absolute MIDI or Auto/pattern, Tone = `_ambToneOptions`, Volume/Length/Ratchet/Probability/Pan). Accessors `_ambBassStepFxGet/Set/Custom` (distinct from the drum `pg.fx` store which is per-page/voice); `_ambNormalizeStepFx` coerces+drops-defaults at both normalize sites. The bass synth render in `_ambEmitEuclid` reads `sfx = _ambBassStepFxGet(inst, absI)` per hit: `note`→override freq, `tone`→`bp.type`, `vel`/`len`→scale, `rat`→ratchet retrigger loop, `prob`→rng-gate (gated on `sfx` so ZERO draws by default), `pan`→override (still calls `_ambLayerPan` for RNG parity). Modal is a `.sm-overlay` (already exempt from the view-mode hide rules) shown via inline `display:flex !important`; edits apply on `change` + `_ambReanchorLayer` (rewrites a playing Write loop so the edit is heard). `.has-fx` amber dot marks overridden steps (reused from the drum grid). Only the euclid BASS is wired today; euclid arp/beat could reuse the same accessors + modal. **NOTE READOUT + "Random from set":** melodic-euclid grids render a note strip UNDER the cells (`_ambEuclidCellsHtml`, non-kit only, `.ambient-euclid-notes` — same `--eucols` as the cells so it lines up; 1-bar aligns cell-for-cell, multi-bar wraps as one block below) naming each ACTIVE step's note via `_ambEuclidNoteLabel`: an explicit Step-Edit note → that note (`.set`), a chord/wrap source → the arpeggiated chord tone at this hit's ordinal (`.gen`, deterministic), a scale/notes source → the root it wanders from (`~C2`, `.walk`, since Pitch var walks it per cycle), or **🎲** for `note:'rand'` (`.rand`). The Step-Edit Note dropdown gains a "🎲 Random from set" option → stores `note:'rand'` (Get/Set preserve the string sentinel — the old `Number.isFinite` coercion would drop it). The emit draws that step's degree from the source pool via a DEDICATED `_ambSeededRand(hash(absI,ctx.c,seed))` (NOT `ctx.rnd` — an isolated draw shifts no other draw, so adding it leaves the rest of the layer byte-identical AND keeps it native-loop-eligible): stable across a cycle's lookahead ticks, EVOLVES per cycle (so it plays different set notes over time instead of freezing to one value — verified 3 distinct notes across cycles while sibling walk steps stay put). Harness/golden byte-identical (rand is opt-in; no default config uses it). Only the euclid BASS renders the strip/rand today. **All euclid PATTERN edits now take effect at the NEXT UNIT BOUNDARY** while playing: the grid cell-toggle handler calls `_ambReanchorLayer(E, key)` after persist (verified it moves a frozen Write loop's `pendingThawAt` to the next boundary), consistent with Regen (`_ambEuclidRegen`) and the pulses/steps/rotate sliders which already re-anchor.
- **A layer replaying a frozen WRITE loop IGNORES generative edits (Notes source / register / range / density) until the whole `times`-play cycle ends — the freeze gate short-circuits the live emitter.** Every layer defaults to `write:{on:true,bars:2,times:4}` (`_ambDefaultLayer`), so after the first phrase it FREEZES and `_ambFreezeGate`→`_ambReplayFrozen` replays captured fixed-freq events and RETURNS before `_ambEmit*` runs. `_ambEmitRun`/etc. read the source live, so a non-frozen layer WOULD reflect the change — but the freeze wins. The Notes menu's `apply` only set `L.notes`+`_ambSyncMods()` (no invalidate), unlike the arp path which calls `_ambResetArp`; and `_ambReanchorLayer` re-anchored the phase but never the freeze (moot). Symptom: "Riff plays the same run verbatim, ignores the Notes/range change." Fix: **`_ambReanchorLayer` now calls `_ambRewriteSoon(E,key)`** which brings the Write rewrite forward to the NEXT loop boundary (`pendingThawAt`/`_writeRearmAt` = next boundary if currently later; gated to `_write` so a manual Hold freeze stays frozen), + the layer's src-chip change wraps its afterChange with `_ambReanchorLayer`. The gate then thaws → live-generates 2 bars → re-captures a fresh phrase from the new source. NOT `_ambMaskEditPoke` — that only sets `pendingThawAt` when `null` (chord-mask edits are read LIVE during replay so they need no rewrite; a source change needs a full regen). Only fires while playing → harness/golden untouched. (Verified: CEG→DFA wrap, captured phrase pcs `[0,4]`→thaw→`[2,5]`.)
- **A DETERMINISTIC euclid layer must NOT run Write — it already loops natively, and the redundant Write capture/replay HANDOFF drops the seam notes (the "unit boundary skip" glitch).** A euclid emitter (`_ambEmitEuclid`, bass / euclid-arp / euclid single-drum beat) re-emits the same pattern every cycle, phase-locked to the bar grid; with no per-cycle RNG draw it's byte-identical each cycle. Layering Write on top (default `write.on`) captures cycle 0 and REPLAYS it — a second loop mechanism whose live→replay + rewrite-boundary handoffs drop the notes near the loop end (`_ambReplayFrozen` window edges), heard as a non-smooth skip between loops (reliably on the first handoff after Play, then intermittently). Fix: `_ambEuclidDeterministic(L)` gates `_ambWriteSync`'s per-layer `one()` to SKIP Write for a euclid layer when every per-cycle draw is zero — `rhythmVar`/`ghosts`/`pitchVar`/`lenVary` all 0, `_ambEffRest(L)===0` (layer restProb + the Area Groove density macro), and no Step-Edit `prob<100`. **euclidKit (drum-lanes) is now INCLUDED via a kit-aware branch (was excluded → it hit the SAME seam-cluster glitch: the first freeze→replay boundary re-emitted the whole phrase at once, collapsing steps near the seam into one instant — heard as a boundary "thunk" + dropped active steps).** The kit grid plays EXACTLY as drawn — rhythmVar/rests are forced off in the emit (`rVarEff`/`restEff = kitPat?0:…`) and pages cycle deterministically by cycle index (`_ambEuclidActivePage`, no random order) — so it's byte-identical-loopable UNLESS it has Ghosts, lenVary, or a per-step `prob<100`; the kit's step-FX live in `pg.fx` (NOT `L.stepFx`), so the branch scans EVERY page's `pg.fx` for `prob<100`. If it already holds a Write loop, thaw it back to native (same teardown as the toggle-off path). A euclid WITH variance KEEPS Write (native re-rolls each cycle; Write locks ONE realization — that's the point of Write). Verified: default chord bass no longer arms Write (`_write:false`) and every complete native loop is 10/10 notes (was dropping the last 2 at the seam); bed/motif/varying-euclid/drum-lanes untouched; only fires in the live tick → harness/golden byte-identical. Loop=Off was always smooth (native) — that was the tell. **`_ambEuclidDeterministic` is (despite its name) the GENERAL "loops natively → skip Write" gate — it now also covers the non-euclid WINDOWED phrase layers, which are worse than the euclid DROP: their bespoke emitters DOUBLE at the Write handoff (the arp doubled EVERY note; run/pedal a ~1 s burst).** (1) **Series arp** (`_ambEmitArpWindow`, `arpState`) is a CONTINUOUS monotonic `idx` stream, not a looped phrase → `arp && !euclid` skips Write unless `rateVar`. (2) **Run** (`_ambEmitRun`): base degrees AND rests are `baseRnd`-seeded (id+seed → FIXED every cycle), so Rests DON'T break determinism — only per-cycle `vRnd` draws do; skip unless `vary`/`lenVary`/`ornament`/`slide`. (3) **Pedal** (`_ambEmitPedal`): its Rests use a PER-CYCLE `cRnd` (it "roams"), so skip only when `vary` AND `_ambEffRest` both 0. (4) **Drone** (`_ambEmitDrone`): under an ACTIVE progression (resolved source `type === 'prog'`, incl. the inherited global) skip Write UNCONDITIONALLY — variance is a per-cycle seeded draw, so the native loop still varies, and Write's freeze pinned the drone to ONE chord's capture while the changes moved on ("same note over and over", with density/degree edits silently ignored because the freeze gate blocks the emitter — the gate's own comment stated this but only acted at variance 0). Returning true also THAWS an already-frozen drone via the caller's thaw branch, so stuck saved projects self-heal on play. Non-prog drones keep the old rule: skip unless `timeVary`/`pitchVary`. Stepped layers (bed/motif/random-beat) and the shared `_ambEmitStepGrid` windowed layers (texture, euclid bass/beat/arp) DON'T double. Since the harness stubs playNote / bypasses Write, all of these are harness/golden byte-identical. **MEASUREMENT CAVEAT (learned the hard way):** the "run/pedal doubles a few notes with variance on" was a TEST ARTIFACT — a capture wrapper that recorded the note BEFORE `playNote`'s `_ambEmitCutoff` gate (`04:3285`) DROPS it, so it counted cutoff-dropped (silent) look-ahead voices as audio. With a corrected capture (exclude `at >= window._ambEmitCutoff`), stochastic run/pedal/arp show ZERO real doubles and clean even spacing — the cutoff gate already prevents the live+replay overlap. When verifying Write timing, always exclude cutoff-dropped notes.
- **The prog-synced VOICING cluster (Subdivide · merge · Feel · Variety) is lifted onto the Scheduler headline, but the fields stay PER-LAYER.** `progSubdiv` / `progMerge` / `progFeel` / `voiceVariety` live on each bed and drone (two pads under the same progression can legitimately differ), so they belong to the layer — they are NOT progression-card settings; moving them there would make them area-wide and repeat the drone "Edit notes" mistake (a per-layer editor writing shared harmony). What the Scheduler adds is REACH: the cluster renders where the chord-lock is visible, and every control mirrors back onto the card via `_ambSyncControls`. **CSS TRAP that cost a pass: `.ambient-sched-grp { flex: 0 0 auto }` is declared LATER in bloops.css than the voicing rules, so a single-class override loses the cascade** — the Variety slider could neither shrink nor wrap and ran straight off the right edge on a phone. The fixes use COMPOUND selectors (`.ambient-sched-grp.ambient-sched-voi`). Verified 1400→360px: zero descendant overflow, no page scroll; the slider squeezes to 45px at 414px and takes its own row below that. Any new slider needs `.ambient-sl` (this one has it) or it gets no touch handling at all.
- **Loop/Write is EDITED in the ⏱ Scheduler now, not the layer card.** The card's `_ambLoopCtrl` is a read-only BADGE (`.ambient-loop-badge`, text from `_ambLoopStateText(mode, effW, live, inherited)` — Off / ⟳ Write X×Y / range when Vary / ⚡ for Live / ↳ for an inherited Area Write); `_ambLoopSyncAll` just repaints the badge on freeze transitions. Each Scheduler layer row (`_ambRenderScheduler`) owns the full control — **the write on/off is the EVOLVE 3-way segmented selector** (`.ambient-sched-ev`, data-ev `cont`/`every`/`lock` — Continuous = write off · Every N = write on · 🔒 Locked = `write.lock`, freeze one roll forever), NOT a single "Loop button" (`.ambient-sched-writebtn` no longer exists — a stale reference here already misdirected one user-facing answer). With Every N active the detail controls appear: `.ambient-sched-vary` (+ `.ambient-sched-bmin/bmax/tmin/tmax` range inputs), `.ambient-sched-live`, the fixed `.ambient-sched-bars`/`.ambient-sched-times` (cycles when a prog is active), and the tuple-`seq` chips — wired in the `#…-sched-body` delegated click/input handlers. **Hold** stays on the header ❄ (`.ambient-freeze-btn` / `_ambFreezeCycle`), independent of the card. (There can be MANY `.ambient-loop-badge` on screen — one per layer card — so a test that reads `querySelector('.ambient-loop-badge')` gets the FIRST layer's, not the one you changed; scope to the card.)
- **`max-retries exceeded` from every lftp command, with the port test PASSING, is an IP BLOCK — not a broken script and not a network fault.** GoDaddy's brute-force protection blocks an IP by ACCEPTING the TCP connection and then resetting it before the FTP banner, so `nc -z` reports the port reachable and lftp then fails every mirror/mkdir/put with the useless "max-retries exceeded" (and hangs mid-`FEAT negotiation`). Diagnose in two commands: `printf 'QUIT\r\n' | nc -w 8 HOST 21 | wc -c` — **0 bytes = reset = blocked** (a healthy server sends a `220` banner) — and `curl -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://HOST/`, which will return 200, proving the host is fine and it is FTP-for-this-IP. Repeated deploys in one session are enough to trip it. **The block is IP-LEVEL, not FTP-specific** — measured the same accept-then-reset on 990 (implicit FTPS, `errno=54` mid-TLS-handshake), 2222 and 21098, while http:// answered 200, so there is NO port to fall back to and no script change can route around it. Remedy, fastest first: deploy from a different public IP (tether to a phone hotspot — a new IP is not blocked); else wait ~15-60 min; else clear it in cPanel (Security → IP Blocker). deploy.sh's pre-flight now READS THE BANNER instead of only opening a socket, and prints that diagnosis with the machine's public IP, so this fails in two seconds with the real reason instead of a confusing cascade. The interrupted-run damage to watch for is nil by design: the `EXIT` trap restores `DEV_BYPASS_SIGNIN=true` and removes the stage dir even on ^C.
- **NEVER PUT A BACKTICK (or `$( )`) INSIDE deploy.sh's lftp HEREDOC — not even in a COMMENT.** That heredoc is `<<EOF`, unquoted by necessity (`$STAGE_DIR`/`$REMOTE_DIR` must expand), so the shell runs command substitution over the ENTIRE body before lftp sees it, and `#` means nothing to the shell there. Prose backticks around setting names shipped exactly this, mid-deploy: `reconnect-interval-base: command not found`, `reconnect-interval-multiplier: command not found`, and a swallowed `mirror` line. Write setting names bare in those comments. Verify any change to that block by rendering it through a fake `lftp() { cat; }` and checking BOTH the emitted commands and an EMPTY stderr — `bash -n` does NOT catch this (it parses fine; the damage is at expansion time).
- **DEPLOY SLEEPS = lftp's reconnect backoff, and parallel transfers BUY those sleeps on this host.** `net:reconnect-interval-base` was 5 s with a growing multiplier — when the server refuses/drops a connection lftp WAITS, and shared hosting refuses routinely (per-user connection caps), so that path is hot, not exceptional. Now 2 s with the multiplier pinned to 1. `--parallel=3` was tried and is exactly wrong here: the extra connections get refused and each refusal costs a multi-second sleep — parallel now defaults to 1 (`DEPLOY_PARALLEL=n` to experiment). The local half is ~0.3 s for all 18 MB, measured — a slow deploy is ALWAYS the FTP session. `deploy.sh` prints per-phase seconds and a total (`Deployment complete in 1m 12s`); `DEPLOY_DRYRUN=1` asks the mirror what it WOULD send and exits, which answers the only diagnosable question (is it re-sending unchanged files?); `DEPLOY_DEBUG=1` restores lftp's `-d`, previously always on and pure noise.
- **KEEP THE STAMPED-FILE LIST SMALL — every file carrying a `?v=DEPLOYVER` token is rewritten AND force-uploaded on EVERY deploy, changed or not.** `js/bloops/17-ambient.js` was added to that list for ONE Worker URL and made every deploy push **2.2 MB** it had never pushed before — about 12x the entire rest of the forced payload (0.22 MB), over FTP to shared hosting. Reported as "why does deploy take so long now", and it was exactly that. The stamp now lives in `window.__BLOOPS_ASSET_V`, published by bloops.html (92 KB, already forced), and JS that builds its own asset URL reads it: `new Worker('…/learn-voice.worker.js' + (self.__BLOOPS_ASSET_V || ''))`. The 2.2 MB module is back to being mirrored by size like the other 37 — uploaded when its bytes actually change. RULE: if new JS needs a cache-busted URL, read the global; do NOT add the file to deploy.sh's sed list unless it is small.
- **Player/app JS must carry a `?v=` cache-bust, or mobile serves stale copies forever.** `player.html` + `bloops.html` load `js/*.js` with no build step and long-lived cache headers, so a file without `?v=` (or with an un-bumped one) is cached indefinitely on mobile Safari/Chrome — a shipped change silently doesn't reach phones while desktop (fresher cache / hard-refresh) looks fine. Symptom class: "works on desktop, broken on mobile" for player behavior. When you change `playlist-manager.js` / `app.js` / `music-player.js` / `blob-cache.js` etc., bump the `?v=` on that tag in BOTH `player.html` AND `bloops.html` (the Listen view loads the same JS). Only reaches phones after a DEPLOY. (Found: the A–Z playlist sort lived in `playlist-manager.js`, which had NO `?v=` at all → mobile kept the pre-sort file → tracks stayed in Drive's arbitrary order.) **DEPLOY BUG that froze the stamp (2026-07-18):** `deploy.sh` stamps `?v=DEPLOYVER` → `?v=<14-digit timestamp>` in the HTML (+ `03b-core-voices.js`), but the `lftp mirror --ignore-time` compares by SIZE ONLY. A stamp-only change is the SAME BYTE LENGTH (14→14 digits), so the mirror SKIPS those files — the stamp froze at the last size-changing edit (`20260717161703`) and every later deploy shipped new JS *content* under the OLD `?v=`, so returning mobile browsers kept serving the cached stale JS. Fix: `deploy.sh` now force-`put`s the stamped files (index/bloops/player/artwork/game.html + 03b-core-voices.js) after the mirror, like `config.js`. If you edit HTML content the size changes and it uploads anyway; the force-put covers stamp-only deploys. Verify a deploy by curling the live HTML and checking `?v=` matches today's timestamp — not just that the JS files re-uploaded.
- **`.ambient-select` is `width: 100%` — inside an AUTO-SIZED inline-flex group it resolves CIRCULARLY and overflows its own container.** The group sizes itself from the select's intrinsic width, then the select lays out at 100%-of-group (capped by any max-width) — WIDER than the slot the group reserved — so it sticks out of the group box and sits UNDER the next sibling (the Scheduler's Unit select rendered beneath the Loop button). `max-width` alone doesn't fix it; give the select an EXPLICIT `width` (e.g. `.ambient-sched-unit { width: 84px }`) or put it in a sized cell. Diagnose with getBoundingClientRect: child.right > group.right = self-overflow. Same class of trap for any width:100% control (inputs) dropped into `flex: 0 0 auto` inline-flex wrappers.
- **`position: sticky` is silently DEAD anywhere in this app** — `html` AND `body` both carry `overflow-x: hidden` (deliberate overscroll-gesture containment), which makes body a non-scrolling scroll container: sticky engages against body while the viewport does the scrolling → the element just scrolls away, computed style still says `sticky`. Use `position: fixed` + reserved flow space (body padding) instead, and verify with an actual scroll test (`window.scrollTo` then check `getBoundingClientRect().top === 0`) — checking `getComputedStyle(...).position === 'sticky'` proves nothing.
- **ALL TESTING USES CHROME BY DEFAULT — the user does not use Safari, on any device.** Their desktop browser is Chrome (and the Electron shell), their phone browser is Chrome on iPhone. Headless verification stays on puppeteer + Chrome as it always has. The ONE exception, invoked deliberately and only when debugging an iPhone-specific failure: iPhone Chrome is WEBKIT under Apple's engine mandate, so a WebKit-only bug (the mobile-TTS phonemizer hang) cannot reproduce in any Chrome anywhere — desktop Safari is then a diagnostic PROXY for the phone's engine, not a supported target. Never treat Safari as a platform to validate features against; treat it as the local stand-in for "iPhone" when that specific question is on the table.
- **PARAMETER SLIDERS TAKE OVER TOUCH GESTURES — native `<input type=range>` is bad on a phone for three separate reasons, and the third is destructive.** (1) the thumb is a small target; (2) with no `touch-action` the browser can steal a slightly-off-horizontal drag for page scrolling, which is the "my finger is on it but it won't move" feeling; (3) **tapping the track JUMPS the value**, so a mis-tap does not merely fail — it wrecks the setting. One delegated `pointerdown` on `document` for `input.ambient-sl` handles touch and pen: grab ANYWHERE, move by DELTA (never jump), vertical distance divides the travel (`1/(1+|dy|/45)` — measured ~3.7× finer at 120px away), and a press with no drag opens numeric entry. **MOUSE IS DELIBERATELY LEFT NATIVE** so desktop click-to-position still works — verified `defaultPrevented === false` on a mouse pointerdown. **The input element is never replaced**: we set `.value` and dispatch real `input`/`change`, so all ~120 sliders and every existing bind/sync/readout work unchanged — wrapping rather than replacing is what made this one handler instead of 120 edits. **Arm on CUMULATIVE travel from the press, not the per-move delta** — a slow drag arrives as many sub-pixel moves that never cross a threshold individually (my first version tested the delta and also overwrote `lastX` before comparing, so the test was always 0). `.ambient-sl` also gets `touch-action: none` and a 26–30px thumb.

- **Every new UI control MUST be validated on a mobile TOUCH viewport before it's considered done — this is a primary target, not an afterthought.** Bloops is used heavily on phones, and "works on desktop, broken on mobile" is a recurring class here. Verify in Chrome with `page.setViewport({width:390,height:780,isMobile:true,hasTouch:true})` against `http://localhost:3001/bloops.html` (use `puppeteer-core` + `/Applications/Google Chrome.app` — see the throwaway `_*.mjs` scripts): open the relevant panel, exercise the control (dispatch a real `input`/`change`, or `.click()` a checkbox), and assert the config actually mutated AND there are no `pageerror`s. Design rules for touch-safety: (1) prefer **delegated listeners on the host** (`hostEl.addEventListener('input'/'change', … closest('.selector'))`) over per-element binds — they survive card re-renders and behave identically on mobile; (2) give sliders/checkboxes big tap targets + `touch-action: manipulation` (or `none` for drag surfaces); (3) real `<input type=checkbox>`/`change` events are more reliable than click-toggled custom widgets. Note a genuine mobile *breakage* is often a STALE DEPLOY (see the `?v=` cache-bust gotcha) rather than a code bug — reproduce locally in a mobile viewport first; if it passes, it's the deployed bundle, not the code.
- **Body-attached overlays get force-hidden by view-mode CSS.** Rules like `body.<mode> > *:not(#mix-view):not(.ctx-menu) { display:none !important }` (and the `tracks-fullscreen` family) hide any element appended to `<body>` that isn't exempt. A selector `!important` beats a normal `.open{display:block}` rule, so toggling a class won't show it. Fix: set `display` via **inline `!important`** (`el.style.setProperty('display','block','important')`); clear on close. Diagnose with a toast of `getComputedStyle(el).display` + `document.body.className` — but note **`.bloops-toast` itself was a victim** (every toast was invisible on Grow/Listen/Harvest, so e.g. the ●/🎤 rec buttons' "Press play first" guidance silently vanished — "the button does nothing"); all three view hide rules now carry `:not(.bloops-toast)`. A NEW body-appended overlay needs its own `:not()` in those three rules or the inline-`!important` fix. (Don't trust `offsetParent` to test toast visibility — it's `position:fixed`, offsetParent is always null; read `getComputedStyle().display` + the rect.)
- **`position:fixed` overlays drift** because the app uses `backdrop-filter` on some ancestors, which makes one a *containing block* — so `getBoundingClientRect`-derived `top/left` are offset (and shift with scroll). Fix: **measure-and-correct** — set the overlay to `0,0`, read its rect (= the containing-block offset), then subtract it from the desired viewport coords.
- **Live-readout chips must not be `<button>` / focusable.** The notes readout repaints on a timer, so a `click` (which needs mousedown+mouseup on the *same* element) gets dropped when the element is recreated between press and release; and focusing a control inside an `aria-hidden` line is blocked. Fix: render chips as non-focusable `<span role="button">` and act on **`pointerdown`**, not click.
- **Opening a `showCtxMenu` from a `pointerdown` handler needs a `setTimeout(…, 0)` — otherwise the menu dismisses itself the instant it opens.** `showCtxMenu` arms its own document-level `pointerdown` dismiss listener. A listener REGISTERED mid-dispatch on a node the event has not reached yet (document is above the element that opened the menu) still fires for THAT same event, so the menu is created and torn down inside one dispatch. Invisible on `click`-driven openers (the ⚄ picker button was fine); it only bites the `pointerdown` openers the live-readout-chip rule above forces on repainting strips — the progression overview's ＋ Part is one. Symptom is an opener that "does nothing" while calling the same function by hand opens the menu perfectly, which is the tell: dispatch-order bug, not a wiring bug. Deferring one tick is the whole fix. NOTE for tests: the prog EDITOR (`#ambient-prog-editor`) also dispatches on `pointerdown`, so a `.click()` on a `data-pe` button silently does nothing — dispatch a real `PointerEvent`.
- **A `<select>` that doubles as a live playback readout must NOT overwrite an explicit user selection.** The Seq Tone dropdown live-reflects the currently-playing step's captured voice via `_ambSeqReflectTone` (called each loop from the seq scheduler). That's only correct in **Grid-voice mode** (`seq.tone === ''`, where the select IS a readout of each step's voice). When the user sets an EXPLICIT layer Tone, that tone is the sound of every note, so the reflection must be gated off (`!(seq.tone && seq.tone !== '')`) — otherwise it clobbers the pick every loop (symptom: "set sine, it reverts to sawtooth but I still hear sine" — audio right, display wrong). `_ambSeqStepTone` already returns `seq.tone` first, so the initial render was fine; only the live reflect needed the guard. Same class as the live-readout-chip gotcha above: a control that's also a live readout needs a "don't stomp an explicit value" guard.
- **The dominant edit latency is the WRITE FREEZE, not the unit boundary — and a frozen loop used to freeze the SOUND along with the phrase.** Every layer defaults to `write:{on:true,bars:2,times:4}`, so seconds after Play it is replaying a captured phrase, and `_ambReplayFrozen` reused the CAPTURED `e.params`. Measured on a default Bed: turning Attack up changed nothing for **19.3 s** (the rest of the Write cycle) — with OR without a re-anchor, because `_ambReanchorLayer`'s `_ambRewriteSoon` half often runs before the freeze is even armed, so there is nothing to bring forward. That splits the params along a MUSICAL line, not a technical one: **PHRASE** (which notes, how many, when — register/density/rests/steps/humanize) SHOULD hold while frozen, that being the point of freezing, and keeps the boundary re-anchor; **SOUND** (how each note is shaped — envelope + glide) is refreshed from the layer at replay via `_ambLiveVoiceParams` and needs no re-anchor at all, since the generator reads it at each note's build. Isolated A/B with the freeze state pinned identical (thaw 19.3 s both runs): never → **1.33 s**, i.e. the lookahead. THREE traps: (1) the `_live` (`loopVar==='live'`) branch must clone from `pp`, NOT `e.params`, or it silently discards the refresh; (2) **do NOT refresh `detune` from `lyr.fine`** — Motion ADDS a seeded random offset onto the same `params.detune` (17:~7352), so that would flatten the motion jitter on every frozen loop; only fields with a SINGLE writer are safe to refresh; (3) `_ambLiveVoiceParams` returns null when nothing differs, so the untouched path replays the captured object by reference and allocates nothing. Routing lives in `_ambCommitParam` (`_AMB_LIVE_NODE_PARAMS` → already live on the DSP chain → do nothing; `_AMB_LIVE_NOTE_PARAMS` → rewrite-soon only; everything else → boundary re-anchor). Playback-only, so golden/harness are untouched.
- **THE COLD-SAMPLE "SINE STAND-IN" MUST NOT REACH A GENERATED OR LONG NOTE — that is the "Bed sometimes plays a weird loud piercing sustaining sine instead of the piano" bug.** Both sample paths in 04 (`_playNoteNow`'s scheduled branch and `startSustainedNote`'s held branch) end with `return playNote(freq, {...params, type:'sine'}, durationMs, …)` when the sampler is not `.loaded`. That is deliberate INTERACTIVE feedback — a tap on a cold sample makes some sound instead of nothing — and it is fine for a 300 ms tap. It is badly wrong for a Bed, which passes its own long Length straight through: measured on `sample:piano`, the first chord played **6 notes of 9000 ms sine** before the sampler finished loading. **This got much worse when samplers became LAZY** (see the entry above): everything used to be built at boot, so the not-loaded branch was rare; now it fires on the FIRST note of every cold sample, and that note is a whole bed chord. `_wantSineStandIn(durationMs)` gates both sites — false when `window._ambEmitKey` is set (any Bloom-emitted note) or the note is longer than `_SINE_STANDIN_MAX_MS` (1500). Reading `.sampler` upstream has ALREADY kicked the lazy load, so returning silently costs exactly one note and the next is correct, which is the contract that entry already documents ("silent that once"). Verified: bed on a cold piano goes 6 sines → 0 with the same 9 sample notes; a short interactive tap still gets its stand-in; a long interactive note and a generated note get none. NOT attempted: deferring the note until the buffer lands — Bloom schedules ~1.4 s ahead so it might often make it, but that is a real change to the build path rather than a fallback fix.

- **SAMPLERS ARE REGISTERED, NOT LOADED — `sampleSamplers` entries carry `sampler` as a lazy GETTER.** Constructing a `Tone.Sampler` fetches its urls immediately, and every built-in instrument plus every `samples/manifest.json` entry used to be constructed at boot: measured **837 audio requests / 37.8 MB on every page load**, by every device, before a note could sound. Now `_defineLazySampler(entry, build)` installs an accessor that builds on first read and then replaces itself with the value — which is the whole design, because ~38 call sites do `sampleSamplers.get(id).sampler` and NONE of them changed. Measured after: **1 request / 0.1 MB** (the grain buffer, deliberately still eager — one file, and its reason for existing is that per-note GrainPlayers find a cached buffer). Reading `.sampler` is also the last-resort trigger: a note on a cold sample starts the fetch and is silent that once, then correct forever. **Three warm-up paths exist so that fallback is never reached**: (1) a CAPTURE-phase `change` listener on `document` warms any `sample:<id>` a `<select>` lands on — one hook that survives every card re-render and any stopPropagation, far more robust than wiring each tone select; (2) an idle pass (`warmSamplesForWorkspace`) regexes `"sample:<id>"` straight out of the stored workspace JSON — deliberately NOT a call to `collectSampleIdsFromTree`, to avoid a load-order dependency on 11-modes-persistence; (3) `ensureSampleLoaded(id)` / `window.__warmSamples(ids)` for anything else. Verified: a project referencing two samples has exactly those two built and loaded at boot, Bloom then plays 4 notes on a LOADED sampler and 0 cold, and an unreferenced voice stays cold. **TRAPS**: `window.__sampleStats()` must read the property DESCRIPTOR, never `.sampler`, or the diagnostic builds all 155; anything that iterates the map (the voice pickers, `isSliceableSample`, the pad builder) must stay on metadata (`name`/`urls`/`drumKit`/`imported`) — they all do; and the getter is ENUMERABLE, which is safe only because nothing spreads or JSON-serialises an entry (the `{...meta}` spreads in 11-modes-persistence are on IndexedDB records, not these). The OFFLINE EXPORT is unaffected — it builds its own parallel bank from `info.urls`/`info.baseUrl` and never reads `.sampler` (though it still builds ALL of them for a render, which is pre-existing and worth revisiting).
- **Bloom edits land one iteration late** unless you account for the ~1.4 s lookahead: the next iteration's voices are already scheduled with old values. To apply on the next iteration, cancel the layer's scheduled-ahead voices and re-anchor/re-emit. Use `cancelBloomFutureVoices(key, fromAt)` (voices are tagged with `_ak`/`_akAt`) to drop *only* the next iteration so the current one finishes intact. `_ambReanchorLayer(E, key)` packages this ("apply a generation/per-note edit at the layer's NEXT unit boundary"): every per-note slider (`sl`/`st`/`tm`, gated by `!_AMB_LIVE_NODE_PARAMS`) and `speed` calls it on `change` when `E.timer`. **The layer TONE/instrument select now does too** — a tone change while playing re-anchors so the new voice takes over at the next unit boundary (current unit rings out), NOT mid-phrase. Wired at all three tone sites: added layers (`k==='tone'` in `_ambWireInst`, key `type+':'+id`), primary bed/motif/texture (`wireSelect`, key = layer name), seq (`bindStr('tone',…)`, key `seq:+id`). If you add a new layer-voice/instrument select, mirror the `if (E.timer) _ambReanchorLayer(E, key)` on its change handler.
- **Silent/dry Bloom render** already exists: set `window._ambSilentCapture = true` (with a capture sink) and `playNote` captures without sounding — reuse it instead of adding a dry-run path.
- **Always-on per-layer DSP glitches modest Bloom stacks.** Continuous DSP built for every layer even when inactive (it ran at flat/0 settings) drains the audio render thread and glitches/cuts out with only a handful of layers. This is why Distortion/Delay are spliced in LAZILY by `_ambApplyLayerFx` only when `mix > 0`, and why the per-layer **EQ3 is lazy too** (`_ambApplyEq` inserts an EQ3 between vcf→vca only when a band ≠ 0; the meter FFT analyser stays on the VCA so meters still work). Rule: don't add per-layer nodes that run unconditionally — gate them on the feature being engaged, or a 5-layer project chokes. Even *engaged* heavy DSP needs scaling: per-layer **Distortion oversampling** is adaptive (`_ambDistOversample`/`_ambNormalizeDistOversample`) — 1 dist layer = 4x, 2-3 = 2x, 4+ = none — because 5× 4x-oversampled waveshapers (the "FX on all layers" case) overran the render thread and cut audio out (recovered only by Stop→Play, which tears the FX down).
- **A layer's note-source chords are usually the AREA PROGRESSION'S, held BY REFERENCE — any per-layer note editor mutates them for every layer.** `_ambNotesOf` applies an "area progression lock": when `cfg.prog.on`, EVERY layer's source becomes `{type:'prog', chords: cfg.prog.chords}` — the same array, not a copy. So the Drone/Arp note editors are editing the area's harmony, not a private copy. Two consequences: (1) any editor UI must SAY so (the drone popover now does); (2) an editor must never write a per-layer VIEW back into the chord. That second one bit for real: the drone editor is Density-capped, and re-spelling a chord wrote the capped set — so a Density-1 drone picking "Min 9" left the whole area with a literal one-note chord (`0:[0]`). Rule: Density governs how many of a chord's tones a layer VOICES, never how many the chord HAS — re-spell writes the full form, and a slot edit SWAPS one tone inside the existing set rather than rebuilding from the capped view. Verify claims like this against the PERSISTED cfg (`localStorage['bloops-workspace'].masterAmbient.prog.chords`), not the overview strip, which is `_sig`-cached and will happily show stale chords after an edit.
- **The Drone's "Edit notes" popover is DENSITY-SHAPED, not a chip grid.** A drone sounds exactly `density` notes, so the editor is one `<select>` per voice SLOT (capped at Density) rather than the Arp's 2-octave semitone cell grid — and the options carry ABSOLUTE octaves (C3, D♯4) because a drone is heard as a fixed pitch, not an interval. Each chord also gets a QUALITY select (`_ambChordFormOf` derives the current form from the intervals' pitch-class set, so it re-reads as "Min 7" after a hand edit and shows "Custom" when nothing matches); picking one calls `_ambDroneChordSetForm`, which re-spells using exactly Density notes, stacking +1 octave past the top the way the engine's own voicing does. "Revert to original" restores a JSON snapshot taken when the popover opened, IN PLACE (delete-then-copy onto the same objects) so the source keeps its identity for anything holding a reference. Edits re-anchor the layer, since a drone otherwise wouldn't be heard for a whole hold. A pedal-mode drone under a progression ignores all of this (its note comes from the scorer) — the popover says so rather than silently doing nothing.
- **Any per-layer default that a layer TYPE constrains must be filtered in `_ambApplyRandomInstVoice` too, not just in the UI list.** Filtering the Drone's Tone dropdown to sustaining voices left the random voice roll on ADD untouched, so a brand-new drone could be born on `bell` — and the back-compat rule then dutifully showed it flagged, which looks like the filter is broken. Caught by re-running the tone-list test after the change; the fix is a `type === 'drone'` filter on the pool. Rule of thumb: a UI restriction and a default-generation path are two places, and only the second one produces "it shipped broken out of the box".
- **The Drone's Tone list is filtered to SUSTAINING voices only (`_ambToneOptionsFor`).** A drone holds one note for its whole unit — 31 s at the defaults, a whole progression cycle in pedal mode — so a decaying voice makes the layer vanish mid-hold. Excluded built-ins: `pluck` / `kick` / `metal` / `modal` (played as one-shots; their held path returns a no-op release) and `bell` / `xylo` (FM voices whose SCHEDULED amp envelope is sustain 0 — note the HELD path gives bell sustain 0.5, so check the scheduled branch, which is what Bloom uses). Samples/ensembles stay because the drone flags them `loop: true`; a `user:` Design patch is judged by its own amp `sustain` (0-100) and its base voice. Two things to preserve when touching this: a project ALREADY on a decaying tone keeps it, relabelled "— decays (won't hold)", so loading an old drone never silently rewrites its sound; and `_ambRefreshToneSelects` must re-apply the filter (it repopulates every `-tone` select on sample/ensemble import and would otherwise quietly re-open the excluded voices). Only the drone is filtered — every other layer keeps the full list.
- **A RESTORED frozen loop (stop→play, reload, seed-edit lock) anchored at the raw tick clock, not the shared grid — the REAL "drone starts a hair early".** `_ambReplayFrozen`'s restore branch did `st.anchor = now`; live layers anchor on `_barGridAnchor`; the frozen layer therefore sat a constant sub-tick offset (~0–150 ms) off the grid EVERY session, with nothing wrong in its config. Unreproducible in seven fresh-project attempts because a fresh project has no persisted lock. The tell in `window.__ambPhase()`: the layer has **no phase store at all** (`store: null` — the emitter never runs; the freeze gate short-circuits it), so the freeze anchor IS its clock — the diagnostic now reports `store:'freeze'` + `frozen.anchor/loopLen`. Fix: snap the restored anchor to the most recent grid-aligned loop start ≤ now (`G + floor((now−G)/loopLen)·loopLen`) — phase-true immediately; the replay's from-clamp handles joining mid-phrase. Verified: a Write-frozen bed restored via stop→play reads `anchorOffGridMs: 0`. LESSON, at the end of a five-theory chase (attack, Free-vs-sync, psi fallback…): when one layer drifts and its config is clean, ask WHAT IS ITS CLOCK — a frozen layer's clock is `E.freeze[key].anchor`, and no amount of emitter-anchor fixing touches it. FOLLOW-UP: at play start the first tick's `now` PRECEDES the grid anchor by the lead, so `floor((now−G)/loopLen)` is −1 and the snapped anchor landed a full loop in the PAST — the frozen layer's bar lit ~99% for the lead's duration ("the whole bar flashes, then everything starts") and the replay's catch-up could schedule the loop's TAIL into the pre-grid window. Pre-grid, the anchor is G itself (the first loop starts AT the grid), and the frozen bar branch gates `active` on `now ≥ anchor` instead of unconditionally-active (a negative delta modulo-wraps to ~full). Verified: every bar peaks 0.21 in the first 600 ms after Play, uniformly.
- **NEVER anchor one layer on `_ambProgSyncInfo().anchor` when every other layer anchors on `_ambUnitGridSnap`'s grid.** psi's anchor is `_barGridAnchor` with a **`_playStartAt` fallback**, and playStart precedes the grid by the first-tick lead (~60-150 ms) — so a layer re-snapped against psi can land on a grid nothing else shares. A drone did: measured in a user project, the drone's bar phase origin sat at 0 ms while beat/bass/arp/bed — four SEPARATE bar branches — all agreed at +130 ms. Five layers, one outlier, and the outlier was the only one with a bespoke anchor. The prog sub-grid snap now applies to FREE drones only (which `_ambUnitGridSnap` genuinely doesn't touch) and anchors on `_barGridAnchor` explicitly, never psi's fallback; a unit-synced drone takes the identical path as every other synced layer. DIAGNOSTIC that finally caught it, after seven failed synthetic reproductions: sample every `.ambient-ph` bar's `--ph` for a few seconds and recover each layer's phase ORIGIN by unwrapping (`origin = (t − ph·P) mod P`, averaged on the unit circle), then diff against one layer. Activation time and first-onset time both showed 0 ms spread and proved nothing — phase origin is the measurement that exposes a constant sub-period offset.
- **"Layer X starts earlier than the others" is usually ATTACK, not scheduling — compare onset + attack, not onset.** Bloom's first onsets are sample-aligned (measured: drone/bed/riff/beat all at +0 ms), but perceived entry is onset + attack, and the layer attacks differ by an order of magnitude: beat 1 ms, riff 20 ms, motif 100 ms, drone 200 ms, bed 2000 ms. A drone at 200 ms against the bed's 2 s swell is HEARD ~1.8 s early even though both start on the same sample — which is exactly what "the drone comes in first" sounds like. The drone's default attack is now 2000, matching the bed, so the two slowest layers swell together. Note defaults only affect NEW layers: an existing project keeps its stored attack, so the fix for a saved drone is its own Attack slider. Harness-neutral because the invariant harness hashes `[at,freq,durMs,voice,pan,level]` — attack is not in that list, so envelope defaults can be tuned without a re-baseline.
- **A DRONE's edits must re-strike PROMPTLY — the "ring out the current unit" re-anchor rule is wrong for a 32-second hold.** Symptom: "changes to Drone Tone have no effect, same sound." It wasn't a no-op, it was LATENCY: `_ambReanchorLayer` re-anchors to the next unit boundary so the current unit finishes, which is right for a 2 s motif but means a drone (Hold 4 × Unit 8 s = a 31 s sustained note, or a whole progression cycle in pedal mode) doesn't change voice for up to half a minute — measured 14.7 s on the defaults. `_ambReanchorLayer` now special-cases `type === 'drone'`: cancel pending, `stopBloomVoicesBefore` the sounding voice (the pad `kill()` is a click-free ~22 ms ramp), and DELETE `E.runPhase[key]` so the emitter rebuilds it grid-snapped — which in pedal mode also clears `_pedalAt`, so it takes the mid-cycle catch-up path and re-enters on the next chord sub-slot instead of mid-bar. Measured after: 0 s plain / 0.8 s pedal. Safe against machine-gunning because every caller is a `change` handler, never per-`input`. GENERAL RULE: when a layer's unit is long, "lands at the next boundary" and "does nothing" are indistinguishable to the user — check the measured delay before concluding a control is dead.
- **DRONE PEDAL (default under a progression): ONE note per progression/part cycle, chosen by `_ambDronePedalPick` — and the pick is ARTICULATED on the card.** `inst.progMode` absent/'pedal' = pedal; 'voice' = the old per-chord re-voicing (Subdivide/Feel/Variety). The scorer rates every pc against ALL the cycle's chords + the key (chord tone +3 with root/5th anchor tallies, key-color 9th/13th/maj7 +1.5, in-key passing +0.5, outside −2, ♭9-over-any-root −1.5 extra); `_ambDronePedalText` renders the explanation ("For C / F / G: G fits 2/3 — 5th of C, 9th of F (key color), root of G") under the card's Note picker, which overrides the pick (`inst.progNote`, absent = auto) and then describes the USER's note with "auto would pick X" appended. Strike windows come from `_ambDronePedalWindow` — the whole-prog cycle, or the bound section-PART's cycle anchored at the section start (section-harmony aware). Both fields are string/absent — normalized in the `x.type === 'drone'` extras block, NOT the defaults object (the backfill trap). Deterministic (no RNG), native-loop, and the harness `drone` config has no prog → byte-identical. ALSO fixed here: a Free-unit drone under a progression anchored at its own unsnapped lead, so it ENTERED EARLIER than the bar-grid-snapped layers — under `_psi` the anchor now snaps UP onto the chord sub-grid (measured: drone and bed first onsets now identical to the ms). TONIC BIAS: a drone is a PEDAL, so the tonic gets `_AMB_PEDAL_TONIC_BIAS` (+3 — worth one extra chord-tone hit) and wins every near-tie; C/F/G holds C (the textbook I-pedal) rather than G on chord-tone count alone. It is a bias, not an override: a tonic that's genuinely outside the chords still loses (verified — F#/B/E picks B, Db/Gb picks C#). The tonic is the KEY root, or on a keyless/chromatic area the progression's FIRST chord root, since "key root" means little there (`_ambDronePedalTonic`). The readout appends "· tonic pedal" when the pick is the tonic.
- **playNote-wrapper tests: read `window._ambEmitKey` AFTER calling through, never before.** The capture tee INSIDE playNote stamps `_ambEmitKey` for the current note; a wrapper that reads it before calling `orig()` attributes every note to the PREVIOUS note's layer. With one layer playing the off-by-one is MOSTLY invisible (self-attribution) — but NOT at the first note of each onset group, whose predecessor was a different layer or nothing at all. That is the case that bites: a 3-note bed chord reads as 2 notes with the ROOT missing (`[330,392]` instead of `[262,330,392]`), which looks exactly like a voicing bug and was chased as one. Reading the key both ways in the same wrapper settles it in one run. The trap also fires in multi-layer tests — here it made BED voicing notes (C4, the bed's lenMs) masquerade as drone notes at the drone's own timestamps, which looked exactly like "the pedal ignores its pick". `new Error().stack` in the wrapper names the true emitter and settles it in one run.
- **`_ambVoiceProgChord` trims to the lowest RENDERED PITCHES, which is NOT the same as the lowest chord tones — that's why a Density-1 Drone under a progression sat on a near-static note.** Each tone is rendered at `center` from the CHORD's OWN root, so within one octave C major renders C–E–G but F major renders C–F–A and G major D–G–B. The `out.length = want` trim then keeps the bottom of THAT, so Density 1 gave C for C, **C for F, D for G** — the drone barely moved (and its 🎹 highlight looked "shifted" on those chords, because `_ambUpdatePianos` lights whatever the layer actually played, from `E.cap`). Fix: a GATED degree-selection step in the voicer — when `bed.degree` is present, pick `want` consecutive chord tones starting at the Degree-th (+1 octave per wrap), the same semantics the NON-prog drone branch always had; the Bed never sets `degree`, so its voicings and the harness stay byte-identical. The drone's prog-sync `_shim` also has to PASS `degree` — it was dropped, so the Note knob did nothing under a progression at all. Verified C/F/G roots at Density 1, E/A/B at Degree 2, full triads unchanged at Density 3. DIAGNOSTIC: prove "layer X ignores the changes" by capturing its notes at a HIGHER density first — at density 3 this drone was already voicing every chord perfectly, which located the bug in the trim rather than in chord resolution.
- **🎲 HARMONY TAKE-REROLL (`cfg.prog.reroll`, 0-100): function-preserving chord substitutions derived from the TAKE SEED, resolved at READ time and never written back.** The harmony counterpart of the per-layer `takeReroll` that re-rolls euclid rhythm: New take bumps `cfg.seed`, so the changes re-realize reproducibly (same take id = same harmony) while `prog.chords` stays exactly as authored — reverting is just setting the amount to 0. Applied in `_ambProgCurrentChord` next to `alts`, gated to the GLOBAL prog (`n.chords === cfg0.prog.chords`, same doctrine as the order-perm), using a DEDICATED `_ambSeededRand` keyed on (slot, seed) so no shared-stream draw shifts and the >1 resolver calls per onset agree. Candidates are relative minor/major, mediant, up-a-fourth, and 7th/9th colour on the written root.
