KEY TRAP: the in-key filter originally required a real key scale, and a keyless area resolves to 'chromatic' — so on the common setup only COLOUR candidates survived and the feature read as "it just adds 7ths". It now falls back to the PROGRESSION's own pitch-class pool (a substitution may only use notes the progression already contains), which is what makes the diatonic swaps actually appear. The readout names what the take did ("take jwh: C→Am (relative minor) · F→Am (mediant)"); a colour keeps the root, so it renders "C +maj7" rather than the no-op-looking "C→C (maj7)".
- **SECTION KEY OFFSET (`cfg.sections[i].key`, semitones): applied at the ONE root chokepoint, plus the frozen-replay path.** `_ambSrcRootPc` is what every pitch resolves through (`_ambNoteFreq` calls it), so wrapping it — `_ambSrcRootPc` shifts, `_ambSrcRootPcRaw` is the original — modulates chords, wraps, progressions, scales, chord-locked phrases and yoked layers together with ZERO emitter changes. Resolved by the NOTE's play time via `_ambKeyTime` (the same stamp the keyMaster schedule uses), so the change lands exactly on the boundary with no rescheduling. TWO paths need it, and missing the second is the trap: a layer running a frozen Write/Evolve loop replays captured ABSOLUTE pitches, so the modulation passes it straight by — the Bed (Write-on by default!) sat in the old key while live layers moved. Fixed by transposing at `_ambLockHarmonizeFreq`'s result in `_ambReplayFrozen`. The drone pedal is a THIRD path (it reads `cfg.prog.chords` directly, not via the chokepoint). DIAGNOSTIC: when a per-section measurement can't be aligned to the engine's anchor from outside, SEARCH the bucketing phase — if no phase makes the sets match, it's the engine, not the bucketing (that's what proved the Bed was genuinely not transposing rather than merely mis-binned; after the fix, 2/2 layers matched at phase 0.13 s).
- **SECTION-BOUND HARMONY: `cfg.sections[i].part` binds a section to one PART of the area progression (verse changes / chorus changes).** While that section runs, the chord clock walks ONLY that part's contiguous chord slice, looping, anchored at the SECTION's own start bar — so the changes restart from the part's top each time the section comes round. Implemented as an EARLY BRANCH at the top of `_ambProgStepAt`, before the existing salt/order/lens code, and gated on (sections exist ∧ this section names a valid part ∧ the source isn't a LAYER's own prog) — so the default path falls through untouched and the invariant harness (whose configs have no sections) is byte-identical by construction. The branch returns `(sectionStep + loops) * chords.length + absChordIdx`, which keeps BOTH caller contracts: `step % len` is the chord and `floor(step / len)` is the variation cycle that `alts` and the ↻ order-perm hash on. THREE things to know: (1) the sections normalize `.map()`s a FRESH object per section, so any new field must be carried explicitly or it's dropped every normalize; (2) `_ambSectionAt` and `_ambProgStepAt` share the same anchor (`_progAnchor || _playStartAt`), which is the only reason a static bar-space walk in the renderer matches the engine's time-space one — don't give either its own anchor; (3) the length-SALT and ↻ ORDER grid re-partition/permute the WHOLE written cycle, so they're deliberately bypassed while a part is bound (permuting the full list would pull in chords from outside the part). The Scheduler chord lane duplicates the walk for drawing — verified by diffing lane text against a live sample of `.ambient-pov-chord.cur` (which is driven by `_ambProgStepAt` itself): lane `|C F G |C |Cm A♯ G♯ G` vs walk `0 1 2 0 | 3 4 5 6`. Diagnose harmony-timing claims with that chord-index walk, NOT with per-window pitch-class sets — a pad over a 4-bar window saturates 8–9 of 12 pcs and two different progressions look identical.
- **`_ambMixerLayers` must SKIP `seq`/`samp` in its `cfg.extras` loop — `_ambSeqList`/`_ambSampleList` already enumerate the extras-hosted ones, so emitting both DUPLICATES every Seq/Sample layer.** Symptom: a Sample layer shows as TWO Scheduler lanes (and two mixer channels) under the SAME `data-schkey`, the extra one labelled with the raw type — `samp` next to `Sample1`, `seq` next to `Seq1` — because neither type has an `_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA` entry to name it, so the label falls back to `ex.type`. Since schema v2/C3 made `cfg.extras` the single home for seq/samp (`_ambAddSampleLayer` pushes `{type:'samp'}` there), the dedicated list helpers scan extras FIRST and only then fall back to legacy `cfg.seqs`/`cfg.samples` — so the extras loop double-counts. `_ambRenderExtras` already skips these two types when building cards; the mixer/scheduler list just never got the same filter. Reads as a "zombie lane" because deleting the layer clears one entry and the duplicate is left behind. A raw lowercase type string appearing as a UI label is the tell that a list is emitting a layer through the wrong path.
- **`_ambRenderMixer` TAIL-RENDERS the Scheduler and Groove — so an early `return` inside it silently freezes those two views.** Symptom: delete the LAST layer and its lane stays in the ⏱ Scheduler forever (a "zombie lane"), while the mixer correctly empties. Cause: the empty-list branch appended a "No layers yet." hint and returned, skipping `_ambRenderScheduler(E)` / `_ambRenderGroove(E)` at the bottom of the function — so the Scheduler kept its last non-empty paint. Deleting a layer while OTHERS remain always worked, which is why it reads as an edge case rather than a broken delete path. Two diagnostic notes: (1) `_ambRenderScheduler` clears correctly on its own (`if (!layers.length) → 'No layers yet.'`) — the bug was that it never RAN, so prove which it is with a MutationObserver on `#…-sched-body` rather than inferring from the DOM; (2) a "force a re-render" step that finds no header silently does nothing and looks like "re-rendered and still wrong" — verify the forced path actually fired. Rule: anything a render function does AFTER its main body is a dependency of that function, not an optional extra; don't guard it behind an early return. (Checked and NOT a bug: the primary `✕` / `_ambRemoveLayer` path, which calls only `_ambSyncControls`, still repaints both views by another route — verified by disabling a candidate fix and re-running.)
- **CHORD / SECTION MATRIX cells are a free 0-100 PROBABILITY, not four rungs — and the storage always was.** `L.chordMask.steps[i]` / `L.sectionMask.steps[i]` is the chance the layer plays that chord (or section), drawn ONCE per instance so the layer is in or out for the whole thing (`_ambChordGateOK`, deterministic hash keyed on chord step + layer id, zero shared-RNG draws). `_ambNormalizeChordMask` has always clamped to 0-100 rather than quantizing; only the UI restricted it, cycling a hardcoded `100→60→30→0`. Three things to know now: (1) the tap ladder is **`_ambMaskCellNext` — step down to the next rung strictly BELOW the current value, wrap at 0**, which is byte-identical to the old chain for every rung AND degrades sensibly for a hand-set value (45%→30%, 85%→60%); the old `v >= 30 ? 0` arm sent any custom value straight to silence on one tap. (2) Exact values come from `_ambMaskCellModal` via long-press (450 ms) / right-click, or a tap once the **✎ %** toggle is on (`el._pmEdit`, transient view state — the euclid grid's "✎ Step" shape, so the exact path is a visible affordance and not a hidden gesture); a fired long-press sets `el._suppressClick` so the trailing click does NOT also step the ladder. (3) **The cell tint MUST be value-driven, not per-value attribute rules.** `[data-v="60"]`/`[data-v="30"]` only matched the rungs, so a 45% cell fell through to the base rule and drew FULL green — the colour asserting "always" while the label said 45%. It is now `rgba(53,201,142, calc(var(--pv) * 0.01 - 0.05))` (the line through the two old anchors, so 60→.55 and 30→.25 look exactly as they did), with `data-v="100"`/`data-v="0"` keeping their own endpoint rules and a JS-set `.pm-dark` class above 70% because CSS cannot branch on a number. Cells also need `-webkit-touch-callout: none` + `user-select: none` or iOS answers the long-press with a selection callout. **ROW / COLUMN APPLY**: the slider always writes the ONE cell (immediate, reversible) and the spread is two explicit full-sentence buttons — "Set all of *bass* to 45%" / "Set every layer on *chord 2 (F)* to 45%" — so a wide edit is never something you did by accident. The **row label** and **column header** are also handles: clicking them opens the same modal already scoped (the spread buttons are then absent, since it is already a bulk edit). COLUMN apply is the one that can't work off the clicked row — it reaches every layer through `_ambChordMatrixRows(cfg)`, and **each layer it touches needs its own `_ambMaskEditPoke`** or the ones mid-loop keep the old gate. **The two matrices share the grammar but NOT the code** — `_ambRenderChordMatrix` and `_ambRenderSectionMatrix` each own their handler; a change to one needs the same change in the other (the shared parts are `_ambMaskCellHtml`/`_ambMaskCellTxt`/`_ambMaskCellTip`/`_ambMaskCellNext`/`_ambMaskCellModal`/`_ambWireMaskCells`).
- **UNIT SCHEDULE (`layer.unitGate`) — the per-unit on/off step gate, opened by tapping a unit block in the ⏱ Scheduler lane.** `{ div, period, mode, slots:{i:mask} }`: the unit is cut into `div` equal slices, and the gate for ABSOLUTE unit index `i` is `slots[i % period]` (an absent slot = fully on, which is also how a cleared gate is stored — `_ambUnitGateSet` prunes all-on masks and drops `unitGate` when the store empties, so absence is the neutral state everywhere). `period` seeds from the number of blocks that layer's lane DRAWS; the Apply-to actions grow it to `lcm(period, N)` so "every Nth" is exact — which can push the period past what the lane shows, hence the "(the lane draws the first N)" note in the popover. Kept OUT of `_ambDefaultLayer` (the object-in-defaults backfill trap) and coerced by `_ambNormalizeUnitGate`, wired into all FOUR layer normalize paths (primaries / extras / seq / sample). **Two modes, two totally different mechanisms:** `skip` gates note ONSETS in `playNote` — placed deliberately AFTER the capture tee so Write/Bar-Lock captures hold the FULL phrase and the gate is a PLAYBACK filter, which is what lets you edit the schedule live on a frozen loop with no rewrite (same reasoning as chord-mask edits); sustains ring through, so a pad gated 50% measures only ~24% silence. `chop` schedules a gain envelope on `e.ugGate` — and needs NO engine split, because that param is a real AudioParam on the node chain and a SHIM param (core param **5**, a plain output multiplier beside `tg_val`) under core strips, so one scheduler drives both (measured 46% core / 45% node for the same gate). Core param 5 defaults to 1.0 and nothing writes it unless chop is engaged, so `x*1.0` keeps every existing render bit-identical (CORE_REV 13, golden 80/80 unchanged + 2 new sections). **The lane and the audio MUST agree** — both take unit length from `_ambUnitLaneBars` (VIEW clamp included) and phase from `E._barGridAnchor`, the same anchor the scheduler playhead uses; if you add a third consumer, use that helper, don't recompute. Merge-mode (prog-locked Bed/Drone with `progMerge`) blocks are chord voicings of varying width, not a repeating unit — they get no `data-ui` and are deliberately not gateable.
- **EXTENDING a `strip_*` wasm export's signature: make the new args `i32`, never `f32`.** The JS→wasm coercion turns a MISSING argument into `ToInt32(NaN) = 0` for an integer but a genuine `NaN` for a float — so an `i32` extension leaves every stale call site (other engines, `test/golden-render.js`, an un-updated branch) rendering the old neutral path, while an `f32` one silently poisons the buffer with NaN. Design the new param so **0 is the neutral/bypassed value** and the extension is free: `strip_dist`'s `tilt` is signed −100..100 (0 = flat) precisely so the UI's 0–100 Tone slider can centre on 50 without 0 meaning "fully dark". Proof it works: golden stayed 75/75 bit-identical with `strip_dist` gaining two args while the test still called it with the old five. (Same reasoning applies to reading a param the host may not push yet.)
- **REMOVING an FX must clear its `dryKill`, not just zero its Mix — otherwise the effect keeps running at FULL WET with no UI left to switch it off.** Dry Kill deliberately ENGAGES an effect at Mix 0 (`wantDelay = (mix|0) > 0 || dk(dly)`) — that is the point of it — so the `off:` handlers in the FX registry, which only did `lc.delay.mix = 0`, left a DELETED delay at `strip_delay enabled=1 wet=1` forever. Reported as "I deleted the delay and now all I have is the wet signal", and it was unrecoverable: the card is gone, so the checkbox that would undo it no longer exists. All six `off:` handlers now clear `dryKill` too, and `_ambNormalizeFx` SELF-HEALS existing projects — an FX absent from an explicit `fxChain` has been removed, so its `dryKill` is forced to 0 (fxChain only materialises once the user adds/removes, which is exactly when the removal happened, so its absence there is meaningful). **A SECOND, softer trap in the same area**: the layer's **Wet only** forces every in-line FX fully wet (`_wetOnlyFx`), so while it is on, unchecking a per-FX **Dry kill** changes nothing — measured `wetOnly=1, dryKill=0 → wet=1`. That reads as a dead control ("I turned dry kill off and the dry didn't come back"), so the row is now marked `.is-overridden` with a "(Wet only wins)" tag and a tooltip naming which switch is in charge. Distortion is exempt from the forcing, so its checkbox is deliberately NOT marked. DIAGNOSTIC NOTE: when reproducing an FX bug, scope the DOM query by `[data-inst="<key>"]` — there is one Dry-kill checkbox PER LAYER, and a bare `.ambient-fx-drykill[data-fx="delay"]` grabs the first card's, which made a working control look broken.
- **NEVER force a WAVESHAPER to full wet as a "remove the dry" gesture — distortion curves are level NORMALIZERS, so on a quiet layer full-wet is a huge volume BOOST.** Symptom: "layer at 29%, click Wet only, it gets very loud." Cause: `_ambApplyLayerFx`'s Wet Only forced EVERY engaged in-line FX to `wet=1`, distortion included. Delay/chorus/phaser/auto-pan are level-neutral there (measured +0.1 / −1.4 / −3.7 / −4.2 dB, constant across level), but `fx_dist`'s output amplitude barely tracks its input — the classic curve is `(3+k)·x·scale/(π+k|x|)`, i.e. small-signal gain ≈ (3+k)·0.111 falling to ≈0.35 at x=1 — so the forced-wet output is essentially the SAME RMS at any layer level (measured 0.13 / 0.22 / 0.26 / 0.28 / 0.33 for levels 10→100). At Level 29 / Mix 10 / Amount 40 that read as +2.4 dB classic, +8.6 overdrive, +10.4 fuzz, **+19.7 fold** — while at Level 100 the same toggle went 10 dB QUIETER. The level-dependence (and the sign flip) is the tell. Fix: `_wetOnlyFx(fx)` exempts `dst`, and dist no longer counts toward `anyInline` — so a dist-only layer takes the `strip_mainout` mute path instead (reverb wash only). Per-FX **Dry kill** still forces dist wet (that IS what that checkbox asks for). Related: the node fallback had NO dry-mute at all (`strip_mainout` is core-only) — it now carries a lazily-built terminal `e.mainOut` Gain, included in `_fxOrderSig` so the chain rebuilds once when it appears and never churns on toggle. When judging an FX's "wet only" semantics, ask whether it produces a TAIL (delay does; a waveshaper does not) — and measure the RMS across levels rather than trusting the curve's shape.
- **A layer's `level` has two synced controls and ~5 writer paths.** The mixer fader and each layer card's "Level" slider are both shortcuts for the same `layer.level`. Writers: mixer fader (`_ambRenderMixer`), primary `bind()`, extras schema `k==='sl'`, seq `bindInt`, sample `bindInt`. Each calls `_ambSyncLevelUI(E, key, v)` to mirror the value into the OTHER control's slider + readout (it only sets `.value`/text — no synthetic `input` dispatch, so no feedback loop). The mixer fader carries `data-mixkey=<layer key>`; cards are found via their header `[data-phkey]`. If you add another place that mutates `level`, call `_ambSyncLevelUI` there too or the two controls drift.
- **Bloom Level is a CONTINUOUS per-layer GAIN node (`e.levelGain`), NOT a per-note volume.** Chain: `vcf → vca → levelGain → gate → pan` (`levelGain` spliced in `_ambBuildMod`). `_ambApplyLevel` is now a NO-OP pass-through; the level is applied as a gain via `_ambLevelGain(level)` (0→silent, 70/default→×1.3 baseline lift, 100→×4 / +12 dB — `_AMB_LEVEL_BASE`/`_AMB_LEVEL_MAX`), written by `_ambUpdateMod` (build/sync), `_ambSyncLevelUI` (every manual writer — real-time fader), and the ramp's `key==='level'` `set` (real-time sweep). The ceiling is ×4 (not the original ×2) BECAUSE several layer types are per-note-staged low (`_ambBassParams` ×0.34, run/pedal ×0.32, drone ×0.2 in their param builders) — the old ×2 couldn't lift them to parity, so quiet layers stayed quiet even maxed; ×4 gives the slider enough range (0.34×4=1.36 > 1). Those base stagings are HARNESS-HASHED (`params.volume`), so boost via `_ambLevelGain` (the gain node) — NOT the base volumes — to stay harness-safe. **Why:** per-note level only affected NEW notes, so on a sustained/overlapping pad the held tails masked it ("the level ramp does nothing"); a layer gain sweeps the WHOLE layer, tails included, in real time. The reverb send now taps `levelGain` (still pre-gate → scales with level AND tails ring through a gate mute). **Harness-safe:** at the default level 70 the no-op equals the old curve exactly, and no harness config sets a non-default level, so the invariant battery is byte-identical. If you add a new `level` writer, push to `e.levelGain.gain` (via `_ambSyncLevelUI`) or held voices won't follow.
- **The TRANCE GATE is a bar-synced step pattern on a dedicated chain node (`e.tgGate`), driven by a `Tone.Signal` (`e.tgSig`).** Chain: `vca → levelGain → tgGate → gate` (tgGate spliced in `_ambBuildMod`, pre-gate so the reverb — tapped off `levelGain` — fills the chopped gaps). Config is per-layer in the FX object: `tg:{on,steps,pattern[],depth,edge}` (`_ambDefaultFx`/`_ambNormalizeFx`; `steps` = steps/bar = pattern length). The driver `_ambScheduleTg` (called per layer from `_ambScheduleStochastic` each tick) schedules the pattern AHEAD onto `tgSig`: **`tgGate.gain` intrinsic is 0 and the connected `tgSig` sets it** (a connected signal SUMS with the param, so intrinsic must be 0, not 1). When `tg.on` is false it disposes `tgSig` and parks `tgGate.gain=1` (passthrough). The grid is anchored to `E._playStartAt` at the cfg `bpm` (`barSec=(60/bpm)*4`) so it locks to the bar. `tgSig` is disposed on stop/teardown so the automation timeline can't accumulate across plays (the codebase's rule — a dedicated disposable Signal, never per-step automation on a persistent shared param). UI: a "Trance Gate" subsection (On/Steps/Depth/Edge + a step grid reusing `.ambient-slice-grid`/`.ambient-slice-cell`) that lives in **`_ambModUi`, grouped under the VCA mod** (both modulate gain — VCA = smooth LFO, gate = step pattern); its element ids are still `fx-tg-*` (unchanged when it moved out of `_ambFxUi`), so the shared `_ambWireTg(E, el, getL, persist)` — called from all four FX wire clusters (seq/sample/extras/primary) — still binds it by id. Harness-safe: `tg.on` defaults 0 and the gate only touches the gain node, never note generation.
- **Portamento (per-layer pitch glide) is GATED and only covers the OSCILLATOR voice path.** A layer's `portamento` (ms, shared default 0 via `_ambNormalizeSpread`) is read by `_ambApplyAdsr` (the shared params builder for all 13 Bloom emits), which — only when `portamento > 0` — sets `p.glideMs` + `p.glideLayer = inst`. In `playNote` (04, the wavetable/oscillator branch), when `glideMs > 0` each `Tone.Oscillator.frequency` is ramped from the layer's previous note freq (tracked on `glideLayer._glidePrev`) to the target over `glideMs`. **Gated** so the default path (and the invariant harness, which doesn't hash glide anyway) is byte-identical. **COVERAGE (all wired):** the glide is applied in THREE `playNote` voice paths — (a) oscillator/wavetable (ramp each `Tone.Oscillator.frequency`), (b) the `.frequency` synths FM/AM/Duo/Mono/Membrane/Metal (ramp `synth.frequency` after `triggerAttackRelease`), and (c) **samples** (ramp `source.playbackRate`, tracked on `glideLayer._glidePrevRate` — note the slice window is computed at the target rate, so a brief glide can drift the slice slightly). Pluck/Noise don't glide (no sustained pitch). The `portamento` property is on EVERY Bloom layer (`_ambNormalizeFx`) with a UI slider: primary layers (literal `sl`), seq+sample (`_ambSl`), extras (`_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA` `['sl','portamento',…]`). **Seed (grid lanes)** are wired too: `lane.portamento` (`_makeLane`), injected into the scheduler's SINGLE-note step path (`07-playback-scheduler.js` — chords skip it, they'd share one `_glidePrev`), `glideLayer` = the lane object, control = a slider in `_showLaneMenu`.
- **Master Bloom decouples the VIEWED area from the PLAYING area.** `getCfg()` (= `masterAmbient` = `areas[activeIdx]`) is the area the PANEL shows/edits; the ENGINE generates `areas[E._playIdx]` via **`_ambPlayCfg(E)`** (returns getCfg for lane/shape/harness — `E._playIdx` is only set on the master while playing, so the invariant harness is unaffected). The two engine cfg sources are `_ambTick` and `_ambSyncMods` (both use `_ambPlayCfg`); generation reads the cached `E._cfg` which the tick sets from the play area. **So while playing you can click a tab to view/edit ANY area without changing playback** (`_ambSwitchToArea` is view-only when `_masterEng.timer`). Orchestration advances `E._playIdx` (NOT `activeIdx`) and does NOT rebuild the panel. CRITICAL: any UI handler that pushes an edit LIVE to the engine (`_ambSyncOneLayerMod`, the FX/EQ `_ambApplyLayerFx` calls) must be gated by **`_ambLiveApplyOK(E)`** (false when editing a non-playing area) so editing a non-playing area can't disturb the running engine's shared-key chains; `_ambSyncMods`-based edits (mod/spread) are inherently safe since `_ambSyncMods` reads the play area. A "Plot" = one full pass through the area sequence (`E._plotCount`); per-area stochastic `playsRandom` → `_ambEffectivePlays` randomizes the play count per entry (`E._curPlays`).
- **SEED progression vs CURRENT progression are separate concerns — `ambient-prog-pick` used to conflate three of them.** The picker's LABEL mirrored the current progression (so a chooser doubled as a readout), and worse, the editor's only commit path (`target.apply`) ALSO called `_ambPublishProgChords`, with a default editor name of `"<name> edit"` — so every save wrote a fresh seed-list entry and repeated edits left "X edit", "X edit edit" behind. Now: **`prog.seedName`** records WHICH seed was loaded and is the only thing the picker shows (`_ambProgSeedLabel`), **`prog.name`/`prog.chords`** are the current progression that every edit lands on, and the seed list is written by **Export ONLY** (`data-pe="export"` — prompts for the name that will appear in the list, then makes it the current name + seed). `prog.seedSig` is the chord signature at load time so the picker can append "· edited" once the current progression diverges; both fields are additive strings coerced in `_ambNormalizeProgMeta`, absent = hand-authored ("Custom"). Save commits to the current progression and touches NO seed. The one other legitimate publish is the Seq "promote to progression" button, which is an explicit export by intent. Verified end-to-end: load seed → picker names it, list stays empty; edit → "· edited"; Save → chords committed, list STILL empty; Export → one named entry, picker and current name both follow it. **FOLLOW-UP — the 8-control `#ambient-prog-sub` row was split by WHAT EACH ACTION ANSWERS, leaving three controls on the outer row (on/off · seed picker · Edit).** Each button moved next to the thing it operates on: **⚄ Generate** and **⌨ Roman numerals** are now the top group of the SEED PICKER menu (they answer the picker's own question — "where does this progression come from"; suppressed in `opts.append` mode, where the menu is choosing a part to chain on); **♺ Reharmonize** and **🔍 Detect key** are a `.pe-ops` toolbar above the chord grid IN the editor (they rewrite chords you can see — area scope only, since both act on `cfg.prog`); **＋ Part** is a chip at the end of the OVERVIEW STRIP (`data-pov="addpart"` — it chains another progression on, so it belongs where the parts are drawn); **⧉ Clone was deleted outright** (the editor footer's `data-pe="clone"` was always a duplicate). The handlers are extracted as `const _pgGenerate/_pgRoman/_pgReharm/_pgDetectKey/_pgAddPart` inside the panel wiring and published through a module-scope **`_ambProgActions`** registry, because their new homes (`_ambOpenGlobalProgMenu`, `_ambPeAct`, `_ambProgOverviewAct`) are all OUTSIDE that closure — declare any new action BEFORE the registry assignment or it's TDZ. The editor's reharm/detect-key branch COMMITS the staged chords (`ed.target.apply`) before running the op and re-seeds `ed.chords` after, since both write `cfg.prog` directly and would otherwise operate on the pre-edit chords and discard what's on screen.
- **EVERY chord DISPLAY must resolve through `_ambProgChordAt(E, prog, step)` — reading `prog.chords[step % len]` shows the AUTHORED chord, which is a DIFFERENT chord from the one sounding whenever any read-time resolution is on.** `_ambProgCurrentChord` layers ↻ order-perm → per-slot `alts` → 🎲 take-reroll → 🌊 vary/tension on top of the written array, and none of that is written back (that's the point — the score is preserved). Three displays were indexing the raw array and therefore lying: the app-header chord pill, the progression-overview highlight, and the ⏱ Scheduler chord lane. Measured with take-reroll at 100% and a pedal sounding each chord's root: the pill read **F** while the engine played **D**. `_ambProgChordAt` sets the same `_ambProgStepOverride` the emitters set and restores it (plus `_E`) in a `finally`. **TRAP:** `cfg.prog` has NO `type` field, so passing it straight to `_ambAsNotes` coerces it to a SCALE and the resolver finds no chords (the first fix silently changed nothing) — build `{type:'prog', chords}` explicitly, reusing the SAME array by reference because the take-reroll and order-perm gates test `n.chords === cfg.prog.chords` identity. For the OVERVIEW, order-perm also moves WHICH CHIP is sounding, so the highlight maps through `chords.indexOf(resolved)` and falls back to the slot for alts/reroll (whose objects aren't in the written list). Verified 0/8 mismatches across plain · alts · take-reroll · order-perm, comparing the readout against the pitch actually played. The piano highlight was already truthful — it reads `E.cap`, i.e. what sounded. **THAT WAS ONLY HALF OF IT — the resolved chord object still carries its AUTHORED ROOT, because the engine transposes progression roots LATER, inside `_ambSrcRootPc`, downstream of `_ambProgCurrentChord`.** Two shifts live there: (1) **KEY in transpose mode — which is the DEFAULT** (`_ambKeyMode` returns `'transpose'` unless `keyMode === 'quantize'`): `_ambProgKeyOffset` re-roots the whole progression so `chords[0]` lands on the key root, so a C–F–G template in A major SOUNDS A–D–E; (2) **section modulation** (`cfg.sections[i].key`). Measured: key A over a written C–F–G, a drone held A/D/E while every readout said C/F/G and the roman numerals read ♭III ♭VI ♭VII for what is plainly I IV V. So the funnel is now two functions — **`_ambProgChordAt` = the resolved object (identity preserved, for `chords.indexOf`)** and **`_ambProgSoundAt` = that plus `_ambProgViewShift`, which every chord LABEL must use** (`_ambChordShift` returns the SAME object at shift 0, so the untransposed path is unchanged). Shift consumers: header pill, Scheduler chord lane, overview chips + romans (the strip stays the SCORE — written chords in written order, so the playhead's identity lookup and the ×N alt badge still line up — but drawn in the sounding key, with `_ambProgViewShift` folded into its `_sig` or a key change repaints nothing; the GLOWING chip relabels itself to the sounding chord, since alts/reroll substitute chords that are not in the written list), the drone-pedal readout, and the per-layer note-pick modal. **`_ambProgViewShift` must be computed from the WHOLE progression, never a slice** — `_ambProgKeyOffset` keys on `chords[0]`, so the pedal (which is handed one window's chords) passes `cfg.prog.chords` explicitly. **Two of these were engine bugs, not just display:** the note pick stores an interval above the chord root and the engine applies it over the SOUNDING root, so `_ambPickCandidates` in `key` mode was re-expressing the key's tones against the wrong root; and the drone pedal SCORED against untransposed chords (it shifted only its result, and only for the section offset) — "A fits 1/3 — 13th of C, 3rd of F, 9th of G" for a note that is the root of A and the 5th of D. **The prog EDITOR is deliberately NOT shifted** — it edits the score, and it can't show sounding names because editing the FIRST chord's root under transpose does not move it (verified: written C→C♯ in A major leaves slot 0 sounding A and moves the OTHER two to C♯/D♯), so a sounding-pitch editor would refuse edits. It gets a `.pe-shifted` banner naming what is heard, and its romans / in-key coloring / roman-prompt prefill use the shifted chord because function and key-fit are properties of the sounding chord (the prefill also has to, or typing the numerals back doesn't round-trip). Verified 11 scenarios — key off · key on at the tonic · key on off-tonic · quantize · section offset · alts · reroll · order-perm · key+reroll · key+section · key+alts — comparing every surface against `_ambSrcRootPc`, plus a captured-audio A/B.
- **PART TABS + REPEATS: `prog.parts[i].plays` is how many times a part runs before the progression moves on (Verse ×2 → Chorus ×1), and the chord matrix scopes to one part at a time.** The written chord list is UNCHANGED — what changes is the ORDER OF PLAY — so `_ambProgStepAt` gets a GATED early branch (`parts.some(p => (p.plays|0) > 1)`) that expands the parts into the sequence actually heard and walks that. It is mutually exclusive with the section-bound branch above it, which pins ONE part and returns first. **Both caller contracts survive**: `step % L` is the chord index and `floor(step / L)` the variation cycle, the latter advancing once per full pass through the part SEQUENCE — so repeats of a part play the same chords (which is what a repeat means) while alts/order-perm still evolve pass to pass. `plays` of 1 is neutral and is DELETED rather than stored, so a plain chain carries no field and stays byte-identical. UI: `el._pmPart` (transient view state, −1 = All) drives one tab per part; the grid then draws only that part's columns while **cell/header indices stay ABSOLUTE (`data-ci`)**, so every setter — cell, row, column — keeps writing the right slot of the mask with no offset arithmetic. The repeat stepper rides ON the tab, because "how many times does the verse run" belongs next to the verse. Tabs appear only with >1 part. **TWO scope traps in `_ambRenderChordMatrix`**: it had no `prog` and no `esc` binding (the section matrix has both), so anything lifted from that renderer needs them declared. Verified: tabs `All | Verse | Chorus`, Chorus tab draws `data-ci=2,3`, an edit inside it writes slot 3, and a bass over Verse ×2 → Chorus is heard as `C F C F G A` looping.
- **PER-PART KEY (`prog.parts[i].key = {root, scale}`) — a real modulation mid-progression, with the AREA key untouched.** Two halves: the part's chords are STORED in the new key (`_ambProgAppendPart` transposes the incoming progression so its tonic lands on the chosen root, keeping the relative motion — I vi IV V stays I vi IV V), and the `key` field records which key is in force so everything key-DEPENDENT follows while that part plays: quantize, scale-source re-rooting, roman numerals, and a transition's diatonic walk. Resolved by `_ambPartKeyNow()`, which is the `_ambSectionKeyNow` idiom — off the note's own `_ambKeyTime` stamp (or `_ambProgStepOverride` when a resolver set it), falling back to the audio clock for UI reads — and consulted at the top of **`_ambKeyRootPc` / `_ambKeyScaleName`**, i.e. the chokepoint everything key-shaped already goes through. Returns null unless some part carries a key, so the default path is untouched and the harness (no parts) is byte-identical. **THE TRAP that cost a debugging pass: the key was applied TWICE.** `_ambSrcRootPcRaw`'s prog branch calls `_ambProgKeyOffset(n, keyRoot)` to re-root the WHOLE progression onto the key — an AREA-level idea — and once `_ambKeyRootPc` started returning the part key, that offset re-applied the modulation on top of chords that were already transposed: a Chorus in F, whose stored chords are F and C, played A♯ and F. Hence **`_ambAreaKeyRootPc`** (the pre-part body), which `_ambProgKeyOffset`'s two call sites — `_ambSrcRootPcRaw` and `_ambProgViewShift` — must use. Rule: anything computing a PROGRESSION-WIDE transpose takes the area key; anything asking "what key is sounding right now" takes the part-aware one. **Static renderers need `_ambPartKeyForSlot(prog, slot)`** instead — the overview strip draws every chip at once and cannot use the time-resolved lookup, so its romans were all computed in the area key (the chorus's I read IV). `_ambRepairParts` builds a FRESH object per part, so `key` must be carried explicitly there or it is dropped on every normalize (the same trap the sections repair has). UI: **＋ Part opens a popover** (`_ambAddPartModal`) that picks Root/Key BEFORE the progression — a part is where a key change belongs, and it defaults to the area key so "another part, same key" stays one extra click; the part header then carries a `.ambient-pov-partkey` chip naming the key, whose menu can change or clear it (clearing does NOT transpose the chords back — the chords are the music, and silently re-keying written harmony because a label changed would be a surprise). Verified: Verse in C / Chorus in F reads `I IV | I V`, a bass plays `C F | F C`, and the key resolves per slot as `C C F F`.
- **TRANSITION slots (`{transition:true, bars}` in `prog.chords`) are a WALK from the chord before to the chord after — a slot with no harmony of its own.** Making it a real slot rather than a property of the following chord means the editor, overview strip, Scheduler lane and chord matrix all render it as a column with zero extra plumbing, and the PER-LAYER OPT-IN comes free: the matrix cell for that column already decides whether a layer plays there (leave it on to walk, set it to *never* to sit the bar out — verified 0 notes in the walk slot while the others keep playing). Resolution is a GATED early return at the top of `_ambProgCurrentChord`, before alts / reroll / salt (none of which apply — there is no written harmony to substitute), using the `pos` that `_ambProgStepAt` already stashes in `_ambProgPosHint` for salt colours, so the line advances WITHIN the slot with no new clock. `_ambTransitionLine` walks the AREA SCALE when a key is on (chromatic otherwise), takes the short way round, and is seeded on (slot, cycle, take) so it re-rolls with 🎲 New take yet loops identically in between. Two details that make it sound composed rather than interpolated: rounding STALLS when there are more beats than scale steps (F→A over four quarters gives F G G A), so an exact repeat sitting a whole tone below the next note is filled chromatically (→ F G G♯ A); and the last step sometimes approaches from the other side. Voiced with the OUTGOING chord's intervals, so the walk keeps the texture it is leaving. **FOUR places assume every slot has a root and needed guarding:** `_ambProgKeyOffset` (used `chords[0].root` — a LEADING transition would have re-rooted the whole progression onto C), the chord-matrix header's `chName` (`ch.root|0` → 0 → labelled it "C", indistinguishable from a real C), `_ambPeRoman` (a walk has no degree), and the prog EDITOR body (the note/quality/alternates machinery would write a root into it — transitions get their own body with just the length). `_ambCloneChord` and `_ambNormalizeProgMeta` both short-circuit on the flag and strip root/intervals/alts. Absent by default → harness/golden byte-identical by construction. Verified end-to-end with audio: a bass over C · F · ⇝ · Am walks `F G G♯ A` through the transition bar. **The editor PREVIEW plays a transition as a LINE, not a chord** — one note per step across the slot, an octave below the chords so it reads as the bass walk it is; the pitches come from `_ambTransitionChordAt` itself, so a preview can never drift from what will play (measured: `C4+E4+G4 · F4+A4+C5 · F3 G3 G♯3 A3 · A4+C5+E5`). `_ambTransitionSteps(bars)` is the ONE definition of the step count, shared by the preview and the emitter for that reason. **Colour**: one amber dashed accent shared by the editor strip (`.pe-trans`), overview strip (`.pov-trans`) and matrix header (`.pm-trans`) so a walk is recognisable wherever the progression is drawn — and a transition deliberately opts OUT of the in/out-of-key colouring, which is meaningless for a line whose whole job is to pass through.
- **Bloom progressions resolve the chord PER-ONSET (bar-aligned), not per-loop or per-ms.** A "Progression" note source's current chord = `_ambProgCurrentChord(n)`, which reads the module-global `_ambProgStepOverride`. Every emit that can play a prog (bass/run/pedal/drone/bed/motif/texture/arp) sets `_ambProgStepOverride = _ambProgStepAt(E, at)` — the bar-aligned chord step at THAT voice's onset `at` (`floor(barsElapsed/barsPerChord)`, anchored at `_playStartAt`/`_progAnchor`) — right before the pitch pick, then clears it. This replaced the legacy ms `progRateMs` clock (now an inert cfg field) and the per-series-loop model, so a multi-bar layer FOLLOWS the changes within its loop. Rate = `cfg.barsPerChord` (Configure → Bars/chord). All gated on `isProg`, so scale layers stay byte-identical (harness scale configs pinned; `prog-bass`/`prog-global`/`prog-arp` cover the new path). **GLOBAL progression**: `cfg.prog={on,name,chords}` (Configure → Prog); `_ambNotesOf` makes any layer on the default `scale:''` source INHERIT it via `_ambGlobalProg()` (explicit scale/chord/wrap/prog overrides). Activating it auto-snaps free continuous layers (bed/motif/texture/beat-random) to the nearest bar-ratio (`_ambAutoSyncFreeForProg`). **ARP**: its chord is `_chordIdx = _ambProgStepAt(_E, at)`, DECOUPLED from `st._loop` (now purely the structural entry-cursor + queue-sizing index); step-mutes/evolve key off `_chordIdx`. `_ambArpSeriesInfo` still sizes by `st._loop`'s chord — exact for uniform chord sizes, minor queue drift only for mixed sizes. **EUCLID ARP resolves pitch PER-HIT at the hit's scheduled time** (`_isProg` branch in the `_ambEmitArpEuclid` slot loop): it builds a whole phrase ahead, and the old once-per-voice pitch used the chord at BUILD time (`_E.progStep` = tick-time fallback) — hits past a chord boundary carried the PREVIOUS chord ("sloppy transitions", hit-or-miss with tick timing). Gated so non-prog is byte-identical (re-resolve consumes no rnd; keep the rnd draw ORDER: rVar → restP → pVary → lenVary). **SERIES pattern RESTARTS at each chord change** (`st._lastChordIdx` in `_ambEmitArp`): the sweep begins the new chord from the TOP of the pattern (classic arpeggiator). Before, the cursor ran CONTINUOUSLY through boundaries — every chord's bar started at an arbitrary rotation of the pattern and the pool-wrap leap landed mid-bar, which read as "sloppy/glitchy transitions" even though every pitch was chord-correct (a proportional remap was tried first and didn't fix the audible mess — the phase drift, not the size jump, was the real symptom). DELIBERATE baseline change: `prog-arp` re-recorded (4d144e5a → be2ab3a6, same count). Diagnose pattern-phase complaints by dumping the MIDI walk around boundaries (first note of each chord's bar should be the pattern start — the root for 'up'), not by checking pitch-class membership (which was always green). **Per-chord `bars` (VARIABLE-LENGTH chords):** a prog chord may carry its own `bars` (fractional OK); `_ambProgStepAt` walks cumulative per-chord lengths (`lens[]`, cycle = Σ) when ANY chord has `bars`, else the uniform `floor(bars/barsPerChord)` fast path (harness-pinned). The whole pipeline round-trips fractional bars: the prog editor edits them (`_ambParseBpc`/`_ambFmtBpc`, den ≤16 + triplets), `_ambCloneChord`/registry preserve them, `_cycleBars` sums them. **"Send saved seq → Bloom As Progression/Pad" PRESERVES STEP RHYTHM** (`_ambProgFromSteps`): each chord's `bars` = the step's `stepLengthFactor` (whole note = 1 bar), snapped to a 1/48-bar grid so quarters/eighths/triplets/16ths stay exact through the sums; consecutive identical chords MERGE (lengths add); a REST **sustains** the current chord across its own length (a prog is a continuous harmonic clock — no silence primitive — so the rest holds the harmony and keeps later changes on-beat); a LEADING rest (before any chord) is dropped (anchors at play start, no pre-roll). CAVEAT: `_ambProgSyncInfo` (bed/drone prog-sync re-voice sub-slots) divides by the UNIFORM `cfg.barsPerChord` (which has NO UI — the global input was removed; it's a fixed fallback of 1, real lengths are per-chord `bars`), so the plain voicing grid is 1-bar-based. **`progMerge` ("merge repeats", per Bed/Drone) spans the actual chord GROUP instead**: `_ambProgGroupsCycle`/`_ambProgGroupAt` collapse consecutive identical chords into one group (a chord that repeats / is multi-bar → one harmonic unit), and `_ambProgSpanAt(E,lc,cfg,atSec)` returns that group's sub-slot grid (`groupLen÷progSubdiv`) so N voicings spread across the whole run (4 voicings over a 2-bar chord = 2/bar). Wired at the emit (bed: onset interval + slot; drone: slot only, uniform cadence), the runLayer prog-sync ADVANCE (group-aware next onset), and the Scheduler block render (precomputed variable-width edges per group). `progMerge` is additive/boolean → kept OUT of defaults, read as truthy (no normalize). TRAP: `_ambEmitBed`/`_ambEmitDrone` have NO `cfg` in scope — pass `_E._cfg || _E.getCfg()` to `_ambProgSpanAt` or it throws and the (try-wrapped) emit is silently swallowed = no notes. Scheduler CHORD-GROUP borders (`.chgrp-a/-b/-start`) tint every layer's unit blocks by chord (respecting `progMerge` on the bed/drone) so you can see which units are in which chord.
- **Euclid init is STOCHASTIC at UI-add-time, `takeReroll` re-rolls rhythm per New-take, and layer presets can be MULTI-layer — all keep `_ambDefaultLayer` deterministic so the harness (which builds from it directly) is byte-identical.** (1) `_ambEuclidStochasticInit(L)` rolls a random euclidean pattern (pulses in a musical band, random rotate) using `Math.random` (UI-time, like `_ambApplyRandomInstVoice` — NOT the seeded `_ambRand` stream); called from `_ambAddExtra` for a bass + the arp→euclid toggle. `_ambDefaultLayer` stays fixed (bass 5/8) so the harness/preset-are-today invariants hold. (2) A layer flagged `takeReroll` re-rolls its rhythm on 🎲 New take: for a euclid layer `_ambApplyTakeReroll(L, cfg.seed)` DERIVES `euclidRegen` from the take seed (the salt path in `_ambEuclidVoicePat` re-rolls pulses±2+rotation deterministically from it → new rhythm per take, reproducible: same take ID = same rhythm); a random-gen Beat already re-rolls off `cfg.seed` so it's a no-op there. Wired in the New-take handler (iterate primaries+extras) + `_ambAddPreset`. `takeReroll` is additive/absent-by-default → survives normalize (not in `_ambDefaultLayer`, like `euclidKit`), harness untouched. (3) `_ambAddPreset` now accepts `preset.layers = [{type,cfg}…]` (multi-layer) alongside single `{type,cfg}` — the **"🥁 Battery"** factory preset (`_FACTORY_LAYER_PRESETS`) is Beat+Bass, both `takeReroll`. All verified harness byte-identical (30 configs) + reproducible-rhythm.
- **Prog v6 = CHAINS (`prog.parts`) + VERSIONS (`prog.versions`) + per-slot ALTERNATES (`chord.alts`) — all additive metadata on `cfg.prog`, coerced by `_ambNormalizeProgMeta` which DELETES empties so plain progs stay byte-identical.** `cfg.prog.chords` stays the flat played stream (engine unchanged, harness byte-identical — proven by a HEAD-vs-branch `__bloomHarness.run()` diff across all 33 configs). Three additions: (1) **parts** `[{name,len}]` label contiguous runs of chords as named sub-progressions ("progression of progressions" — Verse→Chorus→…); a VIEW over the flat list, kept consistent by `_ambRepairParts(parts,total)` which clamps Σlen to `chords.length` (final part swallows the remainder) and returns **undefined** for a trivial single-part cover (so legacy/one-part progs carry no `parts` — the UI renders an implicit single part). (2) **versions** `[{name,chords,parts}]` = whole-prog snapshots + `versionIdx`; switching is a CONFIG edit (`_ambProgSwitchVersion` swaps chords+parts into the live fields), NOT an engine change — KNOWN SIMPLIFICATION: editing chords after a switch diverges the live prog from the highlighted snapshot until you re-add a version. (3) **alts**: a chord may carry `alts:[{root,intervals,bars?}]` + `altMode` ('cycle'|'random'). This is the ONLY engine touch — a GATED branch in `_ambProgCurrentChord`: no `alts` → the exact prior `return base` (zero RNG, byte-identical). The pick is a PURE function of `(idx, cycle, seed)` where `cycle = floor(step/len)` off the MONOTONIC `_ambProgStepAt` step — so the >1 calls per onset (root via `_ambSrcRootPc` + intervals via `_ambScaleIntervals`) resolve the SAME alt (a stream-draw would desync root from intervals). 'random' uses a DEDICATED `_ambSeededRand(hash)`, never the shared `_ambRand()` stream the harness hashes, so no downstream draw shifts. `_ambCloneChord` extended to preserve alts (gated on non-empty). UI: the **overview strip** `_ambRenderProgOverview` (hooked into `_ambSyncFxVis` beside `_ambRenderChordMatrix`; playhead glow `_ambProgOverviewPlayhead` in `_ambVizFrame`) is the chain/version manager — wrapping chord chips grouped under part headers, `×N` alt badges, delegated POINTERDOWN (repaints on the viz timer → click drops). The chord EDITOR (`_ambPeRender`/`_ambPeAct`) gained diatonic ▲▽ (whole-chord + per-note, hidden on ≥12-note scales), `_ambDiatonicShiftPc(pc,delta,keyRoot,scale)` (pc-space, AREA-key — do NOT reuse 08-grid-modes' freq/workspace helpers), a Clone op, and an Alternates row (◆ base + ◇ alts as selectable edit targets via `ed.altSel`). Reminder: this pane is master-only chrome so `ambient-*` ids become `mix-bloom-*` (`_ambTrId`) — query the namespaced id in tests. Schema bumped 5→6. **KEY-axis UI is now TWO INDEPENDENT TOGGLES, not the old 3-way Chromatic|Key|Progression segmented selector** (`keyOn` and `prog.on` were always separate fields; the old selector artificially coupled them): a **Key toggle** (`#ambient-key-toggle`, label flips Chromatic↔Key, active=keyOn) in the Key subsection + a **Progression toggle** (`#ambient-prog-onoff`, active=prog.on) in the Progression subsection. Both can be on = a progression diatonic to a key (key-sub controls stay visible under prog). Sub-control visibility keys off `keyOn`/`prog.on` directly (NOT `_ambResolveKey().mode`, which still resolves to 'progression' when prog wins — used only for the header indicator + hints). Toggling Progression off RETAINS the chords.
- **The euclid Beat's DRUM-LANES mode (`inst.euclidKit`) is a step SEQUENCER over all 8 fixed drum lanes — a drum machine in one layer.** The "Drum lanes" toggle (`euclidkit` token) flips the euclid grid into all 8 named kit lanes (Kick/Snare/Hat/Clap/Open/Tom/Crash/Perc = `_AMB_VDRUM`, `_AMB_KIT_LANES`=8), each its OWN editable step sequence. It is NOT euclidean-generated: `_ambEuclidPat` returns an all-zero row for a kit lane with no `euclidPattern[v]` override (a lane you haven't drawn is silent), so enabling it SEEDS a basic backbeat (`_ambSeedDrumLanes` — Kick 1&3, Snare 2&4, Hat every step; won't clobber an existing valid kit pattern). The euclidean-generator knobs (Pulses/Rotate/Regen/Voices) + the single-drum "Drum" picker are HIDDEN in kit mode (`_ambBeatGenVis` `kit` branch + the `kitVis` closure in `_ambWireEuclidGrid`); only Steps (resolution) + Phrase stay. A Steps change RE-SEEDS (not clears) so it never goes silent (`onGen` clear-branch re-seeds when `euclidKit`). Reuses ALL euclid window/anchor/phrase machinery — NOT a new `gen` mode, so none of the ~15 `gen==='euclid'` dispatch sites changed. GATED: `euclidKit` default-absent → the classic single/auto-kit Beat, byte-identical (harness `beat-euclid` unchanged). Shared by primary + added Beat cards via one schema token. **PAGES (pattern banks):** a kit layer's beat is a bank — `euclidPages: [{pat:[8 rows], plays}]`, `euclidPageIdx` = the page shown/edited via TABS above the grid, `euclidSeq` = Loop-viewed-page vs play-all-in-Sequence (each page loops `plays` times, then advances — like Areas' single/sequence). The grid reads/writes the VIEWED page (`_ambEuclidViewPat`); the EMITTER reads the page active for the emit cycle `ctx.c` (`_ambEuclidActivePat(inst, c)` — cycle → page by summing `plays`), so page-sequencing is deterministic + phrase-anchored. Legacy single-`euclidPattern` kit beats migrate to a one-page bank lazily (`_ambEuclidPages`). Steps/Bars change RE-FITS every page (`_ambEuclidResizePages`, preserves overlapping edits). Adding a page duplicates the current one. Page tabs DRAG-REORDER (pointer-based → touch + desktop, `setPointerCapture`): a horizontal drag past a 6px threshold reorders `euclidPages` via `_ambEuclidMovePage` (= the play sequence; the viewed page follows its content); a plain click falls through to select. A drag suppresses the trailing click (`_suppressTabClick`, one-shot + 350ms failsafe) so the reorder isn't also read as a select. `.ambient-euclid-tab[data-page]` has `touch-action:pan-y` (horizontal = reorder, vertical = scroll). Tabs/orchestration wire via delegation on the grid container (survive re-renders); `euclidPages`/`euclidPageIdx`/`euclidSeq` are additive (NOT in defaults — same boolean-backfill trap below). Tabs + orchestration render on ONE row (`.ambient-euclid-pagebar`). **PER-STEP FX:** each step can be shaped — stored SPARSE per page as `pg.fx["v:i"] = {vel,len,rat,prob,pan}` (only for touched steps; absent → defaults 100/100/1/100 so the emitter + grid are unchanged for plain steps). The emitter (kit path, per hit) reads `_ambStepFxGet(activePage,v,absI)`: `prob`<100 rng-gates the hit, `rat`>1 retriggers N sub-notes across the slot (a roll), `vel` scales `bp.volume`, `len` scales note length, `pan` (≠0) OVERRIDES the layer/spread pan. **"SOUND" vs "PITCH" (two distinct per-step fields):** `pitch` = **"Sound"** — a ± semitone NOTE offset that SELECTS a different drum for that step (a snare on the kick lane, a cowbell…) via the kit's per-semitone zone map / `snapDrumKitFreq`; the inspector readout NAMES the drum (`_ambStepSoundName` → `_AMB_DRUM_NAMES[pc]`, updates live on drag). `tune` = **"Pitch"** — a REAL ± semitone tune of the SELECTED drum, applied as a `playbackRate` multiply INSIDE `_resolveSampleVoice` (new 4th `drumTune` arg, threaded from `params.drumTune` through ALL THREE sample play paths: core WASM `@2656`, node ADSR `@289` via `opts.drumTune`, slice `@3679`) so it pitches the SAME drum in place. You CANNOT do this via the note/detune: a tr808 maps one drum per semitone (C2–B2), so any non-octave note/detune shift lands on a NEIGHBOUR (`_resolveSampleVoice` picks the buffer by nearest MIDI). Synth kit has no zones → Sound+Pitch BOTH add to the synth drum's real pitch (`_ambPlaySynthDrum` stPitch arg). **GOTCHA that cost a debug pass:** the page-fx normalize (`17-ambient.js` ~7833-7839) must both COERCE every new field AND include it in the all-default→delete check — `tune` was silently dropped on EVERY `getCfg` (a direct `_ambStepFxGet` read 12, but the emit saw 0) until `tune` was added to both. Golden/harness byte-identical (drumTune opt-in, no config sets it). **EDIT SCOPE = "one drum, or All" (Stage 3):** the old Lane/Column segmented toggle is now a single **"All drums"** switch (`.ambient-stepmode-all`, still `data-emode` lane↔column under the hood) — OFF edits just the drum you clicked (a lane NAME selects it, `_editMode='lane'` + `_editLane`), ON edits every lane (`column`). **PER-LANE RAMPS (Stage 4):** a deterministic sine LFO on Pan and/or Pitch, per drum lane — `L.laneRamps[v] = {panD,panR,pitchD,pitchR}` (Depth + Rate in cycles/bar), SPARSE + absent-by-default (`_ambLaneRampGet/Set`, coerced in `_ambNormalizeEuclidPattern`). The emit adds `depth*sin(2π*rate*rAt/bar)` (of ABSOLUTE play-time, so it evolves smoothly across the native loop — NOT reset per cycle) onto each hit's `panEff`/`tuneEff`. NO RNG → the kit stays deterministic/native-loop. UI rows show only in single-lane scope (`.ambient-lane-ramp-row`, a Depth|Rate pair per axis); the ramp sliders carry `.ambient-step-fx-sl` (so the Stage-1 `_unitRefresh` exclusion keeps them from wiping mid-drag) + `data-ramp` (the input handler branches on it). Verified: hat lane pans ±90 / pitches ±8 st across 8 phases; survives normalize; 390px no h-scroll. **Drum-lanes CENTER by default** — the per-voice pan FAN (`(v-(V-1)/2)*40`) is a polyphonic-euclid feature and is SKIPPED for `euclidKit` (`V===1 || euclidKit` → `vpan = pan`); at 8 lanes it panned Kick+Snare hard left, and since the per-note panner sits BEFORE the reverb-send tap (voice→vcf→…→levelGain), that left-panned the reverb too ("reverb only in the left channel" + "voices sound effected"). Pan the kit via the per-step Pan or the layer's Space instead. UI: a step is SELECTED by click (also toggles), long-press/right-click (select only), OR by clicking its **lane-name label** (a `.ambient-euclid-drumlbl` BUTTON `data-lane` that focuses the lane — highlights the row `.lane-sel` + label `.on` — and keeps the current step index); `inst._selStep` (transient, NOT persisted-stripped since normalize runs every getCfg) drives an inline inspector (Vel/Length/Ratchet/Prob/Pan sliders + Reset + a **▶ Play** audition = `_ambTriggerLaneStep`, which plays the lane's drum once at the step's vel/len) below the grid; a shaped step shows an amber corner dot (`.has-fx`), the selected one a ring (`.sel`). **ALL page/mode/plays/lane chrome are BUTTONS, never `<select>`** — the mode is a segmented `.ambient-euclid-seg` Loop/Seq toggle (`data-seq`) and Plays a `− N× +` stepper (`data-plays`), because a native `<select>` inside the re-renderable grid could be replaced mid-open by a `render()` (from the grid's own change handler or `_ambRefreshEuclidGrids`), silently dropping the pick — the "Sequence never sticks, reverts to Loop page" bug. All chrome + the audition wire via ONE delegated click handler on the grid (`data-seq`/`data-plays`/`data-lane`/`data-page` + class checks); the trigger `return`s before `render()` (no state change). **LIVE PLAYHEAD:** `_ambEuclidStepPlayheads(E)` (called each frame from `_ambVizFrame`, cleared by `_ambClearEuclidPlayheads` on stop) lights the column currently sounding — it derives the step from `runPhase[key].startAt` at the Bloom bpm (`floor(((Tone.now()-startAt) % loopSec) / slotSec)`, same anchor as the emitter) and toggles `.playing` on every cell with that `data-ei` (all lanes). Cached on `grid._phStep` so the DOM is touched only when the step changes — but the cache re-verifies the class is still applied (a grid re-render wipes it). CSS: OFF cells in the column tint, a firing (`.on.playing`) cell flashes green. Works for kit and non-kit euclid grids. The SAME frame also marks the **now-playing PAGE tab** in Sequence mode: `_ambEuclidActivePageIdx(L, floor((now-startAt)/loopSec))` → the tab whose `data-page` matches gets `.playing` (green glow + ▸), distinct from the viewed/edited tab (`.on`), so you can edit one page while another sounds. Only in Sequence with >1 page (in Loop the viewed tab IS playing); cached on `grid._phPage`, cleared on stop. **UP-NEXT countdown:** on the playing page's LAST loop (`_ambEuclidPageRunInfo(L,c).remaining === 1`) the NEXT page's tab gets `.up-next` (amber pulse + ⤳), so the user sees the change is one iteration away; cached on `grid._phNext`, cleared on stop. Harness-safe: default step + non-kit → `sfx` null, `rat` 1, one note with the same RNG draw (`beat-euclid` unchanged). (An earlier draft used a per-lane drum `<select>` + `Voices`-driven lane count; scrapped for the fixed-8-lane machine — the dropdown was cramped and users wanted all lanes shown. `_ambLaneDrumPc`/`euclidDrums` survive for a possible future reassign, but the UI no longer sets them.) **CSS:** the step chips are a grid `repeat(var(--eucols),minmax(0,1fr))` where `--eucols = min(steps,16)` (so >16-step lanes WRAP onto extra rows instead of shrinking) with `.ambient-euclid-cell{min-width:0;padding:0;overflow:hidden}` — so the per-cell number label is centered+clipped and can NEVER resize a cell or break column alignment (the old flex-wrap + content-width cells misaligned once step numbers hit 2 digits). In kit mode the whole control breaks out of the `.ambient-ctrl` 3-col grid (`.ambient-euclid-kitctrl{display:block}`, toggled by `kitVis` + the token) so the lane labels sit in the "Pattern" label gutter and the cells get the full row width. **THE NORMALIZE TRAP that cost a debugging pass:** `_normalizeAmbientCfg`'s per-layer backfill (`Object.keys(d[layer]).forEach … else if (!Number.isFinite(cfg[layer][k])) cfg[layer][k]=d[layer][k]`) treats ANY non-number default field as "missing" — `Number.isFinite(true)===false` — so a BOOLEAN field added to a layer's DEFAULT object gets clobbered back to the default every normalize (getCfg runs normalize each call). Fix: keep additive booleans/arrays OUT of the layer defaults (like `euclidPattern`/`euclidKit`) and coerce them in `_ambNormalizeEuclidPattern` when present. If you must default a boolean layer field, add an explicit `k==='<field>'` boolean case to that backfill loop.
- **A Design patch's PITCH ENVELOPE (`patch.pitchEnv{on,amount(st),attack(ms),decay(ms)}`) is a dedicated bend from `base+Amount → base` — it powers drum boom→thud (kick/tom) and has TWO independent engine paths that BOTH need wiring.** CORE: `dsp/src/lib.rs` carries `d_pe_amt/atk/dec` (flag 64, dp[55..57] from `03b-core-voices.js designParams`), applied as `m[0] += d_pe_amt * adsr_held(tn,atk,dec,0)` in the control-rate block (CORE_REV bumped; golden re-baselined). NODE: the bend is scheduled onto `refs.synth.detune` inside `_sdBuildModRig` (20-sound-design.js) BEFORE its `if (!matrix.length) return` early-return, so a patch with ONLY a pitchEnv (no modMatrix) still bends. **THE GOTCHA that cost a debugging pass:** `_sdBuildModRig`'s CALL SITES in `04-instruments-samples.js` (the scheduled path ~4099 and the held path ~2937) were gated on `params.modMatrix && params.modMatrix.length` — so the drum presets (which carry pitchEnv but NO modMatrix) never called it and the node fallback lost the pitch drop entirely. Both gates now also fire on `(params.pitchEnv && params.pitchEnv.on && params.pitchEnv.amount)`. If you add another `_sdBuildModRig` call site or another pitchEnv consumer, open the gate for pitchEnv there too. Harness/golden-safe: pitchEnv defaults `on:false`, so dormant patches are byte-identical (flag 64 unset, matrix-gate unchanged).
- **Seamless Bloom AREA transitions = stop-generating + let tails ring + reuse shared chains (NO bus dip, NO full teardown).** `_ambOrchAdvance` hands off between areas gaplessly by: (1) `cancelBloomFutureVoices(key, now)` for each layer — this cancels only **pending** (not-yet-started) voices, so currently-sounding voices ring out (release/reverb tails overlap the incoming area = the gapless feel); (2) swapping the active area + reseeding + `_ambResetClocks` (which does NOT clear `E.mod`); (3) a **soft reconcile** — `_ambBuildMod`/update every key in the new area's `_ambWantSet` (shared keys like `bed` keep their chain so tails are uninterrupted, params just update), and for keys only in the OLD area, `_ambGateRamp(…,0,…,0.5)` then dispose after ~0.8 s (no teardown click). An earlier version dipped the bloom bus to 0 and `_ambTeardownMods()`’d everything — that was glitch-free but audibly gapped; don't reintroduce a dip. **Reconfiguring a SHARED chain while its tail rings POPS** (EQ splice / FX re-route / `_ambSyncTarget` LFO dispose+recreate are graph changes = clicks). Fix (`_ambReconfigSharedQuiet`): mute that layer's OWN dry gate ~6 ms, apply the graph change while muted, unmute — localized so the bus stays seamless. (The reverb send taps the VCA *pre-gate*, so a layer's reverb tail keeps ringing through the gate mute — dry-only areas can have a ~20 ms blip, reverb-bearing areas mask it.) Caveats: `cancelBloomFutureVoices` matches by layer KEY not engine, so a lane Bloom playing the same key simultaneously can lose pending voices at a master area switch; and a purely-percussive area (no tails) can have a ~0.3 s pickup gap (the new area's first voices are at the standard `lead`).
- **Unit-synced layers share ONE phase grid (`E._barGridAnchor`) — anchors snap to it via `_ambUnitGridSnap`, and a Unit edit while playing re-anchors through `_ambUnitReanchor`.** Layers used to anchor at their own private `lead`, so two layers "locked to the same unit" were only in phase if they happened to anchor in the same tick; flipping Unit-Sync mid-play (or enabling a layer later) left a permanent constant phase offset — audio AND status bars ("Bed slightly ahead of Arp"). Now every anchor site (step `C[key]`, the window layers' `startAt`, both arp paths) snaps a synced layer's anchor to the next grid point, the step advance self-heals onto the grid (also fixes global-Sync's snapped-step slowly drifting off the exact period), and `_ambWireUnitSync`'s handlers call `_ambUnitReanchor` (cancel future voices + drop phase state → next tick re-anchors snapped; gated on `_ambLiveApplyOK`). ALL of it is gated on `unit.mode==='sync'` + `Number.isFinite(E._barGridAnchor)` — only `_masterEng` ever sets the anchor, so lane engines and the harness are byte-identical by construction, and at play start `lead === _barGridAnchor` so the snap is identity (no first-note delay; measured 0.15 s with a 4-bar unit). **`_ambProgSyncInfo`'s chord sub-grid anchor is this SAME grid** (`_barGridAnchor`, playStart fallback) — it originally used `_playStartAt`, which differs from the lead grid by ~60–150 ms, so a prog-synced Bed sat a small constant fraction (measured 0.039 bar) AHEAD of a same-unit Arp; chord RESOLUTION (`_ambProgStepAt`) deliberately stays on `_playStartAt` (lead-grid onsets land just after each boundary → resolve INTO the new chord). If you add a third grid-anchored subsystem, anchor it on `_barGridAnchor` too. Status bars are truthful phase readouts — if they're offset, the AUDIO is offset; fix the engine anchor, not the bar math. (Related: the bed/motif viz branch now uses `_ambLayerPeriodSec` like every other branch, not its own snapped `iv`.)
- **Freeze/Write replay handoff: the REPLAY owns the loop-boundary note, and needs a one-tick GRACE to actually fire it.** At `_ambLockEngage` the barLock branch cancels the layer's live voices ≥ the boundary — INCLUSIVE, and `cancelBloomFutureVoices` sweeps the ACTIVE set too (`_akAt >= fromAt`, 04:~2260), so the live twin of the boundary note is stolen even if it just started (by design — the replay's captured copy owns it). But the gate runs on the ~150 ms tick, so `now` is already PAST the boundary when `_ambReplayFrozen` first runs — clamping `from` to `now` skipped the captured t=0 event → NOBODY played the phrase's first chord ("first chord of the first replay iteration silent"). Fix: `from = max(now − 0.22, anchor)` — playNote fires past-due notes immediately; ≤0.2 s late beats a hole. DIAGNOSTIC TRAP: a `playNote`-wrapper probe counts CALLS, not sounds — engage-time cancellation happens AFTER the calls were recorded, so live+replay both appear in the log ("8 notes") with no audible double; attribute emissions (e.g. tag with `fs.frozen` at emit) AND remember cancelled voices stay in the call log.
- **EVERY playhead/progress branch must guard the pre-first-onset lead — third instance of the same bug class.** A layer's bar computed from `next` (the future onset) without checking whether the FIRST onset has happened renders "~full" right after Play (prog = 1 − lead/P ≈ 92%) and completes at the first note — users read it as "a phantom unit ending, then the band kicks in." Texture and the generic branch gained the guard first; the SEQ branch was missed until 2026-07 (b615e89). The pattern: `pos = iters − (next − now)/P; if (pos >= 0) prog = pos % 1; else empty`. Any NEW layer-type playhead branch needs this guard from day one.
- **The When-cursor cell index is derived, not counted, for phase-anchored layers.** The "When" grid highlight used to ride a wrap-counter seeded to −1 on activation (it assumed the layer goes active during the pre-onset *lead*, so the first onset's wrap bumps it to cell 0). But phase-anchored layers (arp / bass / run / pedal / drone / shape / euclid) go active **AT** the first onset (`now ≥ st.startAt`), not during the lead — so the first onset had no wrap to bump 0 and the cursor trailed the audio by exactly one unit. Fix: in the readout RAF (`_ambUpdatePlayheadsCore`, the `.ambient-ph` loop — this was `_ambSyncBars` when the note was written) compute `cyc = Math.floor((now − st.startAt) / period)` — the SAME absolute index scheduling gates `_ambCondFires(when, c)` by — and use it directly (`el._whenCycle = cyc`). It's self-correcting (no dependence on catching the activation edge). Lead-anchored layers (seq/texture/interval, driven off `E.clocks` future onsets) keep the −1-then-wrap counter. `_ambLayerPeriodSec` for a series arp = the whole **series-loop** length (`totalNotes × interval`), which is exactly what When gates, so `cyc` lines up.
- **The Bloom invariant harness hashes `pan`, and the RNG is one shared sequential stream.** `23-bloom-harness.js` records `[at,freq,durMs,voice,pan,level]` per note, so changing a layer's pan VALUE *or* adding/removing an `_ambRand()` call (which shifts every downstream draw) drifts the baseline. The battery covers the SERIES arp (`withExtra('arp')`) but not the euclid arp, so euclid-only pan changes are free; for harness-covered paths, only override the pan *value* when the feature is engaged and keep the RNG draws identical at the defaults (`space:0`).
- **Per-note pan (`params.pan` → playNote's per-voice `Tone.Panner`) does NOT give a Bloom layer audible stereo spread, and is too costly for a dense layer.** ROOT CAUSE (found in Phase-2 calibration): the layer's output `Tone.Panner` is `channelCount 1` — it MONO-DOWNMIXES the whole layer (0.707/ch at pan 0, the level the mix is calibrated on), so ALL upstream width collapses (measured width 0.000 for spread 100, node engine). **Under CORE STRIPS (`bloopsCoreStrips`) spread now actually works**: the in-core pan applies the node law to the MID (level parity) but passes the SIDE at 0.707 (scaled out toward hard pan) — a deliberate improvement the node engine can't have without re-levelling the mix. ALSO: the primaries' + seq's Stereo control used to pass `sync=null` to `_ambWireSpread`, so Pan-mode drags did NOTHING while playing (both engines) until the next rebuild — they now pass a guarded `_ambSyncMods` callback like the extras always did. For the Euclid Arp it failed twice: (1) at the default 1 voice `_ambSpacePans(1,…)` returns `[0]` (centre); (2) even with non-zero per-note pan the image stayed centred AND a panner-per-hit on a dense arp glitched the render thread (the per-note `Tone.Panner` → `_ambLayerDest` = the mod-chain input `vcf` apparently doesn't survive to the bus as stereo). Pan must be applied at `e.pan` (the layer's output Panner, `_E.mod[key].pan.pan` — the one node confirmed to reach the bus in stereo). **But do NOT schedule it PER HIT** (`setValueAtTime`/`setTargetAtTime` per note): that accumulates automation events on a *persistent* param, a slow build-up that glitches and only clears on Stop→Play (disposing the node) — and turning Spread off mid-play does NOT clear it, so it looks like "glitches regardless of spread." **Audio-safe fix: one persistent `Tone.LFO` per arp layer driving `e.pan.pan`** (`_ambArpSpreadLfo`): bounded (a single node, ZERO per-note scheduling → no timeline growth), click-free, width = Spread, ~2 cycles/bar so consecutive notes land at different points (reads as spread). Disengage/recentre in Pan mode or at `space 0`; tear it down in `_ambTeardownMod` (before disposing `e.pan`) and in the series-arp path (`_ambEmitArpWindow`) so a euclid→series switch drops it. **General rule: never schedule per-note/per-hit automation on a long-lived AudioParam — use a connected LFO/Signal, or per-voice nodes that get disposed.** (The euclid Beat still uses per-note pan via `vpan`; same caveat applies if it ever needs spread.) Fan by SLOT position (`_ambSpacePans(steps, space)[slot]`) so a single voice sweeps L→R with the rhythm. Only when `panMode !== 'pan' && space > 0`; otherwise leave `e.pan` to `_ambApplyLayerPan`. Caveats: `e.pan` is shared by the layer so voices can't pan independently (slot sweep only), the reverb send taps pre-pan (wet stays centred), and `_ambApplyLayerPan` (run on any mod-sync) sets `e.pan.value=0` — momentarily cancelling the scheduled sweep until the next tick re-schedules. The automation timeline grows over a continuous play but resets on Stop/Play (mod teardown).
- **Bloom layer PAN is on a per-layer `Panner` node, not (only) per-voice.** Bloom applies pan at note-onset, so a pan *ramp* via the layer's `space` would only re-pan per note (coarse/inaudible for sustained or sparse layers). Fix: the mod chain has a `pan` Panner (`vca → gate → pan → [FX] → bus`). In **Pan** mode `_ambLayerPan`/`_ambLayerPans` return 0 (per-voice centred) and the Panner holds the position — set from `space` by `_ambApplyLayerPan`, and driven CONTINUOUSLY by the pan ramp (`_ambRampResolve` special-cases `space` → pushes to `e.pan.pan`). In **Spread** mode the Panner stays centred and per-voice pans fan the width. So a "pan ramp" only sweeps the image in **Pan** mode; in Spread mode it animates width.
- **The FIRST tick's lead floor must clear playNote's 0.25s DEFER threshold on a COLD press, or every layer's first voices fight over one bounded 30ms build slice against an empty pool.** Symptom: "after a while of not playing, layers clumsily/jitterily cascade on; stop/play a few times and it's fine". Cause: `_ambStartGenerator` anchors the first onsets to the PRESS (`pressAt + 0.15`) with a `now + 0.06` fallback for when the synchronous chain build eats that margin — and on a cold press it always does. Measured on a 5-layer rig: each layer's first note had **28-63ms** between its `playNote` call and its scheduled onset when cold, vs **127-133ms** warm. Under 0.25s a voice takes the URGENT path (bounded 30ms slice) instead of the paced `_voiceBuildQueue`, so some first voices make their onset and some don't — the stagger IS the cascade. **CORRECTION to an earlier version of this note: do NOT explain that as "an empty voice pool" — synth-body pooling was REMOVED in 2026-07 and every note builds a fresh synth.** What is actually cheaper on a warm press is JIT warmth on the construction path (and whatever `_bloopsWarmup` did on the first pointerdown), not a pool. Tested and NOT reproduced: forcing `gc()` with heap churn, headless AND headful, changes nothing — which is expected once you know there is no pool to collect. Fix: the first-tick floor is adaptive — `_AMB_LEAD_COLD` 0.35s when cold, `_AMB_LEAD_WARM` 0.06s otherwise, where COLD = first play of the page OR more than `_AMB_COLD_IDLE_SEC` (20s) since the last stop (`_ambLastStopAt` is stamped in `_ambStopGenerator`). Verified: cold margin 28-63ms → 338-350ms, idle-then-press re-arms it, and the very next press drops back to the warm 127ms. Cost is deliberate and cold-only: press-to-sound 0.26s → 0.45s on that one press. **What that verification does NOT establish: that the reported symptom is gone.** It proves the MECHANISM behaves as designed (margins, classification), measured headless. The symptom is intermittent by the user's own description ("stop/play a few times and it's fine"), it has never been reproduced in this environment, and one clean field press is not evidence of a fix — a non-occurrence of an intermittent fault is weak. Treat this as "a plausible cause was found and removed", not "resolved", until there is a REPRODUCIBLE case. If you tune these, remember the threshold that actually matters is 0.25s (04's defer gate), not the audible latency.
- **Bloom's first iteration is dropped on a cold-start play** (symptom: "no notes for several iterations, especially Bed"). Pressing Play resumes the AudioContext *asynchronously*; if `_ambTick` SCHEDULES while the context is still `suspended`, its voices anchor to a frozen clock and the first onset is dropped on resume — worst on Bed (long iteration = seconds of silence). Fix: in `_ambStartGenerator` start the interval + flip `cfg.playing`/the Play button IMMEDIATELY (do NOT defer the whole start — that left the button on ▶ and added a ~1 s delay), and instead gate only the SCHEDULING: `_ambTick` returns early while `Tone.getContext().rawContext.state !== 'running'`. The interval keeps ticking, so the first running tick (≤150 ms after resume) anchors cleanly; `_ambStartGenerator` also kicks one extra tick from `Tone.start().then(...)` so cold starts don't wait a full interval. **CRITICAL: this scheduling guard must apply at COLD START ONLY** (gate it behind `E._everRan`, set true on the first 'running' tick). If it keeps blocking whenever the context isn't 'running', a mid-playback context dip (tab backgrounded / power blip) starves the schedule and turns a momentary glitch into a full cut-out — the keep-alive watchdog + 1.4 s lookahead already cover steady-state, so never block once it has run.
- **EVERY area transition is a SECTION transition (depart + per-layer fade), NOT the seamless reconcile, and NOT gated on Bar Lock.** `_ambOrchAdvance` departs every outgoing layer via `_ambDepartLayer` — its chain is moved to a unique `key#depN` key, gate-faded to 0 over that layer's `areaFadeMs`, then (after the fade) its sounding voices are STOPPED via `stopBloomVoicesBefore(key, at)` and the chain disposed. TWO non-obvious musts: (1) the stop/teardown `setTimeout` is timed from the FUTURE boundary `at` (`(at-Tone.now())*1000 + fadeMs + 60`), NOT the advance-call time — the advance fires ~0.6 s BEFORE the boundary, so a delay measured from it disposed the chain BEFORE the gate-fade (scheduled at `at`) even ran, killing the fade. (2) The gate-fade only silences GATE-ROUTED voices; looping sample/pad voices (`_activePadVoices`, and one-shot sample voices) would ring on + the new area retriggers more → "samples run over and get louder". `stopBloomVoicesBefore(key, beforeAt)` (04-instruments-samples.js) stops active+pending synth/sample/pad voices with `_ak===key && _akAt < beforeAt` (outgoing area only; incoming voices start ≥ boundary so they're spared) — pad voices are now `_ak`-tagged at creation so they're targetable. So departure = gate-fade (the audible fade) + voice-stop (the guarantee) — while the incoming area builds FRESH chains on the real keys. Area fade applies ONLY to **Free** layers (the slider, default 250 ms, normalized in `mig`); **synced / bar-native layers HARD-CUT at the boundary** (fade forced to 0 in `_ambAreaFadeMap` via `_ambIsCapturable`) — they're loop/structure, so the slider doesn't apply. EVERY area transition uses this depart path now (NOT gated on barLock — barLock only governs whether layers LOOP); the old shared-chain reconcile (`_ambReconfigSharedQuiet` + old-only gate-ramp) is no longer used by the advance. DON'T re-gate the depart on `barLock`/`_barLockLoopBars` — Free layers have `_barLockLoopBars=0` and would fall through and never fade. Reason for departing to a temp key: a layer has ONE chain per key, so to overlap old-fading + new-fresh for the same key you must move the old one aside first. The advance times off `_barGridAnchor` (the lead grid, reset per advance), not `_orchStartAt`, AND fires EARLY with a GENEROUS margin (~0.6 s lead) so the advance always fires BEFORE the boundary even under tick jank (the tick handler can take 50–70 ms). It passes the precise boundary to `_ambOrchAdvance(E, boundaryAt)`, which sets `at = boundaryAt > now ? boundaryAt : now` and cancels pending voices + starts each layer's fade AT `at`. CRITICAL: the lead must be big enough that `at` is the FUTURE boundary, not a late `now` — if the advance fires AFTER the boundary, `at` falls back to `now` and the outgoing SYNCED layers (which can't cancel already-started voices) bleed past the boundary = overflow. A too-small lead (0.3 s) caused exactly that under jank. The incoming area is anchored to the boundary (`E._barGridAnchor = at`), so its synced layers' first onsets land on the next bar AT the boundary, not before it (overlap) — both the outgoing cut and the incoming start sit on the boundary.

- **ENGINE TIME vs AUDIBLE TIME at an area boundary: `_ambOrchAdvance` fires ~0.6 s EARLY (it must — voices are scheduled ahead) and flips `_playIdx` + `_barGridAnchor` immediately, so EVERY readout jumped a beat early until it was taught the difference.** Three separate symptoms, one cause: the `▶ <name>` header switched at the advance; the bar counter froze (its `bars = (now − G)/barSec` went negative against the future anchor); and every layer status bar rendered EMPTY for the last half-second of an area that was still sounding. The handoff is recorded in the advance — `_orchPrevIdx` / `_orchPrevAnchor` / `_orchBoundaryAt`, captured BEFORE the swap — and resolved by **`_ambAudibleOrch(E, tnow)`**, which returns the outgoing area + its grid while `tnow < boundary`. Used by the bar counter, the ▶ option marker, and `_ambViewIsPlaying`. **A SWEEP of every `_playIdx`/`_ambPlayCfg` consumer found two more display surfaces that resolve a CHORD from engine state and so jumped early — `_ambHeaderChord` (the app-header chord pill, reported as "switching to the next area's 1 chord too early") and `_ambProgOverviewPlayhead`.** Both now run inside **`_ambAsAudibleArea(E, fn)`**, which temporarily restores `_cfg` / `_progAnchor` / `_playStartAt` from `_orchPrevPhase` while a boundary is pending. This swap DOES work where the phase-store swap did not: the prog clock is pure arithmetic from an anchor, whereas the layer phase stores stop being advanced once the tick returns. Everything else on that list is engine-side and must keep following `_playIdx` — `_ambTick`, `_ambSyncMods`, `_ambApplyReverb`, `_ambScheduleTg`, `_ambUnitGateAudio`, `_ambLiveApplyOK`, `_ambProgStepAt`/`_ambProgStepPos`. **The layer bars need a second piece:** their display gate was `_ambLiveApplyOK(E)`, which is correct for *pushing edits into the engine* (it must follow `_playIdx`) but wrong for *drawing* — the display gate is now `_ambViewIsPlaying(E)` while playing.
