And because the advance re-anchors every layer at the FUTURE boundary while the outgoing area's clocks stop being advanced (the tick returns right after the advance), BOTH halves fail to resolve in the gap — so there's a **pre-boundary sweep** at the one common exit of `_ambUpdatePlayheadsCore` that derives the phase from `_orchPrevAnchor` instead (exact for synced layers, close enough for free ones over half a second). Verified: the bar sweeps 0.47 → 0.96 across the gap and flips at +29 ms. **The Pattern step playhead (`_ambEuclidStepPlayheads`) needs the SAME treatment and is a separate code path** — it reads `E.runPhase[key].startAt`, which the advance re-anchors to the future boundary, so `now >= startAt` goes false and the step highlight VANISHES partway through a bar that is still sounding: at 120 bpm the ~0.5 s lead is ~2.3 of 8 steps, reported as "highlighting stops at 6 instead of 8 / it disappears". It now falls back to `_orchPrevAnchor` while a boundary is pending (exact for a unit-synced layer). Measured A/B: without it the sweep died at step 5 and stayed blank for the last 530 ms; with it, 5 → 6 → 7 held through the boundary. **TRAP that cost a pass:** that exit block sits outside the branches where `layer` is declared, so `_ambLayerByKey` must be re-called there — the bare `layer` reference threw a ReferenceError straight into the surrounding `try`, and the whole sweep silently never ran. Two things NOT to do: don't swap the old phase stores in for display (they stop being advanced, so `next > now` fails and the bars blank anyway), and don't change `_ambLiveApplyOK` itself.
- **PER-LAYER NOTE PICK (`layer.chordPick`) replaced the Drone "Edit notes" chord editor — that editor used to WRITE THE AREA'S HARMONY.** Under an active progression `_ambNotesOf` hands every layer the SAME chords array by reference, so the old editor's re-spell/slot-swap changed the chords for every layer at once (it said so in the popover, which is not a fix — it's a confession). Now it records, per chord, which notes THIS layer voices: `{ mode:'chord'|'jazzy'|'key', sel:{ '<chordIdx>':[iv,…] } }` where `iv` is semitones above THAT chord's root (so a pick survives transposition). Three candidate sets via `_ambPickCandidates`: **chord** = the chord's own tones · **jazzy** = + tensions (9 · 11 · ♯11 · 13 · ♭7 · maj7) · **key** = every tone of the area key (verified: over C in C-major, jazzy offers F♯/A♯ and key does not). Absent = the automatic voicing, byte-identical. Consumed by a short-circuit at the top of `_ambVoiceProgChord` that skips the variant/degree machinery entirely (the user has stated the notes; re-voicing on top would fight them) while Register/Spread/Density still place and trim. **TWO traps, both of which made it store fine and sound not at all / throw:** (1) the drone's prog path hands the voicer a `_shim`, NOT the layer — `chordPick` must be copied onto it, exactly like `degree` before it; anything the voicer reads that the layer owns has to ride along. (2) The editor's `chordsNow()` goes through `getCfg()`, which NORMALIZES — and an all-auto pick prunes to absent — so a bare `{mode, sel:{}}` was deleted mid-handler and the next `L.chordPick.sel` threw. The mode therefore lives in a transient `_pickMode` until there is a real pick to attach it to, and any handler that mutates picks must read the chord list BEFORE touching state.
- **SIDE-SWIPE between areas** — a horizontal drag on `.ambient-areas` steps the view one area (wraps both ways); `touch-action: pan-y` keeps vertical scrolling. Works stopped (also moves what plays next) and playing (view-only — `_playIdx` verified unchanged). It ARMS on buttons and selects deliberately: the strip is almost entirely chrome, so bailing on them meant the gesture could only start in the few bare pixels between controls and never fired in practice; safe because nothing calls preventDefault and it only acts past 42 px of horizontal travel with a 1.4× X:Y slope. **TESTING NOTE:** `page.mouse` is the wrong tool here — the strip lays out below the fold (`#mix-view` is `display:none` until the Mix view is shown, and even then the strip sat at y≈893 in a 820-tall viewport) and Chrome coalesces the drag into one move. Dispatch synthetic `PointerEvent`s with `pointerType:'touch'` at the element's own rect instead; that exercises the real handler.
- **CYCLE SCHEDULE (`layer.cycleGate`) — the Pattern control's 2nd tab, on every `euclidgrid` type (Bass / euclid Beat incl. drum-lanes / euclid Arp).** Where the Pattern grid sequences STEPS WITHIN one iteration, this sequences the ITERATIONS: each cell is one full pass of the pattern, and an off cell leaves that whole pass silent — the `When` control drawn as an arbitrary pattern. It COMPOSES with When (both gate the same cycle index `c`), and is distinct from `unitGate` (the ⏱ Scheduler's Unit Schedule), which slices the INSIDE of a unit. Shape `{len, steps:[0|1…]}`; absent = every iteration plays, which is also how an all-on gate is stored (the setters prune it), so absence is the one neutral state. Kept OUT of `_ambDefaultLayer` (object-in-defaults backfill trap), coerced by `_ambNormalizeCycleGate` called from `_ambNormalizeEuclidPattern`. **The whole feature is TWO emit lines** — `if (!_ambCycleGateOK(inst, c)) continue;` next to the existing `_ambCondFires` in `_ambEmitEuclidCore` (covers bass + euclid beat + the shared step-grid path) and in `_ambEmitArpEuclid`. **THE TRAP that cost a render pass: `.ambient-euclid-grid` has TWO innerHTML writers** — `_ambWireEuclidGrid`'s `render()` AND `_ambRefreshEuclidGrids` (the timing-edit / Lock-to re-fit sweep, ~11861). Adding the tab strip to only the first made a Unit change silently replace the tabbed control with a bare step grid; both must emit `_ambGridTabsHtml` and honour `L._gridTab`. The tab-strip/schedule chrome needs its OWN delegated listener on `grid` — the existing one is the drum-lanes handler and returns early for non-kit layers. `_gridTab` is transient (a view preference, never a schema field).
- **DRUM SOLO (`layer.soloLane`) — a real PERSISTED layer setting, third design after two failed clever ones.** The `S` beside the step inspector's ▶ audition mutes every other lane of a drum-lanes kit; one `continue` at the top of the kit lane loop in `_ambEmitEuclid` (via `_ambSoloLane`), absent-by-default = byte-identical. History that matters: v1 stored a transient `_soloLane` on the layer — but persistWorkspace JSON keeps underscore fields, so it could be SAVED and reload with 7 lanes silently muted; v2 moved it to a Map keyed by the live layer OBJECT, cleared on stop — which silently died on stop/copy while the button kept its rendered state ("toggle visual out of sync", "doesn't survive the area coming back"). v3 is the boring design: `soloLane` is a plain persisted field like any control, coerced in `_ambNormalizeEuclidPattern` (kit-only, clamped; migrates any legacy `_soloLane` INTO it so stuck-muted saves become visible), it survives stop/play/reload/area cycling, and the UI is LOUD so a stored solo can never be invisible — the soloed lane's label carries a hard `S` badge, every muted lane label dims, and the S button has THREE states (this lane soloed / `other` = a different lane holds the solo / off). LESSON: monitoring state that can silently die while its widget keeps state WILL be reported as a bug; persist it and make it visible instead.
- **The ⏱ Scheduler draws its STRUCTURE from `E.getCfg()` (the VIEWED area) but its playhead/highlight from `E._cfg` (the PLAYING area) + `E.freeze[key]` engine state — two different areas once Area sequence play moves on and the panel stays parked.** Indexing one into the other lights the wrong row: measured with a Loop SEQUENCE on a beat layer, the viewed area's 2 tuple chips were highlighted from the playing area's 1-tuple write cycle ("the seq chip display gets out of sync with playback"). `_ambViewIsPlaying(E)` is the shared check; BOTH `_ambSchedCursor` and `_ambEuclidStepPlayheads` (the Pattern control's page tabs / step column / up-next flag — same bug, reported separately as "wrong Seq chips in Pattern after one play through") take their clear branch when it's false, so an area you aren't hearing simply shows no cursor — the amber `.viewing-other` rim + the `▶ <name>` bar readout already say why. Don't "fix" it by rendering the structure from `E._cfg` instead: the panel is meant to show what you're EDITING.
- **AREA-BOUNDARY VOICE OWNERSHIP: `stopBloomVoicesBefore` fires ~60 ms AFTER the boundary against the LIVE key, by which time the INCOMING area has already built on it — so the two areas' voices were told apart only by `_akAt < boundary`, and any incoming onset landing before the boundary was destroyed.** `_ambDepartLayer` moves the outgoing CHAIN to a `#dep` key, but its already-dispatched VOICES kept the original `_ak`; the incoming area rebuilds on that same key later in the same advance (step 4 depart / step 5 generate). A layer whose **Area-Groove `push` is negative ("ahead of the beat", `_ambDriftOffset` returns a SIGNED offset down to −200 ms) anchors before the boundary EVERY cycle** — measured: the new area's opening bass note, scheduled at −40 ms for 260 ms, was silent by +90 ms (envelope `0ms .39 → 30ms .24 → 60ms .14 → 90ms .002`). Reads as either "the first note of the next area doesn't play" OR "the last note cuts out" depending on which area you think the straddling note belongs to — same defect. Fix: `retagBloomVoicesBefore(key, beforeAt, newKey)` (04) re-tags the departing layer's voices — all five voice collections PLUS the deferred `_voiceBuildQueue`, whose entries carry their own `ak`/`at` copies — onto `depKey` **synchronously at depart time, while `key` still belongs exclusively to the outgoing area**; the timeout then stops `depKey`. The split becomes structural (which area emitted it) instead of a float comparison. Verified: the note now rings its full 260 ms, and the outgoing area is still fully silent within 250 ms with no `#dep` chain leaked. ALSO fixed here: the hard-cut gate ramp floor was **5 ms**, shorter than one cycle of a low bass note (41 Hz ≈ 24 ms) — `_AMB_DEPART_MIN_FADE` is now 20 ms (the click-free ramp the pad `kill()` uses), and the teardown timeout waits out the ACTUAL `fadeSec` rather than the requested `fadeMs`. DIAGNOSTIC that settled it, after RMS-envelope and slew measurements both came back clean: profile the envelope in 30 ms buckets across the boundary and compare against the note's scheduled duration — a note dying early is unmistakable, whereas whole-window RMS just shows the rhythm's own gaps, and sample-slew on a sawtooth is swamped by the waveform's own reset edge (use a sine to measure gate clicks).
- **The area `<select>` shows ▶ on the PLAYING option, but a collapsed select only renders the SELECTED (= viewed) one — so with the view/play decoupling there was no always-visible "what am I hearing?".** `_ambUpdateBarIndicator` now prefixes the bar readout with `▶ <area name>` whenever there's more than one area (mirrored into the sticky header + app-header pill), and toggles `.viewing-other` (amber rim) on the select when the viewed area isn't the playing one — so an edit that seems to do nothing is explained. An auto-follow (moving the selection to the incoming area at each boundary) was tried and REMOVED at the user's request: the panel belongs to the user, and yanking the view mid-edit is worse than the selector reading stale. `_ambSwitchToArea` stays view-only while playing — whatever area you select stays selected. What's sounding is REPORTED, never enacted: the ▶ prefix on the playing option, the `▶ <name> · Bar …` header readout, and the amber `.viewing-other` rim on the select.
- **`_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA` slider/time rows are `[kind, key, LABEL, min, max, hint|step]` — min/max are `c[3]`/`c[4]`, NOT `c[2]`/`c[3]`.** `_ambRandomizeLayerParams` (the per-layer 🎲 dice button) read them one slot early, so it took the LABEL as min (`'Strum' | 0 === 0`) and the real min as max: every `ri(min,max)` collapsed to `ri(0,0)`. The dice therefore zeroed **every** slider and time param on the layer — sustain 0, release 0, `intervalMs` 0 (a silent layer on a zero-length unit) — while looking like it "randomized" something because the card visibly changed. Silent for a long time because nobody re-read the values after pressing it. Fixed 2026-07-27. When you write a new schema walker, confirm the index against a RAW schema line (`['sl','strum','Strum',0,100,'chord → arp']`), not against another walker.
- **CREATE SIBLING (`❖` in the Areas management row) — clone the active area, then apply a ticked plan of mutations; everything unticked carries over verbatim.** `_ambSiblingModal` → `_ambCreateSibling(plan)` → `_ambApplySiblingPlan(cfg, plan)`. Area ops: Transpose · New progression · Mutate progression (`_ambReharmonize`) · New take · Tempo. Per-layer ops: Drop · Tone · Pattern · Octave · Settings. Four things worth knowing: (1) **Order is authored-then-moved** — a new/reharmonized progression is generated FIRST and transpose runs after, so fresh harmony lands in the transposed key (`_ambResolveStandard` resolves against the WORKSPACE root, not the area's). (2) **`_ambTransposeArea` must detach `keyFollow`** — `keyFollow !== false` means the area MIRRORS the workspace root and ignores its own `cfg.keyRoot`, so transposing the field alone is a silent no-op; pin the effective key via `_ambKeyRootPc`/`_ambKeyScaleName` first, then set `keyFollow = false` (the same move `_ambSetActiveArea` makes for followers). It walks key + `prog.chords` + **per-slot `chord.alts`** (miss these and the area snaps back to the old key whenever an alt is picked) + `prog.versions` + every layer's `notes`, arp `steps[].notes`, and `keyOv` (`mode:'key'` root / `mode:'prog'` chords). `wrap` sources are deliberately NOT transposed (shared registry — it would move every area using them) and `sections[i].key` is already a RELATIVE offset. (3) **"Pattern" is euclid-only** (`_ambSibCaps`) — a non-euclidean layer's material comes from the area take, so a per-layer rhythm re-roll there would be a lie; the modal says so in the disabled button's title and points at New take. (4) **`_AMB_SIB_NUDGE_SKIP`**: the "Settings" nudge (±25% of each control's range) must skip `pulses`/`steps`/`rotate`/`bars` — they're independent `sl` rows with their own ranges (rotate maxes at 31 while steps can be 8), so nudging them both undoes a Pattern roll ticked on the same layer and can push pulses/rotate past steps. All randomness is `Math.random` at UI time, never `_ambRand`; only `cfg.seed` (New take) touches generation determinism — golden/harness/mod-parity verified green.
- **Ramp targets AUTO-DERIVE from `_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA` — numeric `['sl'|'tm', key, label, min, max]` tokens become rampable automatically; everything else needs a manual list entry.** The per-layer Ramp target list is `_AMB_RAMP_PARAMS` (~16260) = hand-tuned entries + `_AMB_STEREO_RAMP` + `_AMB_FX_RAMP` (nested FX like `chorus.mix` ride the generic live-push branch in `_ambRampResolve`), then a derive pass sweeps every schema type's sl/tm tokens (hand entries win on key conflicts). So: adding a slider/time control to the schema = automatically rampable, nothing to do. But when you add/remove a param that is NOT an sl/tm schema token — selects (`speed`/Rate is deliberately NOT rampable: `_ambApplyRamps` rounds to integers, which would destroy fractional rates), toggles, seq/samp hand-built card params, or new nested FX fields — update `_AMB_RAMP_PARAMS`/`_AMB_FX_RAMP` by hand, and give node-pushed params a live-apply case in `_ambRampResolve` (cutoff/reso → `_ambApplyLayerFilter`; level/space/BPM have their own; plain cfg numerics need none — generators read them live).
- **Primary Bloom layers (cfg.bed/motif/texture/beat) RENDER from `_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA` but WIRE from a separate hardcoded `bind()` list — a new schema token appears on the default layer yet does nothing until you add a matching primary bind.** Added-layer cards use the schema-driven `_ambWireInst` (has the `hold`/`strumsync`/`rhythmseed`/`sl` etc. cases), so a new control works on EXTRA layers but is inert on the PRIMARY one. The primary wiring is explicit `bind('ambient-<type>-<field>', type, field)` calls (~17390+) plus `set('ambient-<type>-<field>', …)` for the initial value (~16615+); SELECT tokens (Hold/Strum sync/Rhythm/Pitch) need their own explicit `G('ambient-<type>-<id>').addEventListener('change', …)` block there too (mirror the `rate` select). Symptom: "the new control does nothing on the default bed" while it works on an added bed. Diagnose by dispatching input/change on `mix-bloom-<type>-<field>` and reading `_masterEng.getCfg().<type>.<field>` — UNWRITTEN = missing primary bind. Until primary wiring is unified with the schema, every new primary token needs: render token + primary `bind`/select block + sync `set`.
- **JS-side mod WAVEFORM math lives in ONE shared evaluator: `_modWaveEval(shape, phase, opts)` (03-audio-bus-fx.js, the B2 shape core).** All sampled shape consumers call it — 17's `_ambScheduleCustomSrc`, 17's `_ambSyncTargetCore` 64-pt core-curve sampler, 20's `_sdModSrcFn` filter-path fn, 20's per-voice scheduled-LFO sampler. NOT unified (by design, per measured engine economics): Bloom's long-lived layer LFOs stay native `Tone.LFO` nodes (`_ambMakeSrc`) and the Rust core keeps its in-DSP LFOs — a layer mod is a held-class source, and the designer itself keeps true LFO nodes for that class. Change a shape's math in `_modWaveEval` and every JS sampler follows; `test/bloom-mod-parity.js` is the gate.
- **Adding a mod LFO "Shape" needs THREE places in sync, or it silently degrades.** `_ambSyncTarget` validates the shape against the `_AMB_MOD_SHAPES` whitelist and falls back to `'sine'` if absent — so a new shape added only to `_ambShapeSel` (the UI) plays as sine. Update all three: (1) `_ambShapeSel` (UI option), (2) `_AMB_MOD_SHAPES` (whitelist), (3) `_ambMakeSrc` (how it's built). Native Tone.LFO types (`sine/triangle/sawtooth/square`) just pass through as `type`; non-native shapes need handling — `rampdown` is a `sawtooth` with min/max swapped (inverts direction); `smooth`/`sharp` are random/sample-&-hold via a stepped `Tone.Signal` (`_ambIsStochastic`); `custom` is a harmonic/partials wave (4 amps in `mod[t].partials`) sampled to a sum-of-sines curve and ramped through a `Tone.Signal` by `_ambScheduleCustomSrc` (reusing the seq-curve stepping path, `kind:'custom'` in `_ambSyncTarget`/`_ambMakeSrc`). The custom partials row + bindings live in the SHARED `_ambModTarget`/`_ambWireModTarget`/`_ambSyncModShapeEl`, so one edit covers all layer types; default partials `[100,0,0,0]` == a sine (so it's harness-neutral and a sane default).
- **Sample/pad voices register as ACTIVE immediately (even when scheduled ahead) — so future-scheduled ones live in the ACTIVE sets, not the pending sets.** This bit the area-transition departure: `cancelBloomFutureVoices(key, fromAt)` only scanned `_pendingVoices`/`_pendingSampleVoices`, so a sample voice scheduled to start AT/AFTER the boundary (in `_activeSampleVoices`, `_akAt >= fromAt`) was neither cancelled (not pending) nor stopped by `stopBloomVoicesBefore(key, beforeAt)` (which only stops `_akAt < beforeAt`). It then started in the NEXT area and piled up across transitions = "samples run over and get louder". Fix: `cancelBloomFutureVoices` now ALSO scans `_activeVoices`/`_activeSampleVoices`/`_activePadVoices` for `_akAt >= fromAt` (not-yet-sounded → safe to cancel, no click). Synth voices mostly defer to `_pendingVoices` so they were already caught; SAMPLES are the ones that leak. Diagnose voice leaks with the `_activeSampleVoices.size` / `_activePadVoices.size` totals.

- **`window._ambEmitKey` is stamped by the CAPTURE-SINK TEE inside playNote, not by the emitters — a playNote stub that fully replaces the function must call `window._ambCaptureSink(freq,params,dur,at)` itself or every captured note reads as unowned.** This bit the seed-phrase preview (`_ambSeedRender`, 17): the harness-style silent render captured 56 notes but the per-layer key filter dropped all of them because the tee never ran. Also: `Tone.now` is an own NON-configurable accessor in this Tone build — `stubNow(Tone)` fails silently and determinism comes from stubbing `Tone.getContext().now` (generation reads through it); don't "fix" the failed first stub.
- **ONE COMPOSING WORKFLOW on a Bloom layer — the transport picks the semantics, the LANE UI is the composing view.** Stopped, the card's ● / 🎤 buttons AUTHOR: they enter the Author-in-Grid session and arm the Perform recorder (`_ambAuthorTakeToggle`, mic armed for 🎤) — the take transcribes into the seed and starts on Play; a second press finalizes. Playing, they RECORD along with the area (the original `_ambPerfRecToggle`/`_ambHumToggle`). The Author-in-Grid dock renders the scratch lane's STRIP into `.ambient-seedgrid-striphost` (renderSequence routes the `_bloomGridEdit.lane` row there; `_placeStepEditRow` brackets the step editor under it), so ⤸ BAR and ✎ PLACE work in the dock and the phrase is visible lane-style while composing (the user prefers the lane UI over the roll). CAVEATS: an active WRAP still owns grid presses (Perform capture yields — same as Make view), so an armed ● take with a wrap active records nothing; the strip host is cleared on `_ambGridEditStop` (renderSequence won't re-append once the scratch lane is gone). The roll views stay ENABLED but are gated by ONE flag (`_AMB_ROLL_ENABLED` in 17, added when an OFF experiment showed the roll toolbar is the ONLY home of the seed preview/🎲/Sim/✓ Keep shopping loop — hiding the roll silently loses that flow. Re-home those affordances before any future roll removal).
- **Seed-phrase preview (`seedroll`)**: with a layer's 🎹 piano open and the engine stopped, the notes line shows a silent offline render of the next Play (all layers tick for RNG fidelity; only the target's notes kept). First roll edit PROMOTES it via `layer.lockState{seedEdit:true}` + `_ambRestoreLocks` into the standard lock machinery; "↺ Original" (`_ambSeedRevert`) drops it. Cache `E.seedPv` invalidates on any card input (debounced re-render). Multi-unit frozen loops page one unit at a time (`cv._unitIdx`/`_ambRollUnits`; draw + hit-test are windowed by `cv._W0`/`cv._P`).
- **A LOCKED layer's notes render as an EDITABLE piano-roll canvas (`lockroll` mode), not chips.** `_ambUpdateNotesLive` switches a `.ambient-notes-live` line to a `<canvas class="ambient-np-roll ambient-np-lockroll">` when `_ambLockedNotes` returns notes (which it does for ANY frozen layer, incl. Bar-Lock `_barLock` — so Bar Lock auto-exposes editing = item "#3B" needs no extra code). `_ambDrawLockedRoll` draws the loop STATIC over its period (`_ambLockMeta` gives list/offKey/durKey/P) with a playhead from `E.freeze[key].anchor`; `_ambWireLockedRoll` hit-tests a pointerdown to a note index → `_ambOpenNoteEditor` (click block = edit) or `_ambAddNoteAt` (click empty = add there). GOTCHA: the editor-dismiss check in `_ambUpdateNotesLive` must NOT require a `.ambient-np-note` chip (there are none in roll mode) — it falls back to validating `_ambNoteEd.index` against `_ambLockedNotes(...).list`, or the popover closes itself one frame after opening. Drawn every frame by `_ambDrawRolls` (canvas created once on mode-change, redrawn in the viz rAF). Pure UI/edit (edits apply via `_ambReemitLockedNext`), so the note-generation harness is unaffected.

- **The "Riff" layer is internally the `run` type (display rename only).** `_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA.run.label` and the Add-layer menu say "Riff", but the config key, `_ambEmitRun`, `E.runPhase`, the period/notesOf maps, and saved projects all still use `'run'`. Don't rename the TYPE — it would orphan saved projects and break the harness. Riff's controls map: # notes = Density×Bars, # bars = Bars, rests = Rests, note-length distribution = **Len var** (`lenVary`, via the gated `_ambVaryLen` → no rng draw at 0, so the default stays harness-identical).

- **The Bloom tick captures `cfg` ONCE at the top; the orch-advance runs MID-tick and swaps the play area — so after firing the advance the tick MUST `return`, or it re-generates the OUTGOING area with the stale `cfg`.** `_ambTick` reads `cfg = _ambPlayCfg(E)` (the play area) near the top, then the orch boundary check (`now >= boundary - lead`) calls `_ambOrchAdvance` which swaps `E._playIdx` to the new area, cancels the outgoing layers' voices, AND generates the new area (its own `_ambTick`). If the original tick then CONTINUES into its layer-generation section, it emits the OLD area's layers again using the captured (stale) `cfg` — those voices are scheduled INTO the new area's time and AFTER the cancel ran, so nothing cancels them → they bleed into the next area (symptom: "bass/pedal play in the next area even though it has no bass/pedal layer"). Fix: `return` immediately after `_ambOrchAdvance` (the advance already generated the new area; `_orchSwitching` is reset at the end of the advance so the next tick proceeds normally). This is why the depart/cancel/stop machinery all looked correct in isolation but layers still overflowed — the re-emission happened downstream of all of it.

- **The PACKAGED desktop shell serves from `app.getAppPath()`, never a hand-built `Resources/app` path — guessing it produces a window that loads a blank page and reports no error at all.** With `asar: true` the staged files end up in `Resources/app.asar`, a single ARCHIVE FILE; `path.join(process.resourcesPath, 'app')` is a directory that does not exist, so the embedded static server 404s every request, `did-fail-load` never fires (a 404 body is a successful load), and the smoke check reports `title: "" , bloomHost: false` with nothing in the log. `app.getAppPath()` is the one thing that knows whether it is a directory (dev) or an archive (packaged), and Electron patches `fs` to read straight through the archive, so the server needs no other special case. Diagnose by listing `Contents/Resources/` — if you see `app.asar` and no `app/`, that's it.
- **`@electron/packager` is PINNED to v18.3.6 because 19+ require Node >= 22.12.** v20 crashes with `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE` out of `dist/common.js` — it uses `import.meta.dirname`, which is Node 20.11+, and this machine runs 18. v18.3.6 declares `engines >= 16.13` and is CommonJS, so `tools/build-desktop.mjs` pulls it in through `createRequire` rather than a named ESM import. Don't "update" it without checking the Node version first.
- **macOS does NOT throttle a backgrounded Electron window — measured, and the flags could not be shown to matter here.** With the window minimised AND the app hidden (⌘H) for 30 s, `document.visibilityState` stayed `visible` and timers/rAF/audio clock all ran at full rate (9.9/s, 60/s, 1.000x) — and a CONTROL build with `backgroundThrottling: true` and no `powerSaveBlocker` measured identically. So on macOS desktop the shell's anti-throttling settings are insurance (other platforms, real occlusion, low-power states), not the thing making background playback work; what actually makes it work is being a standalone app rather than a browser tab. If you need to A/B those flags, note that neither `win.minimize()` nor `app.hide()` is enough to reproduce a throttled state on this platform.

- **IMPROVISE SCHEDULE (`layer.improv`) — a euclid layer alternates N iterations of its WRITTEN pattern with M improvised ones, and the whole feature only works because it also switches Loop/Write OFF.** `{on,pat,imp,rhythmVar,pitchVar,restProb}`, absent = off = byte-identical (kept out of `_ambDefaultLayer` — the object-in-defaults backfill trap — and coerced in `_ambNormalizeImprov`, called from `_ambNormalizeEuclidPattern` beside the cycle gate). `_ambImprovAt(L,c)` is a pure function of the iteration index (no RNG, no state), so every tick that re-emits a cycle agrees. Three emit hooks: the shared `_ambEmitEuclidCore` cycle loop swaps `ctx.rVar`/`ctx.restP` for the improv values (covering every renderer at once, since they all destructure those off ctx); the synth renderer additionally IGNORES the hand-drawn `euclidPattern` override and calls `_ambEuclidVoicePat` with a per-cycle salt (`_ambImprovSalt`) so each improvised iteration gets its own rhythm, plus a `pVarEff` for the walk; the drum-lanes branch relaxes its `kitPat ? 0 : rVar` forcing on improvised cycles only (a kit improvises BY loosening its drawn grid, not by replacing 8 lanes with generated euclidean rows, which is mush). **THE TRAP: every layer defaults to `write:{on:true,bars:2,times:4}`, so without a Write opt-out the layer would capture ONE phrase and replay it — the freeze gate short-circuits the emitter, so the alternation would be completely inaudible and look like the feature doing nothing.** `_ambEuclidDeterministic` therefore returns TRUE for any improvising layer. That makes its name lie (the layer is deliberately NOT byte-identical per cycle), but the gate's real question is "does this loop natively, so Write should stay out of the way" — same call as the drone-under-a-progression case, and returning true also thaws a layer that was already frozen when improvise was switched on. The euclid ARP has its own cycle loop (`_ambEmitArpEuclid`, predates the shared core) and needed the same three swaps by hand; it has no `pitchVar` because an arp walks its chord. Verified by bucketing real emitter output per iteration: control = 15/15 iterations identical to the drawn pattern, improv 2/2 = `W W I I` exactly, every improvised iteration a distinct rhythm.
- **DELAY BPM SYNC (`delay.sync`) is capped at 1/4 because the CORE delay line is one second.** `DELAY_LEN = 48000` in `dsp/src/strip.rs` and `strip_delay` clamps to `(DELAY_LEN-130)/SR`, so anything resolving past ~0.99 s plays at one time on the core path and another on the node path. `_ambDelaySec` is the single resolver both engines call, and it clamps the SYNC path only — the free-ms path is left exactly as it was, because the Time slider goes to 1500 ms and clamping it too would change existing projects (that node-vs-core divergence above 1 s is pre-existing and separate). Divisions are keys into `_AMB_RATES`, which gained dotted entries (`1/4.`, `1/8.`, `1/16.`) — additive, since nothing iterates that table to build a UI, and an unknown key still falls through `_ambRateBeats` to 0 = use the ms value. A synced delay must FOLLOW the tempo, and FX params are only pushed on an edit (`_ambSyncMods`) — the tick does not push them — so `_ambArmTempoResync` self-installs a `change` listener on `#tempo-input` the first time a synced delay resolves. Modeled throughout on `pecho.sync`, which already did all of this for the pitch echo.

- **CORE DSP HYGIENE (2026-07-30 sound-quality pass) — three conventions any new DSP code must keep.** (1) Every RECURSIVE write (biquad state, delay/chorus feedback lines) goes through `flush()` (strip.rs — zeroes below 1e-18): strips process silence 24/7 and WASM has no FTZ, so unflushed feedback decays into x86-microcoded subnormals and burns the render budget the voice watchdog protects. Voice filters in lib.rs are deliberately NOT flushed (live input at oscillator scale, freed at note end — the check would be pure hot-loop cost). (2) High-Q filters route through `df2t_sat` / the design filter's `g_sat` flag (thresholds: strip vcf > 8 dB, design q > 2.5) — saturating the recursion bounds self-oscillation and adds the analog "sing"; below threshold the plain kernel runs, which is why golden needed NO re-baseline for that change. (3) `sat4`/`dc_block` live beside `flush` in strip.rs; the DC blocker runs ONLY on the asymmetric dist flavors (fuzz/fold/crush) — classic and overdrive are odd-symmetric, generate no DC, and their golden-pinned output must stay byte-identical. Sampler reads are 4-pt Hermite (`read_frame`) — at fr==0 it returns y1 exactly, so unpitched playback is bit-identical to the old linear read; only repitched paths differ. SIMD: do NOT add `+simd128` (measured regression — see the note in dsp/build.sh; the hand-SIMD follow-up was closed by PROFILING: the hot loop was square_additive's per-partial sin(), fixed scalar by Chebyshev recurrence — FM 6.1→13.3× realtime — and what remains is serial feedback f32x4 can't take). (4) The MODULATED filters (strip vcf, design voice) are ZDF SVFs (`svf_g`/`svf_lp` in strip.rs; the design filter inlines the same section with per-type outputs — BP is `k·v1`, the 0 dB-peak normalization the old biquad used; raw `v1` peaks at Q and ships a Q-times-louder band). Both swaps measured 0.000 dB against the biquads they replaced — when touching filter topology, THAT is the proof standard (magnitude match, not ear-guessing), and coefficients refresh every chunk (no cutoff cache — that stepping is gone deliberately). Unmodulated filters (EQ3, phaser, dist tone/focus, sample lowpass) stay biquads on purpose.
- **🎲 RANDOM (Pattern control) WRITES A REAL RANDOM ROW INTO THE GRID — it is not a randomly-chosen preset, and it is not a generative knob.** `_ambRandomizePattern(L, type)` fills `euclidPattern` (melodic euclid) or the VIEWED page's `pat` (drum-lanes) with per-cell coin flips, so the roll lands as an ordinary hand-editable override: press again for another, tap cells to fix it, keep it with ⬇ Save. Rolled with **`Math.random` at UI time, NEVER the seeded `_ambRand` stream** — the same split `_ambEuclidStochasticInit` uses — which is why the invariant harness stays green (verified ✓ ALL PASS) and golden stays 82/82. **Density is deliberate, not 50/50:** non-kit uses the layer's own **Pulses/Steps** ratio, so a 5/8 bass rerolls at roughly 5-in-8 and the Pulses knob keeps meaning what it says (Random reads as "same feel, new placement"); drum lanes have NO Pulses knob (`kitVis` hides it), so each lane draws its own density in a 1/8–1/2 band — one flat density across 8 lanes gives eight equally-busy lanes, which is mush rather than a beat. An all-off roll is forced to one hit, because empty reads as a broken button rather than an unlucky roll. **Kit rolls respect the existing edit scope** (`_ambEditScopeCells` — one drum, or All drums, × any step subset), so "roll just the hat" comes free and needs no new concept; verified only lane 0 changes by default and 8/8 with All drums on. Commit path is the cell-toggle's: clear `euclidPreset` (the pattern is Custom now) → `render()` → `persist()` → `_ambReanchorLayer` while playing.
- **THE PROGRESSION EDITOR IS PART-TABBED, AND PARTS ARE STAGED IN THE EDITOR — that staging is the whole fix for "a part can't be extended".** `_ambProgEd` now carries `parts` (a deep-ish copy) and `part` (the active tab, −1 = All) beside `chords`. Before, parts lived only on `cfg.prog` while chord edits sat in the staged copy, so `apply()`'s `_ambRepairParts` — which clamps Σlen and gives the remainder to the LAST part — handed every chord you added to whichever part happened to be last. Now `addchord`/`addtrans`/`rmchord` call `_ambPePartBump` on the part that OWNS `ed.sel` (insertion is positional, after the selection, so growing that part is exactly right), and Save commits chords + parts together. Verified chords 4→6 with Verse 2→4 and Chorus untouched. **Chord indices stay ABSOLUTE in the scoped strip** (the chord-matrix doctrine) — the strip returns `''` for out-of-range chords rather than re-indexing, so every existing `sel:i` setter and the drag-reorder work unchanged. **TRAP:** `_ambPeParts` deliberately treats a SINGLE part as "no chain" (returning null, matching `_ambRepairParts`), so the FIRST split cannot go through the range helpers — they return −1 and you get `parts[-1].len`. `partadd` bootstraps both sides directly. Deleting a boundary merges into the neighbour and never touches a chord; dropping to one part collapses the chain. Its control bar (`.pe-partbar`) is one LABELLED group per setting (Name / Repeats / Salt) with 40px targets — the first pass was a row of 0.7rem pills with terse glyph labels ("✎ Verse", "🧂 own", "✕ Part") that read as decoration rather than as controls. **The salt control is SEGMENTED and each side sends an EXPLICIT value (`partsalt:i:0|1`), not a toggle** — a single toggle button flips you OUT of the state you press, which is exactly wrong for a two-state control that shows which state is active. The tab bar is also where **rename** and **repeats** now live — both already existed but only on the overview strip (`data-pov="partren"`) and the chord-matrix tabs respectively, which is why they read as missing.
- **THE OVERVIEW STRIP TOGGLES BETWEEN NUMERALS-FIRST AND NAMES-FIRST (`Ⅰ Numerals` / `♪ Names` on the strip's own bar).** Default is today's `**I** C`; toggled gives `**Fmaj7** (IVmaj7)` — the sounding chord leading, the numeral parenthesised after. **THE TOGGLE SWAPS ORDER AND EMPHASIS, NEVER THE ELEMENT ROLES**: `.ambient-pov-nm` stays the NAME in both modes, because `_ambProgOverviewPlayheadCore` relabels exactly that span to the sounding chord as the playhead moves (and restores `_writtenNm` on leave) — putting the numeral there would make the glow write a chord name into the numeral slot and, worse, leave it there. Verified while playing: the glowing chip walks `C|(I) → Am7|(vi7) → Fmaj7|(IVmaj7)` and every chip is restored to its written name on stop. The mode lives on the strip ELEMENT (`el._povNames`, like the matrix's `_pmPart`/`_pmEdit`), is folded into `el._sig` or it would never repaint, and is not a config field — nothing about the music changes. Numerals stay part-key aware in both modes (`_ambPartKeyForSlot`), so a chorus in its own key still reads I.

- **"⚄ Generate" IS NOW "✎ Create" — a two-tab popover: ROLL ONE (the old generator, unchanged) and BUILD BY HAND (root panel → character panel → Add).** Roots are all 12 coloured in-key/borrowed via `_ambPeInScale`; characters come from `_AMB_CHAR_GROUPS` in three families (Triads · Sevenths · Extended·jazz, 26 in all) and are coloured PER SELECTED ROOT by `_ambCharInKey` — a character is "in key" when every tone it puts on that root is in the scale (measured: 8/26 in-key on C in C major, 0/26 on C♯). Add appends and CLEARS the panel so the next chord starts blank; built chords drag to reorder; Save prompts with a random name from `_ambRandProgName` (Math.random at UI time — it is a label, not material). **INVERSIONS ARE RE-ROOTED, because there is nowhere else to put them**: chords here are root + PITCH CLASSES (`_ambNormalizeProgMeta` mods every interval into 0-11, so an octave-raised voicing is folded straight back), and a chord carries no voicing slot — so `_ambCharInvert` expresses C/E as root E, [0,3,8]. That IS the bass change, and it sounds right; the builder keeps the spoken name for its own chip, but anything downstream names the chord from its own intervals, so a saved inversion will not read as "G7/B" elsewhere. **TWO DRAG TRAPS, both invisible at desktop width and both hit at 390px:** the reorder threshold was HORIZONTAL distance, but the chips WRAP, so dragging to the row below is an almost purely vertical move that never armed it — use `Math.hypot`; and the drop target was a rect CONTAINMENT test, which finds nothing in the dead space between rows — use nearest-centre. Both worked perfectly at 800px and did nothing on a phone.

- **PARTS MERGE BY DIRECTION, into a NAMED neighbour ("⇦ Merge into Intro" / "Merge into Chorus ⇨") — the old "✕ Remove part" was always a merge and read as destructive.** Parts are contiguous runs, so a merge is purely additive on `len`; the chord array is never touched, which is why no chord can be lost. The TARGET's name/repeats/key/salt survive and the absorbed part's are dropped with it. **A COLLAPSE MUST STAGE `[]`, NOT `null`** — `apply()` falls back to `c.prog.parts` when the editor "never staged parts", and `Array.isArray(null)` is false, so merging a 2-part chain down to one silently brought all the original parts back on Save (caught by asserting on the SAVED cfg, not the editor state — the editor was right the whole time). An empty array still says "staged", and `_ambRepairParts([])` returns undefined → the key is deleted, which is exactly what collapsing a chain means. All three collapse sites (`_ambPePartBump`, `partmerge`, `partdel`) use `[]`.

- **THE PROGRESSION AREA IS COLLAPSIBLE SUBSECTIONS TOO — 🧂 Salt (salt row + the salt/order readout) · ↻ Order · ▤ Overview · ⌗ Chord matrix · ☷ Section matrix — and they REUSE the panel's own `.ambient-grp` markup rather than a second bespoke fold.** The Key row and the Progression on/off + picker + Edit row stay outside; they are the switches everything else depends on. Overview is open by default, the rest closed. **DO NOT ADD A TOGGLE HANDLER FOR THEM** — the panel build already binds EVERY `.ambient-grp-head` in the host (`host.querySelectorAll('.ambient-grp-head')`), which now includes these; adding a second delegated handler made both fire and cancel out, so a click toggled twice and the group never appeared to open (cost a debugging pass — the tell is `classList.contains('open')` reading the SAME value before and after a click that demonstrably reached a live handler). **`_ambProgGrpSync(E)` hides a subsection whose body is entirely hidden**, called at the end of `_ambSyncFxVis` right after the three renderers that flip those bodies — otherwise Progression-off showed five empty accordions. Each row/matrix keeps setting its own `display` exactly as before; the sync only mirrors the result onto the wrapper, so no renderer had to change.
- **REMOVED from the progression editor: `#pe-name` and the `.pe-shifted` banner.** `pe-name` was wired (it drove `cfg.prog.name`) but the name it set is almost never displayed — the seed picker reads `prog.seedName`, not `prog.name` — so it read as inert; `ed.name` still carries the current name through Save unchanged, and Export is where a name is actually chosen. `.pe-shifted` was the banner explaining that under Key/transpose the editor shows the SCORE while everything else shows sounding names ("↳ transposed +4 into A major — sounds as …"). It only ever appeared when a transpose was active. Removing it does not change any behaviour, but the underlying discrepancy it explained is still real: **the editor still edits written chords while the header pill, overview and Scheduler lane all show sounding ones**, so anyone confused by that now has nothing pointing it out.

- **THE PROGRESSION EDITOR IS TWO INDEPENDENT COLLAPSIBLE SUBSECTIONS: "Part settings" (`.pe-partbar`) and "Chords" (`.pe-chords` through `.pe-ops` — strip, header, length, root, quality, nudge, dig-in notes, alternates, ops).** Editor children are exactly `pe-title · pe-parts · pe-sec · pe-sec · pe-save`. The part TABS stay outside because they are how you move around the chain, and the `.pe-save` footer stays outside because folding Save away would hide the only way to commit. **BOTH START COLLAPSED** — the editor opens compact (title · tabs · two headers · Save, 273px against 686px fully open) and you unfold what you came for. `_secOpen` is transient per-section view state where ABSENCE = closed, so it only ever records what the user deliberately opened; it is read on every paint, so folds survive tab switches and edits. **A folded header carries a SUMMARY** ("Verse · 3×", "3 chords") so a fold never hides what state it holds — the drum-solo lesson: monitoring state that can vanish while its widget stays put gets reported as a bug. `.pe-secbody > .pe-partbar` drops its own border/background, since the section card already frames it and nesting both double-boxes. **THERE IS NO "All" TAB** — the strip is always scoped to one part when a chain exists, so `ed.part` clamps to 0 rather than −1 whenever `_ambPeParts` is non-null; a flat progression has no parts and shows everything by definition. **Two REJECTED designs, recorded so they are not re-attempted:** labelled subsections INSIDE the part bar (Identity/Playback/Variation/Structure) — built and removed, "has no value"; and a suggested-split feature (cadence / repeated-half / transition heuristics for where to divide a part) — built and reverted, because "split each tab into subsections" meant subsections of CONTROLS. Splitting stays manual (✂ Split at selection). **PER-PART KEY (`parts[i].key`) survives from that work**: always in the engine (`_ambPartKeyNow`/`_ambPartKeyForSlot`) but previously reachable only from a chip on the overview strip — the same burial that made rename and repeats read as missing. Setting it records the key in force and does NOT transpose the written chords (only `_ambProgAppendPart` transposes, and only when a part is first appended).

- **THE SCHEDULER PAGES BY PART ITERATION — one pass at a time, with tabs.** A chain of 8 part-passes is unreadable however wide the rows are, so the strip shows a single iteration (`All` still gives the whole chain). The window is a BAR RANGE `[WFROM, WFROM+CHAIN)` and **every lane is drawn relative to it while anything asking an ABSOLUTE question adds `WFROM` back** — `_ambProgGroupAt` (which chord group tints this block) and the unit-gate `uIdx`. The layer walk's natural edges are aligned to the ABSOLUTE unit grid then expressed relative, or a window starting mid-unit draws blocks out of phase with the chords above them. **THREE off-by-window bugs, all found by measuring block counts per page:** (1) the single-row path bypassed the window entirely — it appended every slot with no window test, so paging to pass N showed passes 0..N; the windowed segment list is now the ONLY path and `_wrapRows` always returns rows. (2) `CHAIN` is the window LENGTH, so remaining room is `WFROM + CHAIN - cb`; using `CHAIN - cb` clipped every slot at or past `WFROM` to zero and pass 1 onward rendered empty. (3) A windowed layer lane must span the WINDOW, not the layer's own write walk, or the strip stops lining up with the chords. Measured on 2 parts × 4 plays × 3 chords: All = 24 blocks at 4.2% and none labelled; any pass = 3 blocks at 33.3%, and pass 4 correctly shows the SECOND part's chords.

- **THE SCHEDULER HAS A BARS-PER-ROW PICKER, and the chord/part lanes WRAP — one row per chain made a long chain unreadable.** With 2 parts × 4 plays × 3 chords (24 bars) every chord block was 4.2% wide and **zero of the 24 carried a label**; at 8 bars/row they are 12.5% and all 24 are labelled. `CHAIN` is the full played length, `VIEW` is now the ROW WIDTH, and `CHAIN_ROWS = ceil(CHAIN/VIEW)`. **The layer strips already tiled at exactly `VIEW`**, which is what makes this cheap — they simply wrap more times and stay column-aligned under the chords, with no offset arithmetic and no horizontal scroll (banned by UI rule 1). **The playhead needed NO work**: emitting each row as `<span class="ambient-sched-strip" data-srow="i">` inside a row carrying `data-total` is the same contract `_ambSchedCursor` already implements for multi-row layer strips. A block straddling a row boundary is SPLIT (the bar-grid continuation idiom) and only the FIRST segment carries the label and the `cyc` marker — a continuation is the same chord, not a second one. **The layer row cap had to move**: it was a flat 4 rows, so narrower rows would have silently shown less music than before; it now scales with how many rows the chain itself needs.

- **THE SCHEDULER CHORD LANE WALKS THE PLAYED CHAIN, NOT THE WRITTEN CYCLE — it was lying about the harmony whenever a part had repeats.** With `Verse(2 chords) ×2 → Chorus(2)` the engine plays `C F · C F · G Am` (6 bars) while the lane drew `C F G Am` on loop (the 4-bar written cycle), because it walked `ci % chords.length`. `_ambProgChainSlots(cfg)` expands `parts[].plays` into the heard order and `_ambProgChainBars` measures it. **Deliberately SEPARATE from `_ambProgCycleBars`**, which the Write/Evolve phrase snapping also uses — making THAT part-aware would resize every Write loop under a parted progression, which is a sound change and a different decision. The section-bound branch still wins where it applies (it pins one part per section — a third walk again). **The view is now ONE full chain and never a cycle repeated to fill 16 bars** (`_ambSchedViewBars`); the old widening drew the same chords two or three times and read as "why is this part shown twice". **A PART ROW sits above the chords**, one block per pass badged `1/2 · 2/2`, so "Verse twice then Chorus" is legible instead of an undifferentiated run. **Chord labels clip from the END with an ellipsis, and the show/hide threshold accounts for LABEL LENGTH** — a fixed percentage let `C♯maj7` into a block that fits `C`, and the CSS centred it, so it rendered as the unreadable middle `#ma`. Roughly 1.6% of the strip per character on a phone.

- **❄ CAPTURE PASS freezes one iteration of a salted progression into literal chords — a "loop" is just a VERSION with no salt.** Salt is a pure function of (seed, step, segIdx, cycle), so a pass is RESOLVED rather than recorded: `_ambProgCaptureCycle(E, cfg, cyc)` walks the cycle asking the same funnel the engine asks (`_ambProgCurrentChord`), so alts · take-reroll · vary · tension · ↻ order all bake in alongside the colours. **Colour segments become their own chords with FRACTIONAL `bars`**, which the per-chord length model already supports, and adjacent segments that resolve identically are merged — so the captured pass keeps the written cycle's total length exactly (measured: 11 chords totalling 3 bars from a written 3-bar cycle, and a salt-free progression captures as itself). **`prog.versions[i]` now carries its own `salt`**, which is what makes a version a whole progression STATE rather than just notes: a captured pass is stored with NO salt so it replays fixed, while a version saved from a salted progression comes back salted. Absent salt on a version means "no salt" (normalize deletes an all-zero object anyway), so switching to a capture clears it. The live progression is untouched — switching versions is how you choose between "keep surprising me" and "loop that one". **TEST TRAP:** the colour funnel reads salt from `_E._cfg`, NOT from the cfg handed to the capture, so passing a doctored cfg to test "no salt" does nothing — clear salt on the ENGINE's cfg or the capture will colour anyway (this produced a convincing fake failure).

- **SALT COLOURS ARE KEY-FILTERED — `_ambProgColorVocab` used to build candidates from the CHORD'S OWN INTERVALS and never consult the key, so a plain C-major progression offered 10 of 45 colours containing an out-of-key note** (Gmaj7's F♯ where the V wants a dominant 7, Em add9's F♯, Fsus4's B♭, four of Bdim's six). **The filter runs in WRITTEN space, via `_ambSaltColorAllowed(cfg)`** — that is the part that is easy to get wrong: the engine re-roots the whole progression later in `_ambSrcRootPc` so `chords[0]` lands on the key root, so filtering against the key's ABSOLUTE root is wrong the instant a transpose is on. In written space the tonic IS `chords[0].root`, which makes it transpose-proof. The allowed set is the progression's own tones (a written chord can never be out of key with itself) UNIONED with the key scale rooted there; a chromatic or absent key falls back to the pool alone — the same shape, and the same reason, as the take-reroll's filter. **Filtering alone would have made the V chord worse, not better**: the vocabulary only ever offered maj7 (+11) on a major triad, so removing G's F♯ left it with no seventh at all. ♭7 and 9 are now offered on major triads and m7♭5 on diminished, which is why the counts hold up after filtering (G stays at 7 colours, now `G B D F` instead of `G B D F♯`; Bdim keeps `B D F A`). An empty filtered vocab is returned as-is — the caller falls back to the written chord, which is the right answer for a chord with no in-key colour. Audit: 0 of 45 out of key across every diatonic triad.

- **CHORD LAYERS CAN FOLLOW SALT'S COLOUR CHANGES WITHIN A UNIT (`layer.followSalt`) — shared tones ring through untouched, character tones stop and start.** Colour salt sub-divides one chord instance into segments (C · C(no3) · Cmaj7), but a pad samples its chord ONCE at the onset and holds, so those changes were inaudible — measured: a bed under colours-4 played one voicing for a whole 8 s instance while the salt moved three times. **It is SCHEDULING, not surgery**: salt is a pure function of (seed, step, segIdx), so `_ambBedSaltPlan` resolves the entire segment plan at the onset and emits ONE note per tone spanning the contiguous run of segments it belongs to. A shared tone therefore gets a single long note (no retrigger, no envelope restart), a leaver simply ends, an arrival starts at its boundary — no per-voice stop handles, and because they are ordinary scheduled notes the rolling capture picks them up so Write/Evolve replays them. Verified live: `C4 8000ms · G4 8000ms · E4 1600ms (leaves) · E5 +3.2s · B4 +3.2s`. **THE SEGMENT SETS MUST BE IN SOUNDING SPACE, NOT WRITTEN** — this is the third time the same trap has bitten. `_ambProgCurrentChord` returns the WRITTEN chord; the engine re-roots the whole progression LATER inside `_ambSrcRootPc` (key transpose · section key · part key). `_ambBedSaltPlan` renders pitch classes straight to frequencies, so it bypassed that entirely and played the WRITTEN key while every other layer played the sounding one — reported as "salted Keys layer still sounding out of key", and worse the further the key sits from the written tonic (in A major it played `C E G B`, where C and G are foreign). `_ambSaltSegSets` now takes the shift from `_ambSrcRootPc` itself — the same chokepoint every other pitch resolves through — so all three offsets come along for free. Verified across C, A and F major: zero out-of-key notes, and C major (no transpose) is unchanged. **RULE: anything that turns a chord into a FREQUENCY without going through `_ambNoteFreq`/`_ambSrcRootPc` has to apply the shift itself.** **`voiceCap` is a ceiling INCLUDING colour tones**, separate from Density and absent by default — without it the feature silently does nothing at common settings, since Density 3 trims an arriving maj7 straight back off. **ENVELOPES ARE THE WHOLE REASON "Keys" EXISTS**: with the pad defaults (attack 2000 / release 3650) an arriving 7th smears in over two seconds and a departing 3rd rings for another 3.6 s, so the change cannot be heard — a fast attack and short release are what make it legible. `_ambApplyAdsr` overrides the emitter's computed attack with the layer's own, so a fast-attack bed needs no emitter change at all. **"Keys" is a PRESET carrying `label: 'Keys'`, not a new layer TYPE** — `'bed'` is referenced at ~142 sites and docs/bloom-layer-model.md plans types collapsing INTO presets, so an 11th type would be a large change in the wrong direction; `_ambLayerLabel(layer, fallback)` ALREADY existed and is already used by the card head, so a named layer cost one field. **A SECOND TRAP, worse because it broke the whole panel:** the primary-layer SYNC function (`_ambSyncControls`) and the primary-layer WIRING function are different closures with different element accessors — wiring has `G(id)`, sync reaches elements through `document.getElementById(tr(id))`. Using `G` inside the sync threw `ReferenceError: G is not defined`, which aborted `_ambSyncControls` PARTWAY: cards still rendered, so the console was clean and playback was fine, but everything the sync populates after that point silently stopped — reported as "I hear playing but I don't see my layers". When adding a primary control, check which closure you are in; a bare `G` in the sync is not a scope slip you will notice from a syntax check or a smoke test that only counts cards. **TRAP that cost a pass:** the bed emitter's note source is `_bedSrc`, not `notes` — referencing the wrong name threw a ReferenceError that the call site's own try/catch swallowed into a silent null, so the planner looked broken when only the call was.

- **PER-PART SALT (`parts[i].salt`) OVERRIDES `cfg.prog.salt` WHILE THAT PART PLAYS — and an all-zero object is MEANINGFUL, so it must not be pruned.** Absence = inherit; `{len:0,colors:0,scatter:0}` = "no salt in this part", which is the only way to silence salt for a chorus against a salted progression. `_ambPartSaltAt(cfg, step)` resolves the colors axis per chord instance. The LENGTH axis is the subtle one: `_ambProgSaltLensParted` re-slices each part's slice against **its own subtotal**, which preserves the cycle total the entire salt design rests on (verified 2 + 2 = 4 with only part A moving). If NO part carries its own salt it falls through to the exact old whole-cycle call, so existing parted+salted projects are bit-identical — deviating only on opt-in is what keeps this additive. `_ambRepairParts` builds a FRESH object per part, so `salt` is carried explicitly or it vanishes on every normalize. The salt READOUT follows all of this (it earns its place on a part-only salt, resolves lengths/segments per part, and its `_sig` includes the part salts or it never repaints).
- **PART-LEVEL LAYER OPT-IN IS A MACRO, NOT A STORED FIELD — deliberately.** "Layer A plays on Part A, and the third chord of Part C" is expressed by writing the ordinary `chordMask.steps` values across a part's span: zero schema, zero migration, no new gate in the emitter, and an individual chord inside the part can still be overridden afterwards (verified `[100,100,100,0,0,0]` → set chord 3 → `[100,100,100,0,0,100]`). It rides the existing spread grammar as a third full-sentence button ("Set all of *bass 1* in *Chorus* to 40%"), and with a part tab open the ROW HANDLE scopes to that part too, since the tab is already scoping what you can see. **NAME COLLISION TO KNOW:** `chordMask.part` was ALREADY taken and means something else entirely — a sub-chord WINDOW (`{size, place}`, "play only the last 25% of each chord"). A progression part is not that. Anything new here needs a different key.

- **BED CHOKE MUST CLAMP TO `_psiSub`, NOT `_ambLayerPeriodSec` — under prog-sync MERGE mode those are different numbers and choke silenced a chord halfway through its own group.** `_ambLayerPeriodSec` returns `psi.subUnit`, the UNIFORM chord sub-slot; the emitter a few lines above already sizes `durMs` off `_psiSub`, which in merge mode is the whole repeated-chord GROUP. So a merged 4 s group was choked at 2 s and sat silent for the other 2 s (measured: gap 4.0 / note 1.988 → now 3.988). Two notions of "the unit" in one function is the smell; the fix is to use the same one the emitter used. Non-prog paths are untouched and were verified byte-identical by measurement first — my initial hypothesis was that Strike/Hold broke choke, and **it does not**: choke tracks the onset gap to 0.99 at strike 0 / +2 / +4 / −2, because `_ambResolvedUnitSec` already folds the hold in. Measure before "fixing" a suspected math bug here.
- **A FROZEN WRITE LOOP REPLAYS CAPTURED NOTE LENGTHS, so toggling Choke (or any length-shaped control) on a PLAYING bed is ignored until the loop rewrites — Choke was the only control on that card not calling `_ambReanchorLayer`.** Its siblings (tone, pitchrule, every slider) all do. Measured on a default bed (Write on, 2×4): without the re-anchor the first 4 s after the toggle are 6/6 chords still ringing their old 8 s length and it only settles at ~7 s; with it, choke bites within about one lookahead (2/10). **Honest caveat, because the tail got LONGER, not shorter:** the thaw is a Write handoff, so occasional unchoked stragglers appear at boundaries out to ~12 s (they carry a NEGATIVE lead — past-due replay notes fired by `_ambReplayFrozen`'s 0.22 s grace). That is the generic handoff behaviour every re-anchoring control on this card already has; the re-anchor trades a longer sparse tail for a response that is actually perceptible. **`_ambRewriteSoon` is a no-op when the freeze is not armed yet** (`!fs.frozen` → return), so an edit made during the initial capture window still waits out the cycle — a general hole in every `_ambReanchorLayer` caller, not specific to Choke, and NOT fixed here. **MEASUREMENT TRAP that cost a pass:** "first choked note after the toggle" filtered on `n.t > t0` and sorted by `at` reported a bogus 12.3 s latency; the real answer only appeared by dumping (scheduled-at, sounds-at, lead, dur) per onset and reading the transition directly — and that dump is also what exposed the interleaved unchoked notes, which no summary statistic showed.

- **A NEW EUCLID LAYER IS BORN WITH A RANDOM WRITTEN PATTERN — rolled at the UI CREATION SEAMS, never in `_ambDefaultLayer`.** `_ambSeedRandomPattern(L, type)` = the existing `_ambEuclidStochasticInit` (roll Pulses/Rotate into a musical band) followed by `_ambRandomizePattern` (write the actual row), wired at four creation points: `_ambAddExtra` (now gated on `_ambIsEuclidLayer`, so a euclid Beat/Arp gets it too — it was bass-only), `_ambAddPreset`'s non-pinned branch, the arp→euclid toggle, and `_ambSeedDrumLanes`. The knob roll must stay FIRST: it sets the written roll's density AND leaves the generator somewhere sensible for when a later Pulses/Regen edit clears the override. **`_ambDefaultLayer` STAYS DETERMINISTIC** — the invariant harness builds its layers straight from it, so a Math.random in there would drift the baseline on every run; verified `_ambDefaultLayer('bass')` returns no `euclidPattern` and is byte-identical across calls, harness ✓ ALL PASS, golden 82/82. **Two placement traps:** (1) the roll must run AFTER `steps`/`bars`/`euclidVoices` are settled, since it reads them for the row length — in the arp→euclid handler those are assigned after the stochastic init, so the roll moved to the end of that block behind the same `_fresh` flag; (2) **do NOT put randomness in `_ambEuclidPages`** — it is called LAZILY from `_ambEuclidPage`/`_ambPageIdx`/`_ambEuclidViewPat`, i.e. during render AND emit, so a roll there would re-roll the beat at unpredictable times. `_ambSeedDrumLanes` is the safe seam (two UI call sites only) and rolls a bank ONLY when it is brand new (`euclidPages` absent/empty) — an existing bank is left strictly alone, so toggling Drum lanes off and back on can never destroy a grid you drew. **Factory AREA presets are unaffected** (they assign `a.beat` directly with pinned patterns), and a `takeReroll` preset layer nulls the written row a moment later by design, so Battery is unchanged. Measured: 6/6 distinct patterns across 6 adds, two fresh kits differ, and the melodic roll is a clean binomial (n=16 p=0.5 → mean 7.89 / sd 1.92 over 400 rolls, against the theoretical 8.00 / 2.00) — worth knowing because single-hit rolls at 8 steps look like a sparse bias and are not.
- **SAVING A DRUM-LANES RHYTHM USED TO SAVE NOTHING BUT THE KNOBS — `_ambSaveRhythmPreset` stored `L.euclidPattern[0]`, and a kit keeps its pattern in the PAGE store (`euclidPages[i].pat`), not `euclidPattern`.** So ⬇ Save on a kit appeared to work and restored an empty grid. It now also stores `lanes` (all 8 rows of the viewed page) and `bars`; `_ambApplyRhythmPreset` writes `lanes` back into the viewed page when the target is a kit, and falls back to `lanes[0]` when a kit rhythm is loaded onto a MELODIC layer (clearing the pattern there would read as a broken preset). **`bars` matters more than it looks:** without it a saved 4-bar row loaded onto a 1-bar layer PLAYS four bars while the grid draws one, because `_ambEuclidPat` accepts any row whose length is a multiple of `steps`. Both fields are additive and absent on presets saved before this, so old ones keep their exact behaviour (`if (pr.bars)` / `if (lanes && lanes.length)`). Verified round trip: save → wipe all 8 lanes → load restores byte-identical.

- **`bloopsCoreStrips` AND `bloopsCore` ARE PURE SETTERS — NEVER CALL THEM TO READ THE STATE.** `window.bloopsCoreStrips = (on) => { localStorage.setItem('bloopsCoreStrips', on ? '1' : '0'); … }`, so `bloopsCoreStrips()` with no argument writes **'0'** and disables core strips PERSISTENTLY. A "is the core on?" check written that way in the Glitch card ran on every panel sync, for every layer card, so simply opening the Bloom panel silently moved the whole engine onto the node fallback — Glitch (core-only) did nothing, and every other layer quietly left the core path, which is also a plausible source of mobile audio dropouts. **Read `_coreVoices.stripsEnabled()`** (and `_coreVoices.enabled()` for voices). **Both toggles now READ when called with no argument**, so the mistake is no longer possible, and 03b carries a ONE-TIME SELF-REPAIR (`bloopsCoreStripsRepair`) that clears a '0' written by the bad build and then never touches the flag again — a browser fixes itself on next load, and a deliberate later choice survives. Verified across all three states: broken → healed, deliberately-off-after-repair → kept, clean → unchanged. **TEST TRAP:** `evaluateOnNewDocument` re-runs for EVERY new document (iframes included), so seeding localStorage that way re-wrote the flag AFTER the repair and made a working fix look broken — seed via a first navigation, then reload. Symptom pair to recognise: a core-only feature does nothing AND unrelated layers change character at the same time.
- **AN ID-BASED FX SLIDER NEEDS PER-CLUSTER BIND + SYNC IN ALL FOUR CLUSTERS (seq · sample · extras · primary); a CLASS-DELEGATED one needs none.** Glitch's controls are class-delegated for exactly that reason — but its Mix was written with `_ambSl`, which is id-based, so it was never wired: dragging Mix left `glitch.mix` at 0, `wantGlitch` never went true, and the whole effect appeared dead. Mix now keeps its ID (the FX registry's `zero` action finds it that way) AND carries the delegated class. If you add one control to a class-delegated card, check which mechanism it is on.

- **GAIN-LIKE STRIP PARAMS ARE SMOOTHED — Wet only and Dry kill were applied INSTANTLY and clicked.** `main` (Wet only, 1→0) and every FX `wet` (Dry kill forces a 0.35→1) stepped in one sample. `main` now glides PER SAMPLE (~25 ms one-pole in the mainout loop); the FX wets carry `*_wet_c` shadows glided once per block (coef 0.08). **Per-block was not enough on its own for `main`** — a block glide is a staircase whose FIRST step carries most of the change, which still ticks; that is why main is per-sample and the wets (smaller jumps) are per-block. **ENGAGING a stage mid-playback fades in**: the shadow is primed to 0 on the off→on edge, because Dry kill engages an FX at mix 0 and snapping its wet to 1 was the remaining click. **The fade MUST be guarded on `_c >= 0`** — a shadow of −1 means "never primed", i.e. render START, where engaging has to be instant; without that guard every render that engages an FX drifts (golden caught it immediately: 13 sections). Measured WORST-case over 100 toggle phases: Wet only 0.0151, Dry kill 0.084 → 0.0183, against a 220 Hz sine's own 0.0144/sample slew. **MEASURE TOGGLE CLICKS ACROSS MANY PHASES** — a single toggle lands at an arbitrary point in the waveform, so one reading is luck: the same Wet-only toggle measured 0.0151 at one phase and 0.0562 at another. **STILL OPEN: engaging DISTORTION mid-note remains 0.111** (~7.7× slew). Its wet fades correctly, so the residue is the stage itself — the tone/focus biquads and the ADAA/DC state are reset on engage and ring. Not chased yet.
- **`_ambReconfigSharedQuiet` EXISTS AND IS NEVER CALLED.** It implements the documented "mute the layer's own gate ~6 ms, change the graph, unmute" protocol, but nothing invokes it — so `_ambApplyLayerFx`'s NODE path still does a bare `disconnect()` / dispose / rebuild whenever the engaged-FX set or the chain order changes, which is exactly what Dry kill (it flips `wantX`) and the first Wet only (it creates `e.mainOut`, changing `_fxOrderSig`) both do. It cannot simply be called from inside `_ambApplyLayerFx` — it calls that function itself, so it would recurse. Fixing this needs the rebuild block extracted so it can run after a short mute. Core-strip layers take the early return and are unaffected, which is why the core-side smoothing above helps but does not cover a layer on the node fallback.

- **GLITCH is ONE FX slot with FOUR read strategies (grain · repeat · tape-stop · reverse), CORE-ONLY, and it runs AFTER the reorderable five.** One ring buffer read four ways, the way `dist` is one slot with five curves — four separate slots would have been four buffers and four sets of plumbing for the same machinery. It is deliberately NOT in `_AMB_FX_INLINE`: it sits outside the `fx_order` permutation (which stays 0..4, so no existing routing moved) because a destroy-the-signal stage belongs at the end. `fx_glitch` in `dsp/src/strip.rs` is feed-forward — the ring takes the stage INPUT, never its own output — so there is no recursive path needing `flush()`, and its RNG is a per-strip xorshift seeded from the slot index so golden reproduces exactly. Engaging or switching mode clears the grain slots and cursors but KEEPS the ring, so the effect has history to work with immediately instead of a second of silence. Memory is 1 s/slot (48000 × 2ch × 16 = 6 MB), matched to the existing delay line rather than doubling it. **Verified: all four modes sound, none NaN, all differ from dry, and wet=0 is an EXACT bypass (max|diff| 0.00e+0) that still primes the ring.** **EVERY MODE THAT JUMPS ITS READ HEAD NEEDS AN EDGE FADE — the first build shipped without one and CLICKED audibly.** Repeat and Reverse restart their slice by moving the read position, and a jump mid-waveform is a click: measured 21 and 24 clicks per 3 s with peaks of 0.67 and 0.42 against a 220 Hz sine whose own slew is 0.0144/sample. A 4 ms fade (capped at a quarter of the slice) takes both to 0.010, BELOW the signal's own slew. Grain never needed it — its Hann window already lands both ends at zero — and Tape-stop rides its own speed ramp into silence, which is why only two modes were affected. **A GRAIN'S LOOKBACK MUST COVER HOW FAR IT TRAVELS, NOT ITS LENGTH:** a pitched-up grain reads faster than the write head advances, so at ±24 st it overtook the head mid-grain and landed on the oldest samples in the ring — a click exactly where the Hann window is at full gain. Lookback is now `size × max(1, inc)` plus jitter into whatever room is left. **Diagnose this class with max sample-to-sample delta against the source's own slew rate** — a click is an order of magnitude above it and impossible to miss, where an RMS or spectral check shows nothing. **NO NODE FALLBACK EXISTS** — a granulator has no sane Web Audio node build, so with `bloopsCoreStrips(false)` the stage simply is not there; the card shows a "needs the core engine" note rather than failing silently. **The UI is CLASS-DELEGATED (`.ambient-glitch-*`), not id-bound** — that is what keeps it off the FOUR per-cluster bind/sync lists (seq / sample / extras / primary) that every id-based FX slider has to be added to; it follows the pitch-echo pattern, and is the cheaper way to add an FX here. The **Rate knob means a different thing per mode** (grains/s · passes-per-capture · stops/s · unused), so the mapping lives only in `_ambGlitchRate` and the card re-labels itself; Scatter and Pitch are grain-only and are DIMMED rather than hidden, so the card does not change height as you flip modes. `_AMB_GLITCH_MODES` index order MUST match `fx_glitch`'s `mode` in the Rust.

- **THE CORE SATURATION IS ANTIDERIVATIVE-ANTIALIASED (ADAA) ON `classic` AND `overdrive` ONLY — 2x oversampling was built, measured, and REJECTED on the numbers.** A waveshaper generates harmonics above Nyquist that fold back as inharmonic tones; `test/alias-measure.js` renders a sine through each dist mode and reports alias-to-signal in dB (it is a real GATE now — it diffs `test/alias-baseline.json`, exits 1 on a regression, and `--update` re-baselines). Measured gains: classic @5 kHz full drive **−15.2 → −22.5 dB**, overdrive @5 kHz **−16.8 → −23.4**, ten of thirty cases improved by up to 8.5 dB and none regressed. The rejected alternative is the useful half of this note: half-band polyphase 2x oversampling was implemented and swept at 11/15/23/31 taps (shorter filters measured BETTER), and its plateau was **+3.5 dB mean with a worst case of −1.3 dB** — i.e. it made one case noisier — for about **2.5x the CPU**. ADAA beat it on both axes, so the oversampling was reverted rather than kept "because it is the textbook fix". **`fuzz` / `fold` / `crush` are deliberately UNTOUCHED**: fold and crush are not smooth (a fold has slope discontinuities, crush is a staircase), so the first-order antiderivative buys little, and their alias floors are the SOUND — crush measures **+13 dB** alias-to-signal, which is the point of a bitcrusher. **THE COST TRAP:** naive ADAA evaluates the antiderivative twice per sample (at `x` and at `x_prev`) and those are `ln`/`cosh` calls — measured **+45% CPU** on a 4-strip dist-heavy scene (28.4x → 19.4x realtime). Carrying `F(x_prev)` forward in `dist_adaa_f` halves the transcendentals back (→ 24.1x, bit-identical output verified across all 5 modes × 3 drives WITH live parameter churn). The cache is only valid while the curve holds still, so `dist_adaa_dirty` is set whenever `strip_dist` changes `k` or `mode` and cleared at the end of `fx_dist` — **any new writer of a dist curve parameter must set that flag** or the first sample after the change divides by a stale antiderivative and clicks. Golden re-baselined in the same commit: exactly 6 sections drifted (the 5 dist variants + kitchen-sink), every other section bit-identical, which is the blast radius the change should have.

- **The master "Glue" (`globalFx.ottOn`/`ottDepth`) is a 3-band OTT worklet spliced masterCompressor → ott → masterVolume — and `masterOtt` must stay declared ABOVE `rebuildMasterChain`.** The boot-time `rebuildMasterChain()` call runs before the installer block executes; a TDZ read inside its try{} silently skips that rebuild (no error surfaces). The worklet is the lookahead limiter's idiom (inline blob, `Tone.context.createAudioWorkletNode`, graceful absence); rebuilds route through the node when present. Its upward lift FADES TO ZERO below −55 dBFS — without that gate, upward compression pumps the noise floor in the silences generative ambient is full of. Default OFF → existing projects and golden untouched. UI = the FX panel "Glue" group, wired like Warmth's toggle; the depth slider reaches audio via applyGlobalFx → applyMasterDynamics → applyMasterOtt.

- **SECTION LENGTH IS A UNIT RATIO (`sections[i].unit = {num,den,ref}`, schema v7) — sections shipped 2026-07-15, EIGHT DAYS BEFORE the Area Unit, and were the last structural length still authored in raw bars.** Every layer's cycle is `areaUnit × ratio`, so a section length that wasn't a whole number of units put every arrangement boundary mid-cycle — and because `_ambSectionGateOK` is a PER-NOTE gate, that silences whatever was mid-phrase. It was a raw `prompt('Section length in bars:')` accepting any float 0.25–64, with no snapping and no advisory. `ref:'area'` scales with the Area Unit; `ref:'bar'` pins an absolute length that deliberately does NOT rescale. **`bars` SURVIVES as the resolved mirror, rewritten on every normalize** — that is what keeps ~10 existing readers (`_ambSectionAt`, `_ambSectionCycleBars`, the Scheduler lane, the section matrix) completely unchanged, and it can never go stale because normalize runs on every `getCfg`. **The v7 migration converts each existing `bars` into the EXACT ratio of the CURRENT area unit**, so a saved arrangement sounds identical on load and only moves if the Area Unit is changed afterwards (verified: 4 bars @1-bar unit → 4/1, 3 bars @2-bar unit → 3/2, 0.5 @½-bar unit → 1/1; unit 2→4 doubles 4,3 → 8,6). **A length with no clean ratio keeps its raw `bars` and NO unit** rather than being rounded — the first version's `DENS` list stopped at denominator 64 without 5/10/20/40, so a hand-typed 3.7-bar section silently became 4 bars, which is exactly the silent length change the design was supposed to avoid; `_ambRatioOf` returns null and `_ambSectionBars` falls back to `bars`. **TWO UI TRAPS in one submenu, both from this codebase's own documented rules:** `showCtxMenu` is **`(x, y, actions)`** — passing `(actions, x, y)` fails silently; and a menu opened from inside another menu's item `fn` MUST be deferred a tick (`setTimeout(…, 0)`), because the parent runs the fn and then dismisses itself, tearing the new menu down in the same dispatch. Anchor a submenu on the clicked element's own rect, NOT `window.__ambMenuAnchor` (that is stashed by the layer ⋯ menu and is stale or absent elsewhere, dropping the menu at 0,0). Sections are absent in most projects and in every harness config, so harness/golden are byte-identical by construction.
- **A SECTION MEASURED IN ITS BOUND CHANGES CANNOT CUT THEM — `sections[i].unit.ref = 'changes'` (the third ref, beside 'area' and 'bar').** Sections run on the BAR clock and a progression's Changes on the CHORD clock; nothing made them agree, so a section bound to a set of changes (`sections[i].part`) sized its own length independently and the docs recorded the result as accepted behaviour ("a part that doesn't divide the section evenly is cut at the boundary"). With `ref:'changes'` the length is `num/den × _ambSectionChangesBars(cfg, secIdx)` — one pass of the bound changes — so it divides by construction. PROOF is the chord walk, not the bar count: a 4-bar section over 3-bar Verse changes walks `0 1 2 0 | 0 1 2 0` (restarting mid-phrase), and at 1× Verse it walks `0 1 2 0 1 2 0 1` (continuous). **Editing the changes moves the section with them for free** — `bars` is the resolved mirror rewritten on every normalize, so adding a chord to Verse took its section 6 → 8 bars with no invalidation and no listener; that is the payoff of the mirror design. `_ambSectionCutInfo` drives the ⚠ (lane badge + tooltip + Arrangement row) for a section that ISN'T measured that way and doesn't divide evenly, naming both lengths; binding a mis-sized section to changes now offers the fit in the same tap. THREE things to keep in step: `_ambSectionBars`/`_ambSecLenLabel` take a SECTION INDEX now (the changes base is per-section — every caller had to thread it), an unknown `ref` coerces to 'area' in normalize, and a `ref:'changes'` section whose binding is later removed falls back to the Area unit rather than to zero (verified — `_ambSectionPart` already clamps at read time because editing chords can shrink the parts list).
- **THE GROOVE MACROS WERE OVERLAPPING, NOT MERELY CRAMPED — and its sliders never carried `.ambient-sl`, so they missed the whole mobile-slider treatment.** `.ambient-groove-macros` used `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(180px,1fr))`, which fits TWO columns at 376px; each macro then held a 68px label + 26px value + a NOWRAP hint needing ~90px, so the slider was squeezed to **0 px wide** and the hint printed on top of the neighbouring column ("STRAIGHT → SH**Accent**"). Fixed by raising the fit floor to 300px (one column on a phone) and giving the hint its own row (`flex: 1 0 calc(100% - 68px)` — a plain `100%` basis PLUS the 68px indent overflowed by exactly the indent, 376+68=444). The sliders carried `ambient-groove-mac`/`ambient-groove-pushsl` but NOT `ambient-sl`, which is the class the delegated touch-drag handler and the 26-30px thumb bind to — so on a phone they were raw native ranges where a tap on the track JUMPS the value. Adding the class fixed both (measured 16px → 34px tall, 0/8 undersized, touch pointerdown now claimed by the handler while mouse stays native). The groove's segmented pills were 19-24px tall and are floored at 30×44. **When adding ANY new slider, give it `.ambient-sl`** — without it there is no touch handling at all, and the failure is invisible on desktop.
