- **VARIANCE vs PERFORMANCE: the take ID now replays EVERYTHING except Humanize.** The Variance group mixed two guarantees with nothing marking them apart — five knobs that replay identically from the take (Rhythm var / Pitch vary / Len var / Rests / Ghosts / Accent / Rate var) and two that did not. **Vel var was the mislabelled one**: `_ambVelJitter01` seeds through `_ambSeededRand` (it was never `Math.random`, contrary to an earlier note) but keyed on the ABSOLUTE audio-clock time, which differs every press. It is now keyed on time ELAPSED FROM `_barGridAnchor`, i.e. position-in-the-performance, so the same take replays the same jitter — verified by capturing two separate plays of one take and aligning them: matching run 11 of 11 at offset 1 (the offset is only because one capture catches an extra leading onset; do NOT compare two captures index-for-index without aligning them, or a working fix reads as broken). **Humanize stays unseeded on purpose** — performance jitter, zero RNG draws, harness-safe. Both moved to their own `Performance` group, applied as ONE pass over `_AMB_LAYER_SCHEMA` after it is defined (`_ambSplitPerformanceGroup`) rather than edited into every layer type, so a type added later is grouped correctly with no one remembering; the pass is idempotent-safe (it only moves `sl`/`tm` tokens it finds, so re-running cannot create a second group).
- **SALT IS SCOPED PER LAYER — narrowest first: the LAYER's own → the CHANGES it is playing → the area.** `layer.salt = {colors,scatter}` — NO `len`; ABSENT = inherit, present-and-all-zero = this layer never salts (the same absent/zero grammar the Changes use). Two identical pads over ONE chord now play different harmony: measured `E G` (colors 0) vs `C D E F G A B` (colors 100). **The layer identity is stamped in `_ambCapSink`** — every per-layer emit scope installs a capture sink before generating (nine call sites), so one line there reaches all of them instead of threading a layer argument through the chord resolver's many callers; `_ambEmitLayerKey` is read ONLY during generation, so a stale value between emits is harmless. `_ambNormalizeLayerSalt` coerces it when present and it is kept OUT of `_ambDefaultLayer` (an object in the defaults is clobbered by the numeric backfill — the documented trap), so the harness is byte-identical. UI: a `['salt']` schema token beside `['keyov']` on all 9 layer types — an Inherit/This-layer select plus Colour and Scatter sliders that enable with the mode. **STRUCTURAL LIMIT, not a choice: the LEN axis cannot be per-layer.** Length salt is applied in `_ambProgStepAt`, the function that decides WHICH chord is sounding at a given moment — the shared harmonic clock — so a per-layer version would put the bass on chord 2 while the pads are on chord 3. So LENGTH stays an AREA axis and a layer carries no such field at all — a stored-but-ignored field reads as wired, so `_ambNormalizeLayerSalt` drops it (verified: a stray `len:100` set on a layer does not survive normalize). The area Length control says so in its tooltip. The area len path (`_ambProgSaltCfg`/`_ambProgSaltAnyLen` in `_ambProgStepAt`) never consults a layer, so per-layer scoping cannot affect it.
- **A RANDOM BEAT SYNCED TO A UNIT PLAYED EXACTLY ONE NOTE, ON THE 1 — and the cause was the UNIT-SYNC HEAL, not the default.** Reported as "the Beat default of 1 note per bar is useless". Measured: as-added (Free) 3/2/2 notes per bar, unit-synced to 1 bar **1/1/1 all at offset 0**, euclid on the same setting 4/3/3. `runLayer`'s heal snaps each onset onto the layer's PERIOD grid (`_ambLayerPeriodSec`), which is right for every stepped layer because one iteration = one unit — EXCEPT a random beat, whose unit is a PATTERN of `steps` hits, so snapping collapsed the pattern to a single note per unit. It now snaps a random beat to `P / steps` — still grid-locked, at the layer's own resolution — giving 5/4/6 per bar at the defaults. Texture escapes the identical trap only by being WINDOWED (`windowLayer`/`_ambEmitStepGrid`) rather than stepped, which is why it was never seen. `_ambNaturalUnitSec` also gained the beat's pattern length (mirroring texture's `stepSec × 16`) so the unit readout stops describing one hit as the unit. Free beats and euclid beats are untouched; the harness has no synced beat, so it is byte-identical. DELIBERATE SOUND CHANGE: any existing project with a synced random Beat goes from 1 hit a bar to ~5.
- **PROGRESSION TEMPLATES CONFORM TO THE KEY — degrees come from the template, QUALITIES come from the key you are in.** `PROGRESSIONS` (13-prog-pad.js) is keyed by scale name (major / minor / dorian / phrygian / lydian / mixolydian) and `_ambResolveStandard(family, steps)` passed **the family name as the scale** and `rootIdx` as the root, so the area key's SCALE was never consulted: picking "I — IV — V" in an A minor area played **A / D / E major** — the parallel major, silently, with every readout still saying A minor. The family WAS the mode, which is why offering "a major progression" and "a minor progression" as separate things made no sense. Now the degree → semitone lookup runs against the AREA scale, and the quality is the triad that scale actually builds on that degree (`_ambDiatonicQuality` stacks thirds WITHIN the scale — major gives maj/min/min/maj/maj/min/dim, and phrygian correctly yields a diminished v). A step whose stored quality is NOT diatonic in its OWN family is a deliberate borrowing (harmonic-minor V, Picardy third) and is kept verbatim — that is the only thing the stored quality is still for. Verified: 1–4–5 gives `C F G` in C major, `Am Dm Em` in A minor, `Dm G Am` in D dorian, `Em Am Bdim` in E phrygian; `i — V — i` keeps `Am E Am`; the Andalusian keeps `Am G F E`. **GUARD THAT COST A ROUND: only conform to a scale with a diatonic answer — seven notes that stack into recognisable triads.** The area key FOLLOWS the workspace scale by default, which is `chromatic`, and conforming to a 12-note scale maps degree 4 to 3 semitones — I—IV—V rendered as `C D♯ E`. Chromatic/pentatonic areas keep the family scale and stored qualities, i.e. the old behaviour. **`_ambProgCatalogForKey(cfg)` is now the ONE source for every picker** (three menus + the Create-Sibling roll): it resolves each template for the current key, DEDUPES by the chords produced (major and minor families collapse into one entry — they were always the same degrees), labels each with the numerals for THIS key, and groups by the only distinction that survives — In key / Borrowed / Vapor. 122 raw templates → 60 in-key + 16 borrowed + 2 vapor in A minor. `_ambProgFamilies` is now internal to that builder; do not add new call sites. **TEST TRAP: `_ambKeyCfg()` reads the module-global `_E`, which a probe must set — `_ambSyncControls(E)` does, `_ambRenderScheduler(E)` does NOT**, so a headless probe that skips it silently measures the CHROMATIC key and every result looks unchanged.
- **`_ambProgWorksInKey` judges the WHOLE progression, not each chord.** Per-chord with a one-foreign-note tolerance let `Cm`, `Fm` and `Gm` each pass in C major ("only one note off") while together they spell C minor — so the filter that was supposed to keep out-of-key progressions off the menu passed everything, which is half of why the major/minor distinction looked meaningless. It now tests the UNION of the progression's pitch classes AND requires **at least one chord to be diatonic**: the extended pool is the union of the parallel major and minor (modal interchange), so a progression borrowing a chord or two rightly passes, but one borrowing EVERY chord is not borrowing — it is the parallel key. Verified: `Cm Fm Gm` rejected in C major, `C F G` accepted, and `C F G` (re-rooted to `A D E`) rejected in A minor.
- **VOCABULARY (2026-08-06): a progression's "Part" is "CHANGES"; the mask sub-window is "WINDOW"; the area anchor is always "AREA UNIT".** "Part" meant three things — a named run of chords, the `{size,place}` sub-window on the chord/section masks, and (to a musician) an instrumental line, which here is a Layer — and it collided with "Section", since a verse is both. The tooltip Parts shipped with said it outright: *"Parts split a progression into named sections"*. Renames are LABEL-ONLY per the standing rule (divergent behaviour ⇒ divergent label, keys stay for save-compat): `prog.parts` / `chordMask.part` / `sectionMask.part` are all untouched in the data, verified by counting every key before and after (56/100/94/2/2, identical). **NOT renamed, deliberately: `layer.unit` → "Cycle".** It was on the list, but "unit" appears 1508 times and "cycle" is ALREADY overloaded five ways in this file (progression / write / section / variation / "one cycle") — it would trade a mild two-way ambiguity for a six-way one at large churn. Qualifying the area one ("◆ Area unit", one string) fixes the same confusion, so a bare "Unit" now always means the layer's own cycle length.
- **THE SCHEDULER'S PASS WINDOW CAN NEVER BE NARROWER THAN ONE SECTION CYCLE — sections run on the BAR clock across the whole area and are not part-scoped.** Reported as "I create a section, it shows one section A; if I delete A, a section B the same size remains". The ＋ affordance seeds A/B (4+4 bars) by design, but one-part-at-a-time shrank the view to a single part PASS (2 bars), so section B — living at bars 4–8 — fell outside the window and was invisible; deleting A slid B to bar 0 and it appeared, reading as a phantom. `_ambSchedViewBars` ALREADY widened the un-windowed view for exactly this reason (`if (secCyc > 0) v = Math.max(v, secCyc)`); the pass window bypassed it. `CHAIN` now floors at the section cycle, so the window still STARTS at the selected pass but always spans at least one full cycle of sections. Consequence, and it is the right trade: with sections defined the chord lane shows more than one part pass (the chain loops on past it, which is truthfully what sounds over those bars). Diagnose "an object I created is invisible" by comparing the CONFIG against the rendered lane — `cfg.sections = A(4b) B(4b)` vs `lane blocks = ["A"]` located it immediately, where staring at the delete path would not have.
- **The AREA UNIT select lives in the AREAS panel (beside Plays / Bar Lock / Bars), not the ⏱ Scheduler.** It is a set-once area-level setting, and in the Scheduler it cost two rows for nothing: a green `.ambient-sched-unitcue` chip that only repeated its own select's value, and `<span class="ambient-sched-strip"></span>` — a strip that was ALWAYS empty. Its handler moved from the `scBody` `input` delegation to `_ambAreaStripHtml`'s wiring beside `playsI`/`barsI`. One deliberate behaviour change: the old handler re-anchored `ref:'area'` layers whenever `E.timer`, even while you viewed a DIFFERENT area; it now gates on `_viewIsPlaying()`, per the `_ambLiveApplyOK` doctrine that editing a non-playing area must not disturb the running engine.
- **The Scheduler shows ONE PART, and by default ONE PASS of it — there is no "All parts" view, deliberately.** Drawn at once a chain is too dense to label (24 chords across the panel = a few pixels each and no names), so "All parts" showed everything and communicated nothing — it read as a single undifferentiated part. `_partIdx` defaults to 0 and `_schedRep` to 0, and CHOOSING A PART RESETS TO ITS FIRST PASS (carrying "2 of 3" across is meaningless, and landing on "all passes" would rebuild the same dense view). "All passes" of the selected part remains an option on the pass tier. The whole-arrangement view lives behind **▤ Part schedule** (`_ambPartScheduleModal`), a popover with the width to show full part names, pass counts, bar ranges and each part's chords — the inline PARTS lane was removed because at panel width four passes of "Neon Nocturne" gave each block ~47px and every one rendered as an ellipsis. `_ambProgPartRuns(E, cfg)` is the shared bird's-eye helper (one entry per part RUN, chords listed for the first pass only).

## Layer-schema evolution rules (Bloom)

Bloom is moving toward a **composable-layers** model (a layer = Voice × Note-source × Generator × Timing × Variation × Mix/FX; today's ~11 types become presets). **`docs/bloom-layer-model.md` is the AUTHORITATIVE spec** (6-axis spine: INSTRUMENT · KEY · SEED · TIMING · VARIANCE · FX/MIX) and its **§11 is the BACKLOG** — open items with their analysis, what was ruled out and how, so they can be picked up without re-deriving. `docs/bloom-composable-layers.md` is the older design + phased refactor plan; the two overlap and the layer-model doc wins on conflicts. Until those phases land, the invariants below govern any change to a Bloom layer's saved shape:

1. **`_normalizeAmbientCfg` is the ONE migration chokepoint.** Every load path (Drive, localStorage, `_cloneLane`, undo snapshots, Send-to-Bloom, Shape-It, area switch, play) funnels through it. Put migrations there, nowhere else.
2. **Each area cfg carries `schemaVersion`** (`_AMB_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `17-ambient.js`; legacy/pre-versioned = 0). A migration reads `_fromVer` at the top of normalize and gates on `_fromVer < N`; the stamp before `return cfg` records the current version so the migration runs **once** and re-normalizing is a no-op. Bump the constant + add a `//   vN — …` comment line ONLY when a layer's meaning/shape changes and needs a one-time migration.
3. **Additive-only; never repurpose a field.** Add new fields with defaults; don't change what an existing field means. An old build reading a new project must degrade safely (unknown fields normalized away).
4. **Backwards compatibility is an invariant, not a migration project.** A legacy layer with only `type` must keep producing byte-identical output. When Phase 2 adds `generator`/`voice`, they are *derived* from `type` on load and dispatch falls back to `type` — same core, same params.
5. **The invariant harness (`23-bloom-harness.js`) is the gate.** It hashes `[at,freq,durMs,voice,pan,level]` per note over seeded RNG/fixed ticks. Any schema change must keep it green; an intentional output change is a deliberate, reviewed baseline bump — never silent drift. Adding/removing an `_ambRand()` call on a covered path shifts every downstream draw, so keep RNG draws identical on default paths.

## Deployment policy

**NEVER run `./deploy.sh` unless the user explicitly asks for it in that message** (e.g. "deploy", "push it live", "ship it"). Committing and pushing to git is fine when the user asks; deploying to the live GoDaddy site is a separate, explicit step. Do not deploy as an automatic follow-up to a code change, a commit, or a push. When work is ready, say so and let the user request the deploy.

## The WASM audio engine (bloops-dsp)

Bloom voices, layer strips/FX, and sample playback render in a Rust→WASM core (`dsp/`, built by `dsp/build.sh` → `js/bloops/core/bloops-dsp.wasm`) inside ONE AudioWorklet (`js/bloops/core/voice-processor.js`, bridged by `js/bloops/03b-core-voices.js`). **Default ON** — `window.bloopsCore(false)` / `window.bloopsCoreStrips(false)` are the kill switches (persisted localStorage `'0'`); the Tone node engine remains the automatic fallback (cold start, ineligible notes, slot exhaustion, pads/held notes, offline export). Rules:
- **Any DSP change must keep `node test/golden-render.js` green** (82 bit-exact sections); an intentional audio change re-baselines with `--update` IN THE SAME COMMIT. `dsp/build.sh` runs the gate after every build.
- **Calibrate against RECORDED node output, never derive from Tone internals** (proven wrong repeatedly — see the engine memory file). Known engine truths: native lowpass/highpass biquad Q is in dB; `Tone.LFO/Signal.connect` ZEROES the destination param; cycle-member DelayNodes keep true delay with the quantum penalty on the feedback edge; `Tone.Panner` is channelCount 1 (mono-downmix — why the core strip's width-preserving pan law exists).
- The worklet must always stay pulled (keep-pull sink on output 16) and `init()` must reset ALL core globals (golden determinism).

## Commands

```bash
npm start        # Start Express server at http://localhost:3001
npm run samples  # Import a folder of audio into the shipped sample library (tools/import-samples.mjs)
./deploy.sh      # Deploy to GoDaddy cPanel via SFTP (requires lftp) — ONLY on explicit user request (see Deployment policy)
node test/bloom-mod-parity.js   # B2 mod-source parity gate (needs npm start running; --update re-baselines deliberately)
```

**Mod-parity gate:** it RE-MEASURES a drifted config once before failing. The harness samples a LIVE audio graph for 3.2 s per config, so a GC pause or a busy host can smear one past tolerance with nothing changed — the long-standing "1/9 configs drifted, passes on re-run" flake. A genuine regression drifts on every attempt, so one retry separates them; tolerances are UNTOUCHED and a config that drifts twice still fails (verified: poisoning a baseline value gives `⟳ re-measuring …` then `drifted (twice — not jitter)` and exit 1). Retries are logged per config and summarised, so a config quietly becoming marginal stays visible instead of being silently rescued every run — if you see the same id rescued repeatedly, treat that as a real signal, not noise. **RUN IT ALONE.** The retry re-measures IMMEDIATELY, so it can't rescue a config when the contention is still there — launching parity in the same shell invocation as `golden-render.js` (or beside a puppeteer run) produced a `drifted (twice — not jitter)` failure that passed four times in a row on its own, with only display strings and CSS changed. "Drifted twice" means "not a momentary GC pause", NOT "not environmental". Any change to Bloom's node-path mod sources (`_ambMakeSrc`, `_ambSyncTarget`, `_ambScheduleStochastic`/`_ambScheduleCustomSrc`/`_ambScheduleSeqSrc` in 17-ambient.js) must keep `node test/bloom-mod-parity.js` green — it fingerprints each shape×target's output range, period, and phase-folded waveform against `test/bloom-mod-parity-baseline.json` (tolerance-based, node path, core strips off). This is the safety net for the planned B2 unification onto the Design engine's mod rig.

**Shipped sample library:** `samples/` + `samples/manifest.json` (`{id, file, name, rootNote}`, `file` relative to `samples/`), loaded at boot by `loadSampleManifest()` in `04-instruments-samples.js` and addressed as `sample:<id>`. `deploy.sh` already copies and mirrors `samples/`, so an import is live on the next deploy. **`npm run samples [folder] [--dry] [--prune] [-y]`** walks a folder at any depth, copies the audio into `samples/` preserving structure, and regenerates the manifest. THREE things to know: (0) **re-runs diff, and size alone is not enough** — an audio file edited IN PLACE (gain normalised, a different take of the same length, a re-render) keeps its byte count exactly, so a size-only check left the app serving the OLD audio forever (reproduced: 440 Hz replaced by 880 Hz, same 22094 bytes, never recopied). The rule is: size differs → copy; size matches but the source is NEWER → compare bytes and copy only if they really differ, so a merely-touched file is not recopied; (1) **ids are derived from the source-relative path and MUST stay stable** — a project stores its voice as `sample:<id>`, so renaming or moving a file in the source renames its id and orphans saved projects, while re-running on an unchanged tree is a no-op (size-compared, nothing recopied); (2) `#` is mapped to `s` (F#3 → `Fs3`) rather than stripped — it is the URL fragment delimiter so it cannot survive in a filename, and DROPPING it would collapse `rhodes_F#3` and `rhodes_F3` onto one id with the second silently renamed `-2`; (3) AIFF/WMA/ALAC/CAF are skipped and reported, because Chrome cannot decode them and importing one would produce a library entry that is silently mute rather than an error. **SAMPLE `kind` — `tuned` / `loop` / `kit` (added 2026-08-08).** The distinction that matters is NOT "can I pitch it" (anything can be pitched) but **"does it carry its own tempo"**: a LOOP does, so the project tempo must reconcile with it and a note must NOT transpose it; a TUNED sample does not, so a note is free to transpose it. Before this, `loadSampleManifest` registered EVERY entry as a `Tone.Sampler` with one url keyed at `rootNote || 'C4'` — a 7.4-second 130 bpm drum loop was an "instrument", and playing it at C5 ran it at double speed. Manifest entries now carry `kind`, plus `bpm`+`seconds` for a loop and `rootNote` for a tuned one (a loop gets NO rootNote — inventing C4 is the fiction being removed). The importer classifies on three signals in precedence order: the pack's own folders (`one_shots/` vs `loops/` — sample packs are organised by exactly this distinction), then **a note name in the filename** (`pad C3.wav` is a tuned instrument however long it is — long pads/strings/organ are precisely what duration alone misfiles), then duration (measured from the RIFF header, no dependency; real packs: 0.62s longest one-shot vs 3.84s shortest loop), then a bpm in the name. `bpm` is what makes a loop usable — without it the engine cannot rate-match it and it drifts. **BACK-COMPAT TRAP worth keeping:** `kindExplicit` records whether the manifest actually SAID a kind. The strict `isSliceableSample` rule (loop → yes, tuned → no) applies only when it did; an older manifest keeps the previous heuristic (any single-url non-kit sample is sliceable), because silently withdrawing that from libraries nobody has re-imported would be a regression dressed as a model improvement. Verified both paths in-browser. STILL TO BUILD: loop PLAYBACK (trigger per bar, rate-match to project tempo, no note transposition) and the Drive picker — this change is the model only.
**OFFLINE BOUNCE: TWO PASSES + THE WASM CORE + SUSPEND CHECKPOINTS — faster than a live capture, and the checkpoints are the delivery spine, not a nicety.** Capture (`_ambCaptureStart`) is REAL-TIME (a 5-minute piece takes 5 minutes); the bounce renders offline. Pass 1 drives the engine on a SYNTHETIC clock and captures every note (`_ambCaptureNotesSynthetic` — the `_ambSeedRender` stub dance widened to all layers; deterministic, verified 210=210 across repeat captures); pass 2 replays the list through `Tone.Offline` (`_ambRenderOffline`). **SPEED (2026-08-09): eligible notes render in the WASM CORE, exactly as live playback renders them.** An all-Tone offline render of a dense 6-layer project ran at **0.34x realtime** — 95% of it native graph rendering (measured by patching `OfflineAudioContext.prototype.startRendering`), dominated by per-voice Tone subgraphs (a MonoSynth's filterEnvelope is a SIGNAL into a biquad param = per-sample coefficient recompute, the documented filter trap — 37 'sub' notes alone cost 5.6s per 10s). `_coreVoices.offlineBegin()` (03b) builds a SECOND voice-processor node on the offline context (wasm bytes cached at live init — `postMessage` TRANSFERS the buffer, so init keeps a copy), `noteOn` routes to it while the session is active, and playNote's existing core gate then works offline unchanged — pass 2 just stamps `window._ambEmitKey = n.key` per note so slots key per layer. Measured on the pinned benchmark (6 layers: sub bass / fm run / beat / 2 pads / sawtooth + prog+parts+salt+sections): 10s → **4.0x realtime** (vs 0.8x no-core), 30s → 4.2x, 60s → **7.4x** (fixed overhead amortizes). **CHECKPOINTS: the core's `alloc_voice` STEALS when its 256-voice pool fills, so the schedule is delivered in batches.** `Tone.Offline`'s `rawContext` is a standardized-audio-context wrapper with NO `.suspend` — the native context is captured by a SCOPED patch of `OfflineAudioContext.prototype.startRendering`, which schedules `suspend(t)` every 2s before calling the original (suspends scheduled at that point are accepted; the patch is restored in `finally`). Each checkpoint: deliver the next note batch (4.5s window), await `_coreVoices.offlineFlush()` — a stats round-trip whose reply is FIFO-ordered after every posted note, the delivery guarantee — report REAL progress, resume. Verified flat per-10s rms across a 60s bounce (no starved tail). The old clock-poll progress hack (ignore readings until the value drops = the context swap) is GONE — progress is checkpoint-driven now. **A followSalt BED ("Keys") UNDER A SALTED PROGRESSION SKIPS WRITE — the drone-under-a-progression doctrine, third instance.** Salt colours are a per-INSTANCE seeded draw (step-keyed: every pass deals new segments/colours), but the default `write:{on:true,bars:2,times:4}` captured ONE realization and replayed it — reported as "salting produces the same effect on every pass", and it did, for bars×times at a stretch. `_ambEuclidDeterministic` now returns true for `bed.followSalt` + prog source + colours in scope (`_ambLayerSaltColorsOn` — own salt wins, explicit all-zero = off, else area/part salt), which also THAWS an already-frozen Keys on the next play. Stated cost: the layer runs live (strum/humanize/voicing picks re-roll per pass — the Loop-off behaviour); ❄ Hold still freezes deliberately. Verified: Write never arms, three consecutive passes all differ, and the same take reproduces byte-identically (the evolution is deterministic per take id). **✎ GRID ON A PLAYING LAYER HARVESTS THE SOUNDING LOOP — it used to replace it with a different take and could silence the layer.** Two stacked causes behind "playback changes when I click Grid / Grid is buggy": (1) the seedmode handler BOOTSTRAPPED the lock by promoting the SEED PREVIEW (`_ambSeedPreview` + `_ambSeedPromote`) — an offline render of what the NEXT play would generate, i.e. a different realization from what is sounding — and `_ambSeedPreview` stubs the global clocks, which is off-limits mid-play anyway; it now runs ONLY while stopped. (2) `_ambGridEditStart` locked the layer to whatever the freeze store held — with an empty store that was an EMPTY 2-bar canvas: the layer fell silent on the click and the editor opened blank. It now harvests the last whole window from the ROLLING CAPTURE (the `_ambLockEngage` lift), sized to the layer's own cycle (prog cycle / unit period, ≥ 2 bars, ≤ 16) — **using the AREA's `cfg.bpm`, not `_ambBpm()`** (the workspace tempo input, which can differ — a wrong bpm harvested a 1-bar window where the prog cycle needed 4, replaying chord-1 pitches over the other chords). Handoff: anchor AT the window end (≤ now — the replay's from-clamp joins mid-phrase) and cancel only the PENDING future (`cancelBloomFutureVoices(key, now)`) — cancelling from the anchor stole up to a bar of already-sounding notes the replay's grace declined to re-fire (measured a 3 s seam; stepping the anchor to the NEXT boundary was worse: up to a whole loop of silence). Residual seam ≈ ≤ 1 bar worst-case at entry, the standard lock-engage handoff class. Verified E2E: canvas seeded with the real loop (32 steps / 22 events / the full 8 s prog cycle), playback continues through the click, ✕ Cancel resumes live generation. **SALT COLOUR SEGMENTS SNAP TO THE 1/8-BAR GRID NOW (`_ambSaltSegFracs`) — the even division placed colour changes at e.g. thirds of a bar, measured ±50-100 ms off the 16th grid on a followSalt Keys layer, which the ear reports as "the Keys layer is out of sync" while every ONSET-grid measurement comes back clean.** Measure this class with per-onset offsets from the nearest 16th relative to `_barGridAnchor` (read the anchor DURING play — stop nulls it); the tell is a layer whose offsets are consistent odd fractions (667/1333 ms patterns) rather than jitter. The snap lives in ONE helper shared by all three consumers — the plan's note times (`_ambBedSaltPlan`), the set builder's pos midpoints (`_ambSaltSegSets`, which now takes the fractions array), and the colour resolver's pos→segIdx mapping in `_ambProgCurrentChord` — so they cannot disagree about where a segment starts; the dedupe after snapping is what makes the EFFECTIVE segment count `fr.length`, not the rolled nSeg. Grid units are POS-SPACE fractions (1/(8·chordBars) via `_ambSaltSlotBars`), which keeps plan and resolver agreeing even in progMerge mode where the plan's unit spans a chord GROUP. **THE SALT SNAP GRID MUST BE GRADED BY THE SPAN IT IS APPLIED TO, NOT BY THE CHORD — voicings (`chord ÷ N`) and salt colours subdivide the SAME chord span independently, and the plan lays its segments across ONE VOICING SUB-SLOT.** Grading the 1/8-bar grid by the chord's bars therefore divided it by N and every segment drifted (measured 125 ms at ÷2, 83 at ÷3, 63 at ÷4; ÷1 was clean, which is exactly why the original snap fix looked complete). `_ambBedSaltPlan` now derives the span's own bar length (`unitSec / barSec`) — identical to the old value at ÷1, absolute at every subdivision. Verified at ÷3: onsets are `0 · 0.25 · 0.5 · 0.667 · 1.167 · 1.333 …` — the sub-slot ORIGINS sit on the triplet grid (that is what ÷3 means, not a bug) and the segments inside each land on clean eighths. NOTE the semantics that remain: the plan re-runs its whole colour sequence inside EACH voicing sub-slot, so at `chord ÷ N` the colours move N× faster than the Colour knob implies, while the resolver `_ambProgCurrentChord` measures pos across the whole chord — the two agree at ÷1 and diverge above it by design (each re-voicing gets its own colour journey). MEASUREMENT CAVEAT: an off-grid metric of the form `at % gridSec` is FRAME-DEPENDENT — pass-1 captures normalize the first onset to 0, so a config change that moves the first note shifts every reading (a colours-off "baseline" run showed a spurious 36 ms on every subdivision). Compare before/after within ONE config, never across configs. THE SNAP IS A PER-(LAYER, CHORD) TOGGLE (`layer.saltFree = {"<slot>": 1}`, absent = snapped): the chord-matrix cell modal's "◈ on the beat / 〰 flow free" button lets one layer's colour changes over one chord keep the unsnapped even division as a deliberate feel — resolved through `_ambSaltFreeNow(step)` (the emit-scope idiom the nudge uses, so the plan and resolver agree by construction), normalized beside `saltNudge`. The toggle button flips IN PLACE (the modal stays open) — a toggle that closes its modal reads as "did that work?". Deliberate sound change for salted projects: colour-change TIMING moves onto the 8th grid (pitch content unchanged); salt-off projects are byte-identical (colors 0 → early return before any of this). Also verified in the same pass: a bounce mid-play does NOT damage the next live play's alignment, and frozen Write replay is grid-exact — the off-grid segments were the whole story. **THE BOUNCE RAN ~10 dB HOT — it skipped BOTH input trims, so everything was crushed by the limiter/soft-clipper, and heavy limiting is exactly what flattens reverb tails and delay repeats until they read as "no FX".** Live, Bloom sums through `_BLOOM_MASTER_TRIM` (0.5) into `masterBus`, itself a `Gain(0.6)` headroom trim, BEFORE the master chain; the offline stage started at Warmth and had neither. Measured on the dense benchmark: peak 0.939 → 0.447, rms 0.225 → 0.055 (0.6 × 0.5 = 0.3, i.e. −10.5 dB). This is a SOUND change, not just a level one — at +10 dB the limiter and the soft-clipper were doing far more work than they ever do live. When reproducing a signal path offline, the input trims are part of the path. **AND THE BOUNCE NOW AUDITS ITSELF INSTEAD OF RELYING ON MY ENUMERATION**: after the chains are built it walks the CONFIG for every engaged effect (per-layer delay/dist/chorus/phaser/autopan/glitch mix>0 or dryKill, reverb sends, bus sends) and checks each has a node in THIS render — anything engaged-but-unbuilt is named in the toast and the Harvest row. Four rounds of "which subsystem did I forget" (core strips → bus sends → dry-catch → trims) is the evidence that the list must not live in a person's head. It also flags a Glitch layer that landed on the node fallback, since Glitch is core-only. **A DRY BOUNCE NOW ANNOUNCES ITSELF — the wet-path build was wrapped in `catch (e) { wet = false; }`, so ANY throw sent every note straight to the output stage and the result was a silent, totally dry render that reported nothing anywhere.** That is the worst possible failure mode for "no FX on bounce": indistinguishable from a routing gap, and invisible to every measurement on a project that happens not to throw (which is why three rounds of synthetic verification kept passing). Three changes: (1) a throw in `_ambSyncMods` falls back to building layer BY LAYER, so one unbuildable layer costs only its own FX instead of the whole render's — verified by forcing `_ambSyncMods` to throw and watching the render stay WET; (2) the reason is kept (`res.wetErr`, `res.lostFx`, both passed through `_ambBounceToBank`) and `console.warn`ed; (3) the toast LEADS with it — `⚠ Bounced DRY — "name"… Missing: EVERY per-layer FX chain — this render is DRY: <reason>` — rather than burying it in a trailing footnote. Verified by forcing BOTH `_ambSyncMods` and `_ambBuildMod` to throw: the file is still produced, `wet:false`, the reason is carried, and the toast announces it. GENERAL RULE, third instance this session: a swallowed catch around graph construction turns a loud bug into an unfalsifiable one — if the fallback changes what the user hears, it must say so. **BLOOM MIX BUSES AND THEIR FX SENDS ARE REPRODUCED OFFLINE (`_bloomOfflineBuses`) — without them a project whose reverb/delay/chorus live on a BUS SEND bounced completely dry.** Live, a layer's destination is `_ambBusDest(L)`: its bus gain (a/b/c/d), and each bus taps per-FX SEND gains into the shared returns (`fxSendBus[name]` → a fully-wet FX → the master bus). The bounce's engine returned `outStage` for EVERY layer, so bus routing and every bus send were dropped silently — the second "still no FX on bounce" report, and a completely different gap from the core-strips one (per-layer FX were fine by then; these are the SHARED returns). The offline rig builds a Gain per bus in use and, lazily, only the returns actually sent to, with 03's own constructor values and `wet` forced to 1 (the send gain is the mix, exactly as `rebuildMasterChain` wires it — the returns are PARALLEL into the bus there, not the serial chain they are constructed as). A return that fails to build is pushed to `res.missing` and named in the completion toast rather than vanishing. Measured on a layer with NO per-layer FX, bus b only: reverb 90 → rms +41% and channel correlation 1.000 → 0.506 (a stereo tail where there was none); delay 80 → tail ×1.25; chorus 90 → correlation 0.554. STILL NOT REPRODUCED: the bus `entry` point (full/postwarmth/postvinyl/direct) — every offline bus enters at the output stage, so a bus deliberately skipping Warmth/Vinyl sounds like one that doesn't. **THE BOUNCE RENDERS CORE STRIPS NOW — before this it ran a DIFFERENT ENGINE from live playback, which is the whole "bounces don't sound the same" report.** Live playback runs each layer's strip + FX inside the WASM core (strips default ON); `stripAcquire` bails on `!_running()`, and an offline context is never 'running', so EVERY bounce silently fell through to the NODE FX implementations — different DSP for dist/chorus/phaser/delay/autopan/EQ/pan-law, and **no Glitch at all** (it has no node build). Measured directly: `LIVE chain: CORE strip · BOUNCE chain: node chain`. `_coreVoices.offlineBegin()` now also provides STRIPS on the offline node (`_offStripAcquire`, `off.strips`, a `connectSend` branch claiming the offline node's output 16 for the offline reverb, and `makeShimParam(slot, which, init, post)` parameterized so shims post to the offline port). `stripAcquire`/`stripRelease`/`stripRekey`/`stripFor` all route to the session while a bounce is in flight, so **17-ambient needed no change at all**. **ORDERING IS LOad-BEARING: `offlineBegin()` must be awaited BEFORE `_ambSyncMods()`** — `_ambBuildMod` asks for a strip and falls through to a node chain when there is none, so starting the session afterwards left every layer on the fallback (that was the state after the first attempt, and the measurement caught it). PROOF used, since an audio A/B is not available headless (the capture path's silence detector never finalizes there): hook `MessagePort.prototype.postMessage` and diff the `strip_*` COMMAND STREAM live vs bounce — **14/14 distinct configs identical including `strip_glitch`**, the only live-only entry being `strip_enable(0)` (the teardown on stop, which a render correctly never does). NOTE a `new AudioWorkletNode` hook does NOT catch these — 03b builds through Tone's `createAudioWorkletNode`. CONSEQUENCES: the node-chain width compensation (`_panScale`) must return 1 for a core-strip layer or the image narrows twice (the strip scales the side itself, as live); and the Glitch "missing" report is now gated on `!coreOn` rather than unconditional. Speed cost is real and expected — the same 10 s dense render goes 4.0x → 1.5x realtime, because it is now doing the full per-layer DSP live playback does (node-fallback for comparison: 0.4x). **SPREAD/SPATIALIZE WERE STRUCTURALLY DEAD IN THE BOUNCE — measured corr(L,R) = 1.000 at spread 100 — while delay, pan position and reverb all worked** (A/B-measure each axis in the buffer before believing any "FX are missing" report: delay = rms lift, pan = L/R ratio, width = channel correlation). Two causes, both offline-only: the node chain's layer `Tone.Panner` is channelCount 1 (the documented mono-downmix — live playback escapes it because core STRIPS carry width, and the bounce's chains are ALWAYS node chains since `stripAcquire` bails on a suspended offline context), and pass 1 captures spread voices at the FULL fan (live core-strip semantics — the strip scales the side by space/100 later, and offline there is no strip). Fixes in `_ambRenderOffline`: every offline chain's panner is widened (`e.pan.channelCount = 2` — at pan 0 a StereoPanner passes L/R through; offline-only, the live node engine keeps its calibrated mono law), and `deliverNote` scales each note's pan by space/100 for spread-mode layers when `cap.stripWidth` (recorded at capture) — Spatialize positions are absolute placements, not a fan, and are exempt. corr 1.000 → 0.716 at spread 100. GLITCH is core-only and therefore absent from a bounce — reported via `res.missing` (named per layer) instead of failing silently. **PER-CHORD RE-ROLL (`layer.saltNudge = {"<slot>": count}`)**: bump the count to deal ONE layer a new realization of ONE chord slot — folded (only when non-zero → byte-parity) into the two dedicated per-slot seeded draws: the colour pick in `_ambProgCurrentChord` and the segment count in `_ambProgSaltSegCount` (folded INSIDE the latter so the resolver and the followSalt plan can never disagree on nSeg). Keyed on the WRITTEN slot (step % cycle len) so it holds every pass. UI: the chord matrix cell modal's "🎲 Roll a new <chord> for <layer>" (with a hint when no salt colours apply to that layer). Verified: nudging bed slot 2 changes ONLY bed|2 (bass + the bed's other slots byte-identical), deterministic per count, re-anchors while playing. NORMALIZE TRAP FOUND HERE: `_ambNormalizeLayerSalt` ran for extras/seq/samp but NOT the four primaries — a primary's salt fields were never coerced. Primaries are now in the normalize sweep. **THE FIELD REPORT THAT SURVIVED EVERY AGGREGATE CHECK — "layers out of sync, most cut out after a few seconds" — was FOUR stacked defects, and the decisive diagnostic was a PER-LAYER SOLO DECOMPOSITION of the render** (wrap playNote, drop every note whose `window._ambEmitKey` isn't the target layer, bucket rms per 2s — the sub layer read `0.104 0.107 0.060 0.000 0.000…` while every whole-mix rms check looked flat because core-rendered voices dominated the energy). (1) **Tone's OfflineContext ticks its OWN clock to the render's END before startRendering, and `Tone.Source.start()` clamps starts against `Tone.now()`** — so every Tone voice built at a suspend CHECKPOINT started past the buffer and rendered silent; batch-0 voices (built in the callback, clock still 0) played, rang to ~6s, then nothing. Node-path notes are therefore delivered UP FRONT in the callback; only CORE notes (wasm-scheduled, immune) batch at checkpoints, and the checkpoint handler re-aligns `tc._currentTime = t` as the safety net for a core-fallback build. (2) **Pass 1's engine skipped play-start init**: no `_ambRestoreLocks` (a saved project's locked/frozen layers regenerate instead of replaying), no `_playStartAt`, and the `_barGridAnchor` pin was `_masterEng`-only — unit-synced layers anchored on private leads, the documented out-of-sync failure. `E2._bounceGrid` extends the pin gate. (3) **Everything TICK-SCHEDULED was dead in pass 2** — `_ambScheduleStochastic` (trance gate, unit-gate chop, stochastic/custom/seq mod sources) runs from the live 150ms tick and schedules ~1.2s ahead; offline nothing ticks, so a gated layer played its initial window and then the gate signal HELD its last value (a 0 step = silent forever). The callback now sub-steps it across the WHOLE timeline (explicit times onto native params are clock-safe). NOTE the scheduler reads the gate from `layer.tg` (`L0.tg` in `_ambScheduleTg`) — a probe setting `fx.tg` tests nothing. (4) **`samp` layers store `sampleId`, never a `"sample:"` string** — the cfg regex missed them and their samplers never entered the offline bank (silent layers). The bank is now built from the CAPTURED NOTES' resolved types (exact by construction), with the cfg regexes as belt. **Disposal became WANTED once delivery was correct**: an undisposed MonoSynth keeps its filterEnvelope signal in a biquad param (per-sample coefficient recompute) for its whole graph life — a node-voice-heavy bounce ran 0.8x; end-aware sweeps at each checkpoint (`_bloomOfflineSweep` in 04 — voices record `start+dur+release+0.25` at the park site) took the same shape to 6.6x with identical rms/tails. `res.master` reports whether the offline master stage built (a failure renders unmastered + is named in the toast) — an earlier silent build failure produced a peak-1.24 file once and was undiagnosable after the fact. **THE TRUNCATION BUG (was live in every slow bounce): voice-dispose `setTimeout`s run on the WALL clock, and on a render slower than realtime they fired while the render clock was behind — disposing voices before their audio rendered** (measured max sample diff 0.84 between timer-on and timer-parked renders). Fixed by setting `_offlineSamplerOverride` during pass 2 (via `_bloomOfflineScope` in 04): playNote's cleanup site then parks voices in `_offlineVoiceRefs` and schedules NO timers — the same mechanism the Tracks export always used. **SAMPLES render in bounces now**: `_bloomOfflineSamplerBank(ids, dest)` (04) builds offline-context samplers for the ids regexed out of the cfg clone (`"sample:..."` — the prefix is 8 chars, an off-by-one here silently empties the bank), wild/dynamic ids pull in built+imported entries; `getSampleEntry`'s override branch carries the LIVE entry's metadata (kind/bpm/rootNote) copied by name SKIPPING 'sampler' (a lazy getter — reading it builds a live-context sampler), so loop tempo-match works offline. **The generator is STOPPED before a bounce** (`_ambBounceBegin` + a belt in `_ambRenderOffline`): inside `Tone.Offline` the GLOBAL Tone context is the offline one until the render resolves, so a live tick would schedule its voices into the bounce. **PHASES**: `opts.onPhase` fires compose → composed → instruments → instrumentsReady → rendered → encode → banked; `onStatus` narrates '1/4 Composing… / 2/4 Preparing instruments… / 3/4 Rendering N notes… X% (Ys) / 4/4 Encoding…' and `onProgress` is real render progress. The narrator is gated on a `_renderStarted` flag or it stomps the phase-2 status. **END FADE**: the bounce cuts dead at `seconds`, mid-tail — a 30ms linear fade in `_ambBounceToBank` ends the file at zero (verified last-40-samples max 0.0007) instead of a click. Verified end-to-end through the real UI: 30s bounce in 7.1s, bank row '30s · 5.3 MB · 120 BPM · 15 bars', blob decodes to exactly 30.000s, audition clean, live playback audible before AND after a bounce. VERIFY A BOUNCE BY PEAK/RMS OF THE BUFFER, never by note count or wall time — two silent-failure bugs (the vq drain, the voice-steal cap) both kept healthy-looking note counts. **THE DEFERRED VOICE-BUILD QUEUE MUST BE OFF DURING AN OFFLINE RENDER — one bug, three symptoms.** `_vqShouldDefer` queues any note more than 0.25 s ahead, and `_vqPump` runs on a WALL-CLOCK `setTimeout`, which does not advance while an OfflineAudioContext renders. So a bounce built only the notes inside its first 0.25 s: reported as "about 1 second of play then it cuts out but I hear the FX trails" (the trails being the tails of the few voices that did build), and as "there don't seem to be any FX" (almost nothing was there to process). Worse, the queue then DRAINED AFTER the render on the LIVE context — reported as "playback starts when I choose bounce". `window.__bloopsOfflineRender` now suppresses deferral (checked beside the existing `_offlineSamplerOverride` bail, which is how the Tracks export already solved this), and the bounce clears the flag and `_vqClear()`s in a `finally`. Verified per-second RMS across a 20 s bounce: 20/20 seconds carry signal (was ~1), and after a bounce `_voiceBuildQueue` is empty with ZERO playNote calls reaching the live path. GENERAL RULE: anything driven by a wall-clock timer is invisible to an offline render — the queue, `setTimeout` voice disposal, the health watchdog. The two-pass design keeps the GENERATOR clear of this, but playNote itself still runs inside the render and had to be told.
**THE VOICE-STEAL CAP MUST BE BYPASSED DURING THE BOUNCE — without it a dense project bounces to PURE SILENCE, and the silence renders FASTER, which is exactly how it hid.** `_enforceVoiceBudget` already bailed on `_offlineSamplerOverride` (the Tracks export's flag) with a comment naming the failure precisely: it "ramps every voice past the cap to -80 dB at offline t=0 — which silently kills the entire render". The Bloom bounce renders offline WITHOUT that flag, so it was stealing its own voices; at offline t=0 every over-budget voice is muted before it starts, so the kill is total rather than partial. Measured on a 6-layer project (bass/run/beat/bed/Keys + progression + salt + sections): wet bounce **peak 0.0000, rms 0.00000 with 174 notes "played"** — a silent file with a healthy-looking note count. With the bypass (`window.__bloopsOfflineRender`, checked beside the existing `_offlineSamplerOverride` bail in BOTH `_enforceVoiceBudget` and the effective-budget read): peak 0.5733, rms 0.05766. **THE TELL WAS THE TIMING, NOT THE AUDIO:** the wet render measured 9.05 s against dry's 25.05 s — wet FASTER than dry is backwards, and it was fast because it was processing nothing. Cost of the fix is real and is the honest price of rendering the notes: that project's 10 s wet bounce went 22.1 s → 36.1 s (0.28x realtime). An earlier A/B that reported a healthy wet-vs-dry difference was run on a 3-layer project that never exceeded the budget, which is why this survived verification. GENERAL RULE: any new offline-render path must be checked against EVERY wall-clock/adaptive subsystem the live engine runs — the deferred voice-build queue and this voice cap were the same bug class twice, and both fail SILENTLY. Verify a bounce by measuring peak/rms of the returned buffer, never by note count or wall time. **WET PATH (same day):** the per-layer mod chains now build ON THE OFFLINE CONTEXT and the notes route through them. Inside `Tone.Offline` the global Tone context IS the offline one, and `_ambSyncMods`/`_ambLayerDest` both read the module-global `_E` — so pointing `_E` at an engine whose `busNode()` returns the OFFLINE destination makes the whole chain (vcf → vca → level → gate → pan → FX → bus) build and route there. A live node there throws "cannot connect to an AudioNode belonging to a different context", which is the tell if it regresses. Pass 1 hands pass 2 its cfg CLONE so `n.key` resolves to the same layers. Proven by A/B on an IDENTICAL 104-note stream: wet peak 0.3728 / rms 0.05279 vs dry 0.3929 / 0.05739 — the chain measurably changes the signal — at 17.4× vs 26.8× realtime, so FX cost ~1.5×. A layer whose chain fails to build still renders dry rather than going silent. **OUTPUT STAGE, NOT THE MASTER CHAIN (deliberate).** The real master (DC → Warmth → Vinyl → Compressor → Glue → Volume → Limiter) is built from module-scope `const`s in 03 on the LIVE context at boot — `rebuildMasterChain()` only REWIRES those existing nodes, so it cannot produce offline ones, and connecting a live node into the offline graph throws. Reproducing it properly means refactoring 03 into a context-parameterised `buildMasterChain(ctx)`: ~59 node constructions plus every consumer of `masterBus`/`masterVolume`, in boot-critical audio code whose failure mode is LIVE PLAYBACK BREAKING — and which the golden gate cannot cover, since that only exercises the Rust core. So the bounce builds the part a FILE actually needs — a −3 dB `Tone.Limiter` and the master `Volume`, reading 03's own values — and nothing else. **A LIMITER ALONE DOES NOT PREVENT CLIPPING — the soft-clipper after it does.** `Tone.Limiter` is a 20:1 compressor, not a brickwall, so transients overshoot: measured peak **1.4918 with 377 samples over full scale** on a hot area with limiter+volume only. Live playback has `masterClipper` (a WaveShaper, identity below 0.85, tanh knee to a 0.97 ceiling) AFTER the limiter, and the bounce now reproduces it with the same constants — peak 0.9200, **0 overs** on the same material. An early version of this note claimed "an export cannot clip" on the strength of one passing test; it was true of that material, not in general. **THE COLOUR STAGES ARE OFF BY DEFAULT** (`warmthOn` false, `vinylOn` false, `ottOn` false) — only `compOn` is true (~2:1 at −3 dB), so the bounce reproduces the master COMPRESSOR from `globalFx` and that closes the gap for a default workspace. **Warmth is also reproduced** (added the same day): it is plain nodes — low-shelf lift at 160, presence dip at 3k, high-shelf cut at 7k, a tanh drive waveshaper, then the fizz low-pass — so it rebuilds offline from `globalFx` with the same constants and mapping as `applyMasterWarmth`, placed BEFORE the compressor as it is live. Verified spectrally rather than by eye: at warmth 80 / drive 40 / cut 8k the high:low band ratio falls 0.3352 → 0.2862, i.e. the top is pulled down, which is what warmth does. (Note the overall level RISES — the tanh drive is peak-normalised, so at drive 40 it gives roughly 2.6× on quiet signal; that is faithful, live does the same.) **VINYL AND GLUE DID NOT NEED THE 03 REFACTOR EITHER.** The plan was to turn 03's module-scope master nodes into a `buildMasterChain(ctx)` factory — boot-critical surgery the golden gate cannot cover. What they actually needed was TWO ADDITIVE EXPOSURES, 8 lines, none of which touch how the live graph is built: (1) the Glue worklet's blob module URL is no longer revoked after `addModule`, and is published as `window._bloopsOttModuleUrl`, so an OFFLINE context can register the SAME processor — re-deriving that DSP in the bounce would be a second source of truth that silently drifts; (2) `_makeVinylBed` is published as `window._bloopsMakeVinylBed`, so the bounce generates the SAME crackle/hiss bed. Vinyl's wobble (wow+flutter LFOs → a short delay), age low-pass and parallel bed are then plain nodes. **THE BUG THAT COST A ROUND, and the reason to distrust a silent catch:** the OTT node was first built with a raw `new AudioWorkletNode`, which is not registered with Tone's graph bookkeeping, so `Tone.connect` rejected it — inside a `catch` that swallowed the error. Glue therefore did NOTHING while every other stage worked, and the tell was rms identical to 5 decimal places (fully bypassed) rather than subtly off. Build it with `Tone.getContext().createAudioWorkletNode(...)`, exactly as 03 does. Verified one stage at a time on identical material: plain peak 0.4155/rms 0.07567 → +Vinyl 0.5376/0.07864 → +Glue 0.2427/0.04344, all with zero samples over full scale. Every master stage is now reproduced, so the bounce's `missing` list reports only a stage that FAILED to build, not a standing gap. **FILE OUTPUT (same day):** `_ambBounceToBank` encodes the rendered buffer through the SAME encoders real Capture uses (`audioBufferToWav` / `audioBufferToMp3`, global function declarations in 10-tracks) and pushes the SAME bank record shape, so a bounce is indistinguishable from a capture once banked — it differs only in `source: 'Bloom (bounce)'`. `Tone.Offline` resolves a Tone ToneAudioBuffer, not a native AudioBuffer; the encoders need `.get()` first. Verified by DECODING THE EXPORT BACK rather than trusting the blob: header `RIFF/WAVE`, 15.00 s @ 44100 Hz 2ch, peak 0.3254, 51.7% non-silent — 30 s bounced to a 5.1 MB wav in 1.6 s (19× realtime). Exposed as `window._bloomBounceToBank(seconds, {name, mp3, folder})`. **UI (same day):** the bounce lives in the ⤓ Capture menu, under an `⚡ Bounce — faster than real time` header with the same lengths (30 s / 1 / 2 / 5 min / custom). It belongs there because it answers the same question ("give me a file of this") and the result lands in the same bank. `_ambBounceBegin` shows a progress modal and yields once (30 ms) before starting — pass 1 stubs globals and CANNOT yield, so it blocks the thread and the modal would otherwise never paint. Measured through the real UI: clicking "30 sec" produced a bank row `Bloom (bounce) · bounce-30s.wav · 30s · 5.3 MB · 120 BPM · 15 bars` with full controls (audition / download / Drive upload) **2.3 s** after the click. TEST TRAP: a first check that just grepped the page for /bounce/i passed in 0.2 s — it was matching the MENU LABEL, not a bank row; assert on the bank host's text for the generated filename. Remaining: master tone colour (the 03 refactor above). **TRAP THAT COST A RUN:** this file's top level is SCRIPT scope, so `function _ambFoo` ALREADY creates `window._ambFoo` — exporting a wrapper under the same name overwrites the function with something that calls itself (instant stack overflow). The bounce is exposed as `window._bloomBounce` / `_bloomBounceCapture` for that reason; same family as the documented note that `_masterEng` is a lexical const and NOT on window.
**DRIVE SAMPLE IMPORT ALREADY EXISTED — the gap was CLASSIFICATION, not the picker.** `triggerImportSampleFromDrive` (04) has long prompted for a folder NAME (`findDriveFolderByPath` → `_listDriveAudioFiles` → `_showDriveSamplePicker` → `fetchDriveBinaryAsBlob` → `registerSampleFromBlob`); there is no filesystem browser and none is wanted. What it did NOT do is decide what the file IS: `registerSampleFromBlob` hardcoded `rootNote: 'C4'` with no kind, so every Drive-imported drum loop landed as a tuned instrument — the same conflation the manifest path had. `_classifyImported(name, durSec)` now mirrors the Node importer's rules exactly (one-shot/loop word in the path → note name → duration → bpm), with duration taken from the DECODED buffer (better than the RIFF-header estimate the Node side must use, and it costs a decode that was happening anyway). Three things have to move together or the classification only survives until you close the tab: `registerSampleFromBlob` sets it, `persistImportedSample` writes `kind`/`bpm`/`seconds` into the IndexedDB record, and `loadImportedSamples` restores them (a record saved before `kind` existed reads as tuned + NOT explicit, keeping the old sliceability heuristic). Verified end to end including a reload: `drum_loop_128`→loop/128, `kick oneshot`→tuned, `pad C3`→tuned rooted C3, `mystery` (3 s, no hint)→loop. The workspace backup needed NO change — it destructures `const { blob, ...meta } = rec` specifically to carry future fields. A caller that already knows what it has (a captured sample states its rootNote, a pad states padLoop) skips classification.
**LOOP PLAYBACK (2026-08-08): tempo-matched, never transposed BY THE NOTE, but a deliberate varispeed survives.** Applied in `_resolveSampleVoice` — the ONE chokepoint the node builder and the core sample path both go through (its own comment says so), so both engines get identical behaviour from a single change. For `kind === 'loop'`: `playbackRate = clamp(projectBpm / info.bpm, 0.25, 4)`, ignoring the note entirely; with no stated bpm it plays as recorded rather than guessing. Rate-matching shifts pitch as a side effect (no time-stretch in Web Audio without heavy DSP) — 125 into 120 is 4%, well under a semitone, which is the standard trade and inaudible on drums. Project tempo is read off `#tempo-input`, since 03's `getBpm()` is module-scope and not exported. **THE TRAP THIS ALMOST CREATED:** the Sample layer applies its Pitch knob by folding it into the note (`baseFreq = rootFreq * 2^(pitch/12)`), so overriding the rate wholesale made that knob INERT for loops — the stored-but-ignored-field trap, caught before shipping. A loop's varispeed now travels down the explicit `params.drumTune` "pitch this in place" channel instead, which the resolver keeps; the note still does not transpose it. Verified: note C4→C5 leaves the rate flat at 0.9231 while tempo 100/120/130/150 gives 0.7692/0.9231/1.0/1.1538, and drumTune ∓12 st halves/doubles it on top. Tuned samples are untouched on both axes.
**IN-PLACE MODE (added 2026-08-07):** if the source folder is INSIDE `./samples` — dropping packs into `samples/sounds/` — nothing is copied; the files are registered where they already are and the folder structure is preserved. Copying in that case wrote a SECOND copy at `samples/<slug>/…` and shipped every file twice. Detected with `path.relative(DEST, src)` not escaping upward; ids derive from the path relative to `samples/` so they still describe where the file is. The tradeoff to know: copy mode SLUGS filenames, which is what quietly removes URL-hostile characters, so in-place WARNS about `#`/`?`/`%`/`\` instead (a `kick#2.wav` is fetched as `kick` and 404s) — it cannot rename your files for you. Files already in `samples/` that the source no longer has are KEPT and still get a manifest entry (they would otherwise ship but be unreachable); `--prune` deletes them after a confirmation and tidies the empty directories. Loading is LAZY (see the sampler gotcha below), so library SIZE is not a boot cost — only what you actually use is fetched.

There is no build step, no linter, and no test suite. All JavaScript runs directly in the browser.

## Architecture

This is a browser-based music player that streams audio files from Google Drive. It is a single-page app served by a minimal Express static server (`server.js`).

### Module structure

Four vanilla JS modules are loaded in order via `<script>` tags in `index.html`:

1. **`js/google-drive-api.js`** — `GoogleDriveAPI` class. Initializes `gapi` and Google Identity Services (GIS) OAuth client, handles sign-in/sign-out, searches Drive folders, paginates through audio files, and fetches artist metadata.
2. **`js/music-player.js`** — `MusicPlayer` class. Wraps the HTML5 `<audio>` element, manages blob URL lifecycle (creates on play, revokes on next load), and handles keyboard shortcuts (Space, Arrow keys).
3. **`js/playlist-manager.js`** — `PlaylistManager` class. Owns the track array, renders the playlist DOM, handles drag-and-drop and touch reordering, and implements shuffle (Fisher-Yates, preserves original order for restore).
4. **`js/app.js`** — Wires everything together, manages auth UI state, loads playlist options from a Drive file at `bloops/playlists`, and shows loading/toast feedback.

### Event-driven communication

Modules communicate via custom DOM events (no external event bus):

| Event | Source | Consumer |
|---|---|---|
| `authStatusChanged` | `GoogleDriveAPI` | `app.js` (updates UI) |
| `trackLoaded` | `MusicPlayer` | `app.js` |
| `trackEnded` | `MusicPlayer` | `PlaylistManager` (advance track) |
| `requestNextTrack` | `MusicPlayer` | `PlaylistManager` |
| `requestPreviousTrack` | `MusicPlayer` | `PlaylistManager` |

### Key data flows

**Auth:** User clicks connect → GIS OAuth popup → access token stored in `GoogleDriveAPI` instance → `authStatusChanged` fires → UI updates.

**Loading music:** Folder name selected/searched → `driveAPI.searchFolders()` → `driveAPI.getMusicFilesFromFolders()` (paginated) → optionally `driveAPI.fetchArtistName()` from a special "Artist name" file in the folder → `playlist.setTracks()` renders list → first track auto-loads.

**Playback:** `PlaylistManager.playTrack(index)` → `MusicPlayer.loadTrack()` → fetch audio blob from Drive with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header → blob converted to object URL → set as `<audio>` src → play.

### Google Drive folder convention

The app expects:
- A `bloops/` folder at the root of My Drive with subfolders `bloops/effects`, `bloops/projects`, `bloops/exports`. The Bloops sign-in init creates these on first run with one consolidated confirmation prompt.
- A text file at `bloops/playlists` listing folder paths (one per line) that the Player turns into playlists. Paths use `/` for parent/child nesting (e.g. `bloops/exports`). The init step seeds this file with `bloops/exports`.
- Audio folders optionally containing a file named **"Artist name"** whose first line is used as the artist, and an image named **"Artwork"** used as album art.
- Supported audio formats: `mp3, wav, flac, aac, ogg, m4a, opus`.

### Google API credentials

`CLIENT_ID` and `API_KEY` are hardcoded in `js/google-drive-api.js`. To use a different Google Cloud project, replace those constants and update the authorized JavaScript origins in the OAuth client configuration. The app uses the `drive.readonly` scope.

### Deployment

`deploy.sh` stages the project, installs production dependencies, and uploads via SFTP using `lftp`. See `pre_deploy.txt` for GoDaddy cPanel prerequisites (Node.js app setup, SFTP credentials).

- **`showCtxMenu` MEASURES ITS BOX TWICE — once on append and again on the next FRAME — because a menu can reflow taller after the first read and then hang off the bottom of the screen.** Reported as the layer ⋯ menu being cut off for lower layers on a phone: the old placement read `offsetHeight` once immediately after `appendChild` and used a hardcoded 160px width, so a 452px menu was positioned as if it were 422px and 22px of it (including Delete) sat below the viewport, unreachable. Placement now: `visualViewport` dimensions when available (`window.innerHeight` can include the area behind mobile browser toolbars), a dynamic `max-height` of the space actually available so an over-tall menu SCROLLS instead of overflowing, real `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, clamping on BOTH edges (`Math.max(pad, Math.min(...))` — the old one-sided clamp could push the top off-screen), and the whole thing re-run in `requestAnimationFrame`. Verified at 390x780 / 390x420 (menu taller than the viewport → scrolls) / 1200x900, with the last item reachable in every case.
- **THE LAYER HEADER'S ⋯ MENU replaced the Solo button AND the whole `.ambient-layer-topctrls` row — the buttons still EXIST, hidden, and the menu items click them.** The row's ten actions (Solo, ●, 🎤, ✎, ⧉, ⇄, 🎲, ✦, ★, ✕) live in a `showCtxMenu` dropdown behind the header ⋯ (`.ambient-layer-menu-btn`, in Solo's old slot). Design: the buttons stay in the (display:none) topctrls div so every delegated handler and state painter (`_ambSoloSyncAll`, `_ambFreezeSyncAll`) works untouched; the menu is a proxy layer with state-aware labels ("✓ S Solo (on)", "● Stop — keep the melody take"). THREE consequences to preserve: (1) states that must never be invisible ride the TRIGGER via `:has()` — an active Solo turns it solo-yellow + an S badge, an armed take pulses it red (the drum-solo lesson: hidden state + a stateful widget = a bug report); (2) any handler that positions from ITS button's rect gets 0×0 now — the ⇄ morph submenu falls back to `window.__ambMenuAnchor`, stashed from the trigger when the menu opens; a NEW topctrls action that opens positioned UI needs the same fallback; (3) every item fn is deferred one tick (showCtxMenu runs fn then dismisses — the Add-area idiom). A new per-layer action = add the hidden button to topctrls as before + one `add()` line in the menu builder. **TEST TRAP: `showCtxMenu` renders the gap after an item's leading icon as an EM SPACE (U+2003)** — `/^S Solo$/` fails against a menu label that prints as "S Solo"; normalize `\s+` → ' ' before matching menu text (plain `\u00a0` replacement is NOT enough, it is not an NBSP).
- **IN-BROWSER TEST TRAPS that cost a cycle each (2026-07-31).** These produced *false failures* — the code was right and the test was wrong — so verify the test before "fixing" the code. (1) **Computed style on a collapsed card lies.** `.ambient-layer` starts collapsed and its CSS hides children, so `getComputedStyle(el).display` reads `none` in every state and cannot distinguish shown-vs-hidden. Read the **inline** `el.style.display` you set, or expand the card first (`card.classList.remove('collapsed')`). Hit on the Fold/Pattern group and again on the Learn Library row. (2) **A child of a hidden parent still reports its own display.** Hiding a row via its `.ambient-ctrl` parent will NOT make `getComputedStyle(child).display === 'none'` — check the parent you actually hid. (3) **New layers are added MUTED** (`_ambMarkFreshLayer`), so a freshly added layer emits nothing until un-muted — a test that adds a layer, presses play and hears silence has proven nothing. (4) **Control ids are `p + '-name'`**, and `_ambNamespaceHtml` rewrites `id="ambient-…"` → `id="<prefix>-…"` per engine, so a primary-panel id like `ambient-vinyl-hiss` is `mix-bloom-vinyl-hiss` in the master panel. Writing `p + 'name'` (no dash) renders a control whose wiring never binds — it looks present and does nothing. (5) **`node --check` is not enough for a 2 MB file**: a dropped `return` is valid JS and rendered the whole panel empty with no console error. Always boot the page and assert on the DOM. (6) **`_masterEng` is a top-level `const`, i.e. a global LEXICAL binding — it is NOT on `window`.** `window._masterEng` is `undefined` while the bare `_masterEng` works, so a probe written as `!!window._masterEng` reports "no engine" on a perfectly healthy page (cost a round). Same for anything else declared `const`/`let` at file scope; `_ambAddExtra` and friends ARE on window. (7) **A schema GROUP needs the `open` class, not merely `collapsed` removed** — `.ambient-grp-body` is `display:none` and `.ambient-grp.open > .ambient-grp-body` is what shows it. Un-collapsing the card alone leaves every control 0×0 and `page.click()` fails with "Node is either not clickable"; click the `.ambient-grp-head` (or add `open`) as well. (8) **`grep` treats `17-ambient.js` as BINARY and silently prints nothing** — every search of that file needs `grep -a` (`LC_ALL=C grep -an …`), or you will conclude a function does not exist.

- **A Hugging Face model card's Transformers.js snippet is BOILERPLATE, not a claim that the pipeline works — check the model type against transformers.js's own mapping tables before believing it.** `onnx-community/kitten-tts-nano-0.1-ONNX` is tagged `library_name: transformers.js` and its card shows `pipeline('text-to-speech', …)`, but its `model_type` is `style_text_to_speech_2`, which is absent from `MODEL_FOR_TEXT_TO_WAVEFORM_MAPPING_NAMES` (only vits / musicgen / supertonic) — so `pipeline()` cannot resolve it at all. StyleTTS2 models are driven BY HAND, the way `kokoro-js` does it: `model({input_ids, style: Tensor[1,256], speed: Tensor[1]})` → `{waveform}`, via `StyleTextToSpeech2Model.from_pretrained`. Verify by grepping the mapping table out of `cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@huggingface/transformers@N/dist/transformers.js` — one grep settles what a card cannot. Related: such models eat **IPA phonemes**, not text (the tokenizer vocab is the tell — `ɑ ɐ ɒ æ …`), so a phonemizer is mandatory; and a repo shipping only `onnx/model_quantized.onnx` has no fp32 weights, which rules WebGPU out (its EP won't take int8 matmuls).
- **SPATIALIZE (`layer.spat`) is applied in the CAPTURE-SINK TEE, not in `_ambLayerPan` — and that is the general shape for "an effect that edits a note in flight".** `_ambLayerPan(layer)` cannot host a per-note pan sequence: it receives only the layer (no note time, no layer key, so no ordinal), and it spends a SHARED-STREAM `_ambRand()` draw — removing that draw to override its result would shift every downstream draw and CHANGE THE LAYER'S NOTES the moment you switched panning on. The tee inside `playNote` has key + params + `at`, runs before the voice is built, and already does exactly this for the pitch-echo dry-kill (`params.volume`). So `_ambSpatApply` sets `params.pan` from `_ambCapSink` (before the capture copy, so a freeze stores the position that sounded) AND from `_ambTagSink` (so a frozen loop recomputes it on replay instead of ignoring edits for a whole Write cycle). THREE things to know: (1) the ordinal is MEMOISED BY ONSET TIME, never counted — emitters re-emit the same window every tick and the duplicates are dropped later by `_ambEmitCutoff`, so a bare counter would advance on dropped re-emissions and the sequence would jump; (2) `_ambApplyLayerPan` must force the CORE strip's width param to 1 while Spatialize is on, or the default Spread of 0 collapses every per-note position to mono and the feature appears dead; (3) voices sharing one onset (a chord) share a position by design — per-voice fanning is what Spread does. **TEST TRAP:** a `playNote` wrapper must read `params.pan` AFTER calling through — the tee runs INSIDE `playNote`, so a value read before the call is the pre-effect one (this measured "all pans 0" on a working feature). Its **Positions default is DERIVED on first engage** by `_ambSpatNaturalSteps`: distinct onsets in one unit, from `E.cap[key]` while playing and from `_ambSeedRender`'s offline render while stopped (never the offline one mid-playback — it stubs `Tone.now`/`playNote` globally). Verified: a default euclid bass picks 5, matching its 5-in-8 pattern. A layer that emits NOTHING (a primary that is not `present`, which is every primary in a fresh workspace) has nothing to measure and falls back to 5 — that is the fallback working, not the derivation failing.
- **ANYTHING THAT MUST SURVIVE A PRESS CANNOT LIVE IN `E.seqState` — `_ambResetClocks` empties it (along with `clocks`/`iters`/`arpState`/`runPhase`) on EVERY play.** Sir Eel pre-renders its lines to audio while stopped so play is instant; parked in `seqState` that cache was destroyed by the very press it existed to serve, and the layer silently fell back to on-demand synthesis — measured 4.0 s from press to the first word, against 0.44 s for a Bed's first note in the same run. The layer OBJECT is not the answer either: `persistWorkspace` serialises underscore fields (the same trap that made `_soloLane` reload stuck-muted), so AudioBuffers would land in the saved project. A `WeakMap` keyed on the ENGINE is neither — it outlives every play, dies with the engine, and is never serialised. Inside it the audio is keyed BY LINE, not by whole text, so Add text / a one-sentence edit only pays for what actually changed (verified: appending 4 sentences to 4 reports "Writing 4/8" immediately, not 0/8); the map is pruned to the current lines after each render. DIAGNOSTIC for "why is my layer late": hook `window.playNote` and `createBufferSource().start` together and compare the first event of each against `rawContext.currentTime` at the press — one layer late while another is on time is a layer bug, everything late is engine cold start.
- **HUM-TO-SEQUENCE has TWO implementations behind ONE button, chosen by the TRANSPORT — and they are different features, not duplicates.** (The layer 🎤 was briefly removed 2026-07-31 as a supposed duplicate of Perform and restored 2026-08-01: Perform authors a SEED with the transport stopped, the layer button ALSO records a hum-along over a PLAYING area and locks it as that layer's loop. Only the stopped half overlaps.) The layer's 🎤 (`.ambient-hum-rec`) calls `_ambAuthorTakeToggle(E,key,true)` when STOPPED — Author-in-Grid plus the Perform/Listen mic recorder in `16-grid-controls.js` — and `_ambHumToggle` in 17-ambient when PLAYING. Both poll an analyser at 35 ms and both transcribe through the SAME `_ambHumSegment`, so a fix to the segmenter covers both, but the mic constraints and the start trigger are duplicated per path and must be changed in both. **What was wrong (2026-07-31):** (1) both opened the mic with `noiseSuppression: true` — Chrome's suppressor is built to remove STEADY-STATE signals, which is exactly what a hum is, so it attenuated the take toward silence (the app's own Track recorder already opens the mic clean; these two were the odd ones out). Now `{echoCancellation: true, noiseSuppression: false, autoGainControl: true}` — AEC stays because the take is hummed along with playback and without headphones the tracker would otherwise follow the speakers. (2) TWO hard `rms > 0.02` cliffs: the segmenter's voiced gate (measured: 0.021 transcribes the whole melody, 0.019 transcribes NOTHING) and, worse, the perform path's START trigger, which left `_performStartMs` null so everything hummed afterwards was discarded. Both are now relative to a measured noise floor and **clamped so they can never be stricter than the old constant** — the segmenter uses the take's own 20th-percentile RMS, the live trigger a fall-fast/rise-slow tracker (which MUST be clamped: it seeds from the first frame, so someone already humming when the take arms would otherwise set the floor at hum level and never trigger). Verified: quiet takes now transcribe, room noise and silence still yield zero notes. (3) [this one applied to the since-removed layer button] the PLAYING path gave NO feedback at all between the press and getUserMedia settling — an unanswered or policy-suppressed permission prompt resolves neither way, so the button was indistinguishable from dead ("I press it and nothing happens"). It now toasts on the press itself (measured +1 ms), warns at 6 s if the prompt is still unanswered, and names the rejection (`NotAllowedError` / `NotFoundError` / `NotReadableError` are three different problems with three different fixes, all previously reported as "denied"). Also closed: `body.tracks-fullscreen`'s hide rule lacked the `:not(.bloops-toast)` exemption the `view-*` rules carry, so every toast was invisible in that view — the same defect that once swallowed all guidance on Grow/Listen/Harvest. **TESTING — the mic path IS exercisable headless, with the right flag.** `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream --use-fake-ui-for-media-stream` alone gives a TONE-GENERATING fake mic, and that drives the whole feature end to end (verified: "Hum kept — 7 notes, 7 bar loop" while playing, "Hum translated — 1 note" while stopped). What does NOT work is `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture=<wav>`: it feeds SILENCE regardless of constraints, sample rate (44.1 and 48 kHz both tried) or the `%noloop` suffix — measured maxRms 0 across three constraint sets. So an end-to-end "no melody" result from the FILE flag is a broken rig, not a broken feature (it fooled this author once). For the halves, `window._ambAcfPitch` is exposed and `_ambHumSegment` can be extracted and fed synthetic frames.
- **THE VINYL BED IS A GENERATOR, so it must follow the TRANSPORT and not just its own switch.** Vinyl's wobble / tone-loss / tape stages all PROCESS a signal and are inaudible with nothing playing, but the crackle+hiss bed is a looping `Tone.BufferSource` — gated only on `globalFx.vinylOn`, it kept hissing after Stop ("why do I hear the vinyl effect even while stopped"). `applyMasterVinyl` now computes `bedOn = on && _vinylTransportOn()` and tears the source down (stop +0.25 s behind the existing 0.2 s gain ramp, dispose after) whenever it should not sound; the bed is rebuilt on the next play. `_vinylTransportOn` reads `sequenceTimer !== null` (the idiom the grid modes and Shape already use), `_masterEng.timer`, and any lane engine's timer, all typeof-guarded because this file loads before them. Playback STARTS from several places, so rather than hook each one there is a 500 ms watcher that calls `applyMasterVinyl` only when the answer CHANGES — no ramps or rebuilds while nothing is happening. Measured: stopped 0.000 rms, playing 0.0165, stopped again 0.000, with `_vinylSrc` null after the stop. Any future master stage that GENERATES rather than processes needs the same treatment.
- **THE ROLLING CAPTURE'S RETENTION *IS* THE MAXIMUM LOOP LENGTH, and it now FOLLOWS the longest phrase a project arms instead of being a fixed 33 s.** `E.cap[key]` is where Write/Evolve and Bar Lock recover a phrase from, so a hard `now - 33` prune meant a hard ~31 s ceiling on any loop. Under an active progression the phrase is snapped to whole progression CYCLES, so a project whose cycle runs longer than that — 16 bars at 120 bpm is already 32 s — could not loop AT ALL: every Evolve layer fell back to playing live and toasted about it, repeatedly (the warn-once flag lives on the write state, which resets on stop/play). `_ambCapWant(E, sec)` is called BEFORE a window is armed (the window is captured going FORWARD, which is what makes widening it then sufficient); `_ambCapKeepSec` floors at the old 33 s and ceilings at `_AMB_CAP_MAX_SEC` 180 s; `_ambCapLimitSec` is retention minus a 2 s margin so the oldest note of a just-completed window cannot be pruned in the tick that locks it. Every guard that used to compare against a literal 31 now routes through it — there were SIX (write-bars, prog-cycle, Bar Lock, and three period checks) and they must not drift apart. Verified: a 16-bar phrase at 80 bpm (48 s, formerly refused) loops with 26/28 notes repeating exactly and the badge reading ⟳ 16×2. If a cycle still exceeds the ceiling the toast now names the length, the limit, and the three things that would fix it.
- **SHARED RAMP TARGETS ARE APPENDED AFTER THE AUTO-DERIVE, and used not to be — any layer type that existed ONLY in the schema had no Stereo and no FX ramp targets at all.** The concat of `_AMB_STEREO_RAMP` / `_AMB_SPAT_RAMP` / `_AMB_FX_RAMP` ran over `Object.keys(_AMB_RAMP_PARAMS)` BEFORE the derive pass created entries for schema-only types, so Learn and Sir Eel (and anything else added to the schema without a hand-tuned ramp list) silently lacked Spread/Pan, Reverb send and every FX target. It now appends after the derive, to every type, with an existence check so hand-tuned entries still win. Word music rides a PER-TYPE list (`_AMB_WORD_RAMP`, appended to `learn`/`sireel` only — a Bed has no `word` object): the five NUMERIC axes are targets (Letter A note/octave, Microtonal step, Word chord size, Word note length) while Words-as / Mapping / Word shape stay out, because a ramp between two modes means nothing. Its `word.*` branch only writes the field — the emitter reads `L.word` live when it builds each passage, so there is no node to push to. Verified by sweeping Word note length: the passage's note steps ranged 0.040-0.130 s across one cycle, where a constant setting gives a single step size. Spatialize rides the shared list; its `spat.*` branch in `_ambRampResolve` writes the value AND re-applies `_ambApplyLayerPan`, because the value alone is read per note but the CORE strip's width gate is not — a ramp from width 0 would otherwise open the pan sequence into a strip still summing to mono.
