- **THE VOICE COMES FROM THE SERVER BY DEFAULT NOW, ON EVERY DEVICE (`layer.voiceFrom`: auto | device | server; absent = auto).** The server runs Kokoro-82M (28 voices) where the tab ran Kitten (8), so 'auto' probes the server first and uses it when it answers — a project then sounds the SAME on desktop and phone, and the desktop downloads NOTHING (measured: 0 ML requests where it used to fetch 16). 'device' forces the local engine (offline-capable, instant from cache); 'server' forces the server and, when it is absent, plays words as notes with a status saying so rather than silently becoming the OS voice. THREE traps, each measured: (1) synthesis must AWAIT the in-flight probe (`_ambServerProbe`) — the pump asks for line 0 within a second and beat the ping, downloading the local model on a desktop that was about to use the server; (2) a failed probe MUST cool down (`_AMB_SERVER_COOLDOWN_MS` 60 s) because `_ambLearnWarmUp` runs on every card repaint — an unreachable server produced 3208 pings in 14 s; (3) server-only must return BEFORE `_ambLearnWorkerGet`, or the setting quietly does the opposite of what it says. The picker lists whatever engine is serving (`_ambVoiceChoices` — 36 ids when the server answers, labelled from Kokoro's `af_heart` shape), and `_ambSpokenVoice` validates a server id by PATTERN, never against the live list: normalize runs on load long before any ping, so a list check would wipe a saved server voice every time a project opened. COST: desktop press-to-first-word goes 0.18 s → 0.41 s and a re-render 6 s → 20 s, because rendering now crosses the network — set Voice from to 'This device' to get the old behaviour back.
- **A SCHEMA TOKEN WITH WIRING BUT NO MARKUP IS AN INVISIBLE CONTROL — `spokenvoice` shipped that way from the start.** `['spokenvoice']` was in both spoken layers' Source group and `_ambWireInst` had a full change handler for it, but nobody ever wrote the `if (k === 'spokenvoice') return '<div…>'` branch, so the wiring looked up an element that was never rendered and `layer.voice` could only be set from the console (reported as "where do I select voice in Learn?"). It renders now, in the **Source** group (which starts COLLAPSED — a control there is not missing, it is folded). When adding a schema token, grep for BOTH halves: the renderer branch and the wiring branch. **TEST TRAP:** `persist()` is debounced, so reading `localStorage` immediately after dispatching `change` shows the OLD value — check the live cfg (`_masterEng.getCfg()`) for correctness and re-read storage after a beat for persistence.
- **WORDS-AS IS ABSOLUTE, AND NOTHING LOADS DURING PLAYBACK — both were reported as "the switching is no good".** A speaking layer used to degrade to words-as-notes whenever the voice was unavailable (`_ambWordOutEff`) AND the tick had a second fallback that played notes for any line not yet written, so a layer could change instrument underneath the user twice over. Both are GONE: `_ambWordOutEff` returns exactly what the user chose, and a line with no written audio is simply not played. When speech is impossible the layer SAYS SO (`_ambSpeechBlocked` names the reason) and stays silent — never quietly becomes something else. This deliberately trades away the old "sound within seconds on any device" guarantee for determinism, which is the trade the user asked for; the take is prepared while stopped instead. **The write gate lives INSIDE `_ambSeelPrerender`, not in the tick** — the card-render hook kicks the pump too, so a repaint mid-play started rendering behind the performance (measured: 4 synth requests during playback with the tick-level gate alone). One check at the top of the render section covers every caller. Fetching is likewise gated on `!E.timer`. STATUS is now a state line that names the state and the action: "⚠ Nothing written yet — press ✍ Write all (4 lines). It will be silent until you do." / "◑ 2 of 10 written — the rest stay silent" / "✓ Ready — 4 lines written. Press play." / "⚠ Can't speak — <reason>. Switch Words as → Play, or fix the voice." It reads from the ENTRY when seqState is absent (before the first tick, or right after a play reset), because falling back to "Voice ready" while the take is empty is the same lie in a different costume. Verified: nothing written + play → 0 notes emitted, 0 network calls.
- **THE RENDER-AHEAD CAP APPLIES ONLY WHILE PLAYING — capping it while STOPPED stranded a take at "2 of 10 written" with nothing able to finish it.** The cap exists so a phone or a remote engine is not grinding while audio plays; stopped, there is nothing to protect and every reason to complete the take. Gate it on `E.timer`. Two companion lies fixed in the same report: the status said "Ready — 10 lines. Press play." while eight were unwritten (it now reads "2 of 10 lines written — … Press play, or ✍ Write all", counted from `ent.cache`), and the Voice picker rendered BLANK because the layer's voice was the DEFAULT `''` while the server's list contains no `''` entry — a `<select>` whose value matches no option shows nothing. `_ambVoiceChoices(L)` now guarantees the current value is always present, including the empty default ("Default — whatever the engine picks"). RULE: any select whose options come from a REMOTE list must include the current value explicitly, because the list and the saved value can disagree at first paint.
- **A TAKE CAN BE A WHOLE PARAGRAPH, NOT JUST A SENTENCE (`layer.chunkBy`: 'sentence' | '2'-'12' | 'para'; absent = sentence).** One take is one continuous spoken sample, and how much text goes into it is a musical choice — sentences give short placeable fragments, a paragraph gives narration you can drop in as a block. Chosen in the LOAD POPOVER (so the first split already matches) and on the card (where `_ambResplit` reshapes without refetching — verified 0 network requests across three shapes: 82 takes of ~11 words → 38 of ~23 → 15 of ~59). **PARAGRAPH BREAKS ARE FRAGILE AND TWO LAYERS DESTROYED THEM.** The server's `stripHtml` collapsed all whitespace (fixed: block-closing tags become blank lines BEFORE tags are stripped, lone newlines — Gutenberg's ~70-column wrapping — collapse to spaces, blank lines survive), and then `_ambLearnClean` on the CLIENT collapsed `\s{2,}` and quietly undid it, so paragraph mode silently fell back to sentences with no error anywhere. Both now hold paragraph marks aside (`\u0000`) while collapsing spaces. A source with no paragraphs at all (pasted text, Wikipedia summaries) falls back to sentences rather than producing one unusable block, and `_AMB_TAKE_MAX_WORDS` (90) caps any single take — a ten-minute paragraph is one render that cannot be scheduled, edited or skipped separately. Line length only applies to SENTENCE takes and hides otherwise.
- **HOW MUCH TEXT AND HOW LONG THE LINES ARE ARE NOW CONTROLS, WITH A MEASURED COST READOUT — all three were invisible constants.** They were: Wikipedia's `summary` endpoint (the lead section, whatever length it happens to be), SEP `slice(0,6000)`, Gutenberg `slice(2000,8000)`, and a hard `_AMB_LINE_MAX_WORDS = 14` split cap. Now `layer.amount` is a character budget (paragraph 1200 / few 3000 / page 6000 / everything 24000) applied at a SENTENCE boundary so the last line is never a fragment, and `layer.lineWords` (4-60) is the split cap; both absent by default. **Changing either NEVER refetches** — the raw text is kept on the entry (`ent.raw`) and `_ambResplit` rebuilds the take locally, carrying over the audio of every line whose TEXT is unchanged (the render cache is keyed voice+text, so unchanged lines cost nothing). Verified: 90 lines → 15 → 8 across Amount and Line-length changes with ZERO network requests. The cost line is MEASURED, not guessed: `_ambNoteWriteCost` keeps a rolling seconds-of-work-per-second-of-speech from real renders, so the estimate reflects this device and this engine (a phone on the server and a desktop on its local model differ by an order of magnitude, and a fixed number would lie to one of them) — and it says it is rough until it has written something. The 14-word cap was tuned when every line was synthesised DURING playback; now that lines are written once, long lines are cheap and read better. **TEST TRAP:** `[id$="-amount"]` also matches FX amount sliders — scope control lookups to the layer card or you will read the wrong control's value and think the setting did not apply.
- **EVERY WRITTEN LINE CARRIES ITS OWN SETTINGS (`layer.lineFx["<i>"]`, sparse, absent by default): selection, schedule, and sound.** `on` includes it in the reading, `every`/`off` gate it to every Nth pass, `cue` makes it wait for an explicit cue whatever the layer's Speak mode, and `gain`/`rate`/`start`/`len`/`rev` shape that take alone — the layer's Speech FX still apply ON TOP, so a line is a DIFFERENCE from the layer, not a replacement. `_ambLineSet` drops any value equal to its default and deletes the object when empty, so an unedited layer stores nothing and behaves exactly as before (golden stayed 82/82). Playback threads the line INDEX through the queue (`st.qI`) into `_ambLearnPlay(..., lineIdx)` — without it a buffer cannot know which line it is, since the queue is the only place that mapping survives. Skipped lines advance the cursor WITHOUT sounding, and a guard breaks the loop after one full lap so an all-excluded take cannot spin. UI: each row has a checkbox, ▶ audition, ✎ editor (marked amber when that line differs) and ↻ rewrite; the row shows `/N` or `⊙` when it is gated. **PLACEMENT LESSON: the Speak control was already built and invisible — it sat in the Timing group, which starts COLLAPSED, on a card that also starts collapsed ("I see no speak mode or cue"). It now sits in Source with the take list it governs. A control folded two levels deep is, to a user, a control that does not exist.**
- **A SPOKEN LINE IS A WRITTEN TAKE: written ONCE, then played strictly in order — writing is no longer a playback activity.** Reported as "playback feels non-deterministic… it reloads randomly and skips around", and the skipping was real code: a bootstrap block JUMPED the play cursor to the nearest rendered line whenever the one at the cursor was missing, so the reading silently reordered itself by which lines happened to be written. Gone. A line is now either spoken (written) or played as notes (not written), IN PLACE, and the cursor only ever moves 0,1,2,3. Writing is also decoupled: the tick kicks the pump only to FETCH text or when `layer.liveWrite` is on (off by default), so what is written when you press play is what you hear. The take is visible and actionable — `['lines']` renders every line with its state, duration, ▶ audition and ↻ rewrite, plus **✍ Write all** (which overrides the render-ahead cap via `_writeAllOnce`, because an explicit ask deserves the whole take) and ✕ discard. `_ambLinesSync` repaints that list IN PLACE — a full card re-render would collapse groups and drop focus while the pump works. Verified: Write all reaches 4 of 4, then playback plays 1.27/1.82/2.73/1.52 and repeats that exact order, with ZERO synthesis requests during playback.
- **SPEAK MODE (`layer.speak = {mode, bars}`) decides WHEN a line starts — Flow / Every N bars / Cue.** A spoken layer used to read straight through (each line followed the last, plus Gap), which is a reading rather than an arrangement. `flow` is that original behaviour and is the ABSENT default (`_ambNormalizeSpeak` deletes the object when mode is flow), so every existing project is byte-identical. `every` holds a FIXED cadence — `_ambSpeakSlot` returns the next N-bar grid point after the line's START, so spacing is exact regardless of line length (measured at 120 bpm: 2 bars → deltas that are exact multiples of 4 s, 4 bars → multiples of 8 s; flow gives irregular line-length-driven gaps). `cue` speaks nothing until asked: `st.cue` is a COUNT armed by the card's ▶ button or `window.bloopsSpeakNext(key)` and decremented per line (verified: silent through 8 s, then exactly one line per cue). Both tick paths implement all three — the speech branch AND the words-as-notes branch — so the control means the same thing whichever way the layer is sounding. TEST NOTE: do not infer line onsets from playNote timestamps (a passage is many notes); read `_masterEng.clocks[key]` instead, and remember the tick schedules several slots inside one lookahead, so consecutive samples can differ by a MULTIPLE of the cadence rather than exactly one.
- **WORD LAYERS CAN BAR-LOCK (`layer.snapBars`, 0/1/2/4/8).** A spoken line is as long as it is, so these layers advanced by "however long that took, plus the Gap", which drifts against the grid. `_ambWordSnap` rounds each utterance UP to the next grid point — a short line waits for the next bar, a long one takes as many as it needs, and nothing is cut off because the next start is the first grid point at or after the end. It snaps the FIRST onset and every advance, in BOTH tick paths (spoken and play-only), off `E._barGridAnchor` — the same anchor every synced layer uses, never a private one. Measured at 120 bpm: free ran 0 / 2.44 / 6.44 / 10.44 s, 1-bar lock ran 0 / 4 / 8 / 12.
- **SPEECH IS A SAMPLE (`layer.speech`): trim / chop / order / reverse / rate.** `_ambLearnPlay` schedules the pieces itself and RETURNS the sounding duration, which the tick must use instead of `buf.duration` — chop order (ping-pong) and rate both change how long a line actually runs, and advancing by the raw buffer length would overlap or gap. Reverse reverses the BUFFER once and caches it on the buffer object (`_ambRev`, non-enumerable so it never lands in a save), because playbackRate cannot go negative and reversing per piece per pass would allocate on every line. Shuffle is seeded on the line so a looping phrase scrambles identically each pass rather than becoming a new random event. Absent-by-default: a layer with no `speech` object takes the original single-source path.
- **THE ALPHABET TRANSLATOR (`layer.word`) turns a Word layer's TEXT into notes — three mappings, one contract: letter index in, frequency out.** `_ambAlphaFreq(i, o)`: CHROMATIC = root + i semitones (26 letters span just over two octaves and keep ascending — the shape of the word is the point, so they deliberately do NOT fold into one octave); IN KEY = the i-th note OF THE AREA KEY, so abcd in C major is C D E F; MICROTONAL = i steps of `cents` (46.15 puts the whole alphabet in one octave). `_ambWordEvents` shapes a word (run / chord / chord+run, where the first N letters sound together and the rest run on), `_ambTextPassage` lays words out in time, `_ambEmitWordPassage` plays them through the layer's own chain so they get its instrument, FX, level and Spatialize. **THREE things that bit:** (1) **`_ambNormalizeWord` MUST NOT resolve the key** — normalize runs inside `getCfg()` and the key resolvers call `getCfg()`, so touching the key there is infinite recursion; it hung the app on a single keystroke (a 3-minute CDP timeout) and it must stay a pure coercion. (2) IN KEY anchors its degrees on the KEY ROOT and snaps "A is" up to the nearest key tone — building the scale shape from "A is" instead would play C major inside a D-major area. (3) A **chromatic** area key would make "In key" identical to the chromatic map, so a keyless area reads as major here, or the control would appear to do nothing. `out: 'play'` never opens the voice at all — verified by reloading a stored Play-only project and seeing zero model/worker requests — so a Word layer can be purely instrumental with no download; `'both'` speaks and plays the SAME line at the same instant (the line rides beside its buffer in `st.qL`, because by playback time the queue is the only place that mapping still exists).
- **ONE ARTICLE PER LEARN LAYER.** The pump used to pull in another article whenever the play cursor neared the end of the last one, which made the layer feel like a radio changing station: the subject drifted mid-listen and the layer never settled. `_ambSpokenWantMore` is now simply "have I got nothing at all?" — a layer reads what it was given and loops it, and a new article is an explicit act (the chooser on add, or the card's **New article…** button, which opens the same chooser and replaces that layer's audio). The subject is persisted as `L.article` and shown in the card HEADER (`.ambient-learn-article`), because what a Learn layer IS reading is its identity and the header never said. The chip is `flex: 1 1 0` — flex-basis ZERO is what keeps the head on ONE line, because `.ambient-layer-head` is a WRAPPING flex row (the live-notes line and playhead take their own 100% rows, so it cannot be nowrap) and a wrapping row BREAKS before it shrinks: a chip sized to its text pushed Solo and the caret onto a second line. At basis 0 it contributes nothing to the natural width and simply grows into the slack. `.ambient-toggle` also went 112px → 90px, and a `:has(.ambient-learn-article)` block tightens that head's gutters and lets the unit readout shrink — measured one line at 390 / 520 / 900px, with the title fully readable from 520px up and ellipsed on a phone. `_ambLearnHeadSync` paints both the header chip and the card's Source-row name — the card is re-rendered BEFORE the fetch resolves, so without the second one the name sits one article behind (measured).
- **THE WORD LAYERS NEED THEIR OWN `tone`.** Words-as-Play emits through `_ambMotifParams(..., L.tone)`, but the spoken schemas had no `['tone']` token and no `tone` field, so the played notes were stuck on the default voice with no way to choose. Both now carry `tone: ''` and a Tone select in the Notes group (212 options); ✦ Design was already available from the card's top controls and seeds from that tone, so choose-and-edit are both covered. Verified the emitted `params.type` follows the select (sub → square).
- **A SWINGING AUDIO CLOCK (rate 0.75x then 1.4x+ catch-up) WITH `cost`/`act` AT 1-2 IS CPU STARVATION OF THE RENDER THREAD, NOT A BLOOM BUG — and NOT the voice budget.** Field-reported as "the Learn layer is killing playback, everything cuts out then comes back"; the `__bloomHealthLog` settled it in one paste: the budget never shed (cost 1-2 throughout), output RMS never hit 0, and the CLOCK oscillated 0.748 → 1.486 → 1.0. REPRODUCED by loading every core with busy-loops — min 0.693/max 1.435 with a spoken layer, min 0.865/max 1.199 with NO spoken layer at all (control). So a saturated machine produces this symptom on its own; a spoken layer only adds to the load. Read the log before believing a layer is at fault: **budget shedding shows as `cost` collapsing, starvation shows as the clock swinging with `cost` unchanged.** Two mitigations shipped anyway, both cheap and correct under contention: the pump paces between rendered lines while playing (`_ambPaceSynth`, 260 ms, or 1400 ms when `_ambCtRate < 0.97` — published by the health watchdog), and the worker CHUNKS its inference whenever the main thread flags `busy` (transport playing) so one line is short bursts with yields instead of seconds of uninterrupted core-pegging (`_BUSY_CHUNK_WORDS` 9 on non-WebKit; WebKit's 3 exists for a memory ceiling, not CPU). Neither converts a saturated machine into a healthy one.
- **SYNTHESIS YIELDS TO AUDIO: the voice pump paces itself between lines while the transport is PLAYING, and backs off hard when the audio clock is behind.** Reported as the Learn layer "killing playback off and on — everything cuts out for a while then comes back", which is the adaptive voice budget shedding under a dragging clock and recovering once the load passes; rendering a line is seconds of flat-out inference and a spoken layer renders line after line. `_ambPaceSynth(E)` waits `_AMB_PACE_MS` (260) between rendered lines while `E.timer`, or `_AMB_PACE_SLOW_MS` (1400) when `_ambCtRate < 0.97` — the rate is published by the health watchdog for exactly this. Stopped, it still renders flat out (that is when a layer prepares). Measured: ~3.4 s between renders stopped vs ~3.9 s playing. Rendering ahead is never worth glitching what is already sounding, and words-as-notes covers a line that is not ready. The JSONP decode (main thread, no worker) also yields every 32k samples — a 15 s line is ~700 KB of base64 and one straight pass is an audible glitch. NOTE: the cut-out could NOT be reproduced on the dev Mac (no stalls, no main-thread blocks, no voice overlap, correct spacing) — this is a principled fix for the mechanism, not a confirmed repro.
- **THE VOICE SERVER LIVES IN ITS OWN REPO — github.com/mikebluz/bloops-tts-server (private) — deployed via GoDaddy Node.js hosting (airoapp.ai URL), NOT via deploy.sh.** Phones fetch speech from it; the endpoint resolves in `_ambTtsUrl` as localStorage `bloopsTtsUrl` (per-device) → `window.BLOOPS_TTS_URL` (site-wide, in bloops.html beside the asset stamp, currently the airoapp URL) → same-origin `/tts`. The server is PURE WASM by construction: transformers.js eagerly imports `onnxruntime-node` and `sharp` (both native; a missing prebuilt binding is a hard crash AT IMPORT), so `apply-stub.cjs` (postinstall) overwrites every installed copy — onnxruntime-node's stub RE-EXPORTS onnxruntime-web so transformers' ordinary Node path runs unchanged (an empty stub fails differently: the device-validation list comes back empty and every device name is rejected). Node ESM refuses ort-web's default wasm locator — `wasmPaths` must be a file:// URL into the local dist. It warms the model at boot; disk cache is best-effort; bare GET / answers 200 (a 4xx there made the platform health check restart the app every ~60 s); PORT is read INLINE in listen() because the deploy checker is a static scan that cannot follow a variable. Verify a server deploy with two curls (ping + a WAV) — no phone in the loop.
- **WHEN THE SCRIPT TRANSPORT COMES UP, RE-KICK EVERY SPOKEN LAYER AND CLEAR ITS `novoice` — otherwise the transport works and the layers stay dead.** Found in PRODUCTION, never in a local test: on iOS the direct fetch fails, the pump had already asked for a line so that synth fails too and parks the layer on `phase='novoice'`, and the JSONP ping succeeds a moment LATER — by which time nothing restarts the pump and the status still reads "Voice unavailable — no connection?" on a phone whose server was answering perfectly. The jsonp-engage branch now clears `novoice`/`nofetch`, re-kicks `_ambSeelPrerender` for every spoken layer, and repaints all statuses; `_ambLearnStatusText` also drops a stale `novoice` whenever `_ambTtsJsonpMode` is on. LESSON: an async capability that arrives after a failure must undo the failure's state, not just set its own flag. **PRODUCTION-VERIFICATION RECIPE that caught it:** point puppeteer at the LIVE site with a desktop UA and an iPhone UA and count requests by host — desktop should show ML downloads and ZERO server calls, iPhone the reverse. Then inject the JSONP script tag by hand in the live page to separate "transport blocked" (CSP, proxy) from "our orchestration is wrong".
- **GODADDY'S FRONT PROXY STRIPS `access-control-allow-origin` FROM EVERY RESPONSE — the voice rides a JSONP transport when fetch fails, because script execution is CORS-exempt by design.** Diagnosed by curl: the app's other headers flow through (its Vary merges) while ACAO specifically vanishes; echoing the origin instead of '*' changes nothing; the proxy answers preflight OPTIONS itself with a bare 204 granting nothing. The server's `&cb=name` returns the same data as `application/javascript` wrapped in the callback (audio as base64, ~33% wire tax); the client (`_ambTtsJsonp`/`_ambWavB64ToF32`, main thread) engages it only after the worker's warm reports 'noserver' — one JSONP ping flips `_ambTtsJsonpMode`, every later synth bypasses the worker, and `_ambWordOutEff` + the status treat jsonp-mode as voice-OK regardless of stale worker errors. Hosts with working CORS never pay the tax. Test locally with the server's `TTS_NO_CORS=1` knob. Verified: speech through the bus against a zero-ACAO server; desktop untouched.
- **MOBILE TTS — SOLVED 2026-08-02: the `phonemizer` package was the whole failure, and it is REPLACED with `@echogarden/espeak-ng-emscripten` (real WASM).** The old package (espeak via wasm2js — one giant JS function) HANGS in WebKit: worker or main thread, alone on a page, never returns — and poisons the process so ort dies beside it with JSC's opaque "Internal error"/"RangeError". Every iOS browser is WebKit under Apple's mandate (Chrome included), which is why it read as "mobile-only" while Blink desktops worked. The model and ort-web were always innocent (they load fine in WebKit alone). The replacement drives `new m.eSpeakNGWorker()` → `set_voice('en-us')` → `synthesize_ipa(text).ipa`, output normalized by stripping `_` separators and collapsing whitespace — byte-identical to the old package's IPA (verified across test sentences; one dictionary-version vowel nuance in number expansion, e.g. fˈoːɹɾi→fˈɔːɹɾi). Warmed in `buildKitten`'s `Promise.all` so the first line pays nothing. Verified: Chrome regression unchanged (0.44/0.18 s press-to-first-word); REAL worker on desktop Safari synthesizes 3.8 s of speech — the first WebKit synthesis ever in this codebase. **iOS DOES NOT ATTEMPT IN-TAB INFERENCE AT ALL — words-as-notes by POLICY (`_AMB_IOS`, 17-ambient), decided 2026-08-02 after every rationing scheme still crashed the real app.** The decisive fact: the model runs in an EMPTY iOS tab (measured on the user's own phone — warm, chunked synthesis, audible speech) but NOT inside this app, whose baseline (Tone, the DSP worklet, samplers, 2 MB of UI) already occupies the tab's memory budget; the margin the probes measured never existed in context. PROBE CONTEXT ≠ APP CONTEXT is the transferable lesson. No worker is created, nothing downloads, the status says "The voice needs more memory than a phone browser tab gets", zero crashes possible. Desktop untouched. Escape hatch: localStorage `bloopsVoiceForceIOS='1'`. The chunking / yields / lookahead rationing below stays (it is what makes desktop-Safari WebKit work, and the escape hatch usable); the strike latch stays as the backstop for non-iOS WebKit. If mobile speech is ever wanted for real, the routes are SERVER-SIDE synthesis (the only guaranteed one — one /tts endpoint, and verification stops involving the phone: a curl proves the whole mobile path) or the Sir Eel word-sprite pack (finite vocabulary pre-rendered per word, no inference; Learn's open vocabulary cannot use it). **WEBKIT ALSO RENDERS ONLY ~2 LINES AHEAD OF THE READING (`_AMB_WK_AHEAD`), NOT THE WHOLE TEXT.** A Learn ARTICLE is ~10 lines and each line is several chunks, so the pump's eager mode — right on desktop, and the reason a fetched article is instant there — runs dozens of WASM inferences back to back on a phone and kills the tab ("crashes while attempting to write 0/10 lines"). Sustained pressure is a different failure from the one-off synthesis that measured fine. So on WebKit the pump stops once a couple of lines past the play cursor are audio, the tick re-kicks it as the cursor advances, and words-as-notes covers the rest; the worker also yields ~40 ms between chunk inferences to give the runtime a window to release intermediates. Measured on an 8-line text: iPhone UA synthesizes 3 lines, desktop UA all 9. **WEBKIT SYNTHESIZES IN ≤3-WORD CHUNKS (`_WEBKIT` + `_chunkText`; 3 not 4 — on-device the boundary sat between 3 words surviving and ~8 killing the tab), concatenated with ~4 ms edge fades — because an ~8-word inference KILLS THE iOS TAB (reproduced 3x on-device with telemetry: 3 words fine at ~2.2x realtime, 8 words = tab reload), and because JSC's inference cost is SUPER-LINEAR in input length, so chunking is also a ~5x SPEEDUP (desktop Safari: 27 s → 5.0 s for ~4 s of speech — near-realtime).** The cost is choppier prosody (each piece carries its own contour), paid on WebKit only; Blink synthesizes whole lines untouched. iOS-probe lesson: an AudioContext must be created INSIDE a user tap or iOS mutes it, and a LAN probe receiver must bind 0.0.0.0, not 127.0.0.1, or the phone's telemetry silently vanishes. **THE VOICE LATCH IS STRIKE-BASED AND ALWAYS RECOVERABLE — v1 was a one-crash PERMANENT kill with no way back, and that shipped as "TTS is effectively not working on mobile".** Two errors: iOS discards backgrounded tabs routinely for reasons unrelated to the voice, so ONE unclean shutdown is not evidence of anything; and a switch the user cannot flip is a dead end, not a safety feature. Now an unclean shutdown during voice work is a STRIKE (`bloopsVoiceCrashLatch` = a count), the voice keeps trying until `_AMB_LATCH_AT` (2), and the legacy permanent `'1'` flag is read as one strike so already-stuck devices recover on update. `_ambVoiceRetry()` clears the count, drops the worker, re-warms and re-renders — reachable from the layer ⋯ menu ("⟳ Try the voice again", shown only while set aside), named in the status line, and on `window.bloopsVoiceRetry`. Verified across five isolated storage states. **ALSO (same report):** iOS ZOOMS the page when a focused input's font-size is under 16px and never zooms back — every field in `.amb-learn-pick`/`.step-div-modal`/the Sir Eel text row is pinned to 16px; and the getting-ready popover used to vanish on any of three unannounced conditions ("non-deterministic as to when or why it closes") — every exit now names itself and lingers 1.4 s, with the guard placed BEFORE the status repaint or the next tick overwrites the reason. **ONE-CRASH LATCH — original mechanism (`_ambVoiceLatched`/`_ambVoiceMark`, 17-ambient):** every voice-work await is bracketed by a localStorage 'inflight' marker; a BOOT that finds the marker means the tab died mid-voice-work, and `bloopsVoiceCrashLatch` permanently retires the voice on that device — layers fall to words-as-notes with a status naming it. `pagehide` clears the marker so closing the app mid-render never false-trips (a real crash fires no pagehide — WHICH ALSO MEANS a reload cannot simulate a crash in tests; plant the marker via `evaluateOnNewDocument` in a fresh browser instead). Both guards sit BEFORE `_ambLearnWorkerGet` so a latched device never even downloads the worker. Final on-device state: chunked synthesis measured WORKING on the phone (4.2 s of speech in 5.4 s); the remaining crash was in the probe page's un-gestured playback, and if any device ever does crash in the app, it costs one crash, once. **DIAGNOSTIC PROTOCOL that ended weeks of blind deploys, reusable for any iPhone-only bug:** a self-reporting probe page (prints on-screen + POSTs to a scratchpad receiver on `location.hostname:3002`), opened in desktop Safari via `open -a Safari` as the WebKit proxy, and on a same-LAN phone via the Mac's IP.
- **A SPOKEN LAYER NOW MAKES SOUND WITHIN SECONDS ON ANY DEVICE, BY CONSTRUCTION — un-rendered lines play as NOTES, and speech takes over per line as audio arrives.** The design flaw behind every "not working on mobile" report: the notes fallback engaged only on a TERMINAL worker error, and a real phone's failure modes (slow download, ort hanging, dying without an error event) can delay that signal for minutes or never fire it — the layer sat silent the whole time. The tick's `if (!st.q.length) break` wait is now: given TEXT, after a 2.5 s grace (so a mid-article render hiccup on a healthy desktop stays a wait, not a ding), the next line plays through the alphabet translator and the reading moves on; the status says "♪ Words as notes meanwhile…". THREE pieces had to move together, each found by tracing, not reasoning: (1) **the pump must re-resolve `st` EVERY loop pass** — it outlives the play press and `_ambResetClocks` replaces the seqState object, so a captured `st` is an ORPHAN: the pump could neither see the play cursor nor report to the state the tick reads (its status writes only LOOKED right because `say()` writes the DOM directly); (2) **the pump renders AT the play cursor**, not top-down — otherwise it forever finishes lines the reading already passed; (3) **the hand-off is inverted: speech JUMPS to the nearest rendered line** and continues in order — cursor-chasing alone never converges when synthesis is slower than a passage (trace-proven: 30 s, 3 lines rendered, zero speech). Verified: never-replying worker → notes at 3.1 s; 10 s-delayed synth → notes at 2.6 s, speech takes over at 31 s and proceeds in order; healthy desktop byte-identical timings (0.45/0.17 s, no notes). The `_ambWordOutEff` terminal degrade stays for the fast-fail case.
- **A DEAD VOICE DEGRADES TO PLAY MODE — words become notes, never a worse voice and never a dead card.** An eSpeak (mespeak) fallback engine was built, verified end to end (asm.js, ~700 KB, PCM through the bus), and REMOVED the same day: the formant voice failed by ear, and a fallback the user mutes is not a fallback (the known-working shape is recorded in a tombstone comment in learn-voice.worker.js if a guaranteed in-graph voice is ever wanted). Instead `_ambWordOutEff(L)` degrades a speech mode to 'play' at RUNTIME when the worker has terminally failed and no usable OS voice exists (iOS: its speechSynthesis seizes the audio session and replaces the mix, so `_ambSysVoiceOK` is false on iPhone/iPad) — the alphabet translator plays the words through the layer's own chain, the status says "Voice can't run here — playing the words as notes", and the persisted `word.out` is NEVER written, so the same project speaks again on a desktop. Verified with a dead worker + iPhone UA: popover closed at 1.1 s, 91 notes through the bus, setting untouched. **TEST TRAP:** Puppeteer request interception does NOT reliably see a dedicated worker's fetches — blocking a host at page level left the worker's requests stalling un-continued and produced a false "cascade broken"; simulate engine failure by patching the worker SOURCE into a blob URL (swap `window.Worker` for app-level tests), never by intercepting worker network.
- **SYSTEM-VOICE FALLBACK: when the WASM voice terminally fails, spoken layers speak through the OS instead of going dead.** `_ambSysVoiceActive(L)` = worker error + `speechSynthesis` present + the layer wants speech. The tick branch drives SpeechSynthesis directly — no buffers, no queue: the utterance starts when its slot is due and the clock advances on its END (`_ambWordSnap`-snapped), because an OS utterance's duration is unknowable up front; a failsafe timer bounds a lost 'end' event (the hang bug in a third costume). THREE platform rules bought with blood: (1) iOS licenses speak() only from a user gesture, so `_ambSysVoiceUnlock` speaks an inaudible utterance on the PLAY PRESS; (2) the OS voice is a GENERATOR outside the graph — gates and the master fade cannot touch it, so `_ambStopGenerator` must `speechSynthesis.cancel()` (the vinyl-bed rule); (3) it cannot enter Web Audio, so no FX and no capture — it is a fallback, never the path, and "Both" still emits its note passage through Web Audio, which works everywhere. **TEST TRAP:** `window.speechSynthesis = stub` is silently IGNORED in Chrome (accessor without a usable setter) while `window.SpeechSynthesisUtterance = fn` IS honoured — the half-installed stub sent fake utterances to the NATIVE speak, which threw and read as total silence, a false "feature broken". Stub via `Object.defineProperty(window, 'speechSynthesis', {value})` and assert `getVoices()` returns the stub's data before trusting the run.
- **PHONES GET 1 WASM THREAD, DECIDED BEFORE ANY ORT INIT — the ladder alone cannot save an iOS that has already thrown.** Threaded ort-web needs SharedArrayBuffer (absent without cross-origin isolation) and some builds THROW the RangeError while probing instead of falling back; worse, ort-web LATCHES its init promise, so once rung 1 rejects, later rungs inherit the same rejection. `learn-voice.worker.js` therefore sets `env.backends.onnx.wasm.numThreads = 1` at module top when `typeof SharedArrayBuffer === 'undefined'` — a no-op on desktop (without SAB ort runs single-threaded anyway), the documented cure on iOS.
- **"no available backend found. ERR: [wasm] RangeError…" is onnxruntime-web failing to START, not the model failing to download.** Reported from a phone. The WASM runtime could not allocate its heap; the knobs that matter are the thread count (a threaded build needs SharedArrayBuffer, absent without cross-origin isolation, and asking for it can fail outright) and SIMD. `build()` now walks a LADDER — default → 1 thread → 1 thread + no SIMD — so a capable device keeps the fast path and a constrained one still gets a voice; each failed rung is posted to the console with its full reason, and the winning rung is reported in the `backend` message. Verified by forcing the first rung to throw that exact RangeError: it fell through to "1 thread" and warmed successfully. **Two reporting bugs made this worse than it was:** the status truncated the reason at a hard `slice(0, 90)`, cutting the one useful string mid-word ("ERR: [wasm] RangeEr") — it now trims on a word boundary at 150 and always logs the full text; and "Voice unavailable" alone left a dead-looking card while Words-as-Play sits right there needing no model at all, so the message now names that way out.
- **EVERY AWAIT ON THE VOICE WORKER NEEDS A DEADLINE TOO — `postMessage` has no timeout, and a worker that dies quietly leaves the promise pending forever.** Reported from a phone as Sir Eel stuck on "Writing 0/4 lines"; same defect as the un-timed fetch, one thread over. `_ambLearnAwait(id, ms)` resolves null on expiry and unregisters the pending entry. The FIRST line queues behind the model download INSIDE the worker, so it gets the warm budget (240 s) rather than the per-line one (90 s) — otherwise a slow phone trips a false "the voice stopped responding" while the download is perfectly healthy. **And a silent wait is indistinguishable from a hang, which is what both mobile reports actually were:** the worker now posts model-download percentage (`progress_callback`, aggregated across files) and the status shows it; while a line is being written the status counts the seconds, so slow reads as slow and frozen reads as stuck; and if NO download progress has arrived after 25 s the status says the voice never responded, because a healthy download reports within a second or two however slow the link. Verified on a throttled connection: `Downloading the voice… 96% → 100%` then `Writing 0/4 lines… (46s)` climbing; and with a stubbed never-replying worker, the no-response message at 27 s.
- **EVERY LEARN NETWORK CALL NEEDS A DEADLINE — a bare `await fetch()` is how the layer hung on mobile.** `fetch` has no timeout of its own, so on a stalled connection the promise never settles: the pump sat awaiting it with its `running` latch held and the layer showed "Fetching an article…" forever, with no path back (reported from a phone). `_ambFetchJson` wraps both the article and the search call in an `AbortController` (12 s / 9 s) and records WHY it failed, because "timed out", "no connection" and "no such article" are three different problems and only the last one is about the search term. One retry, and only for timeout/offline — a phone changing cell drops exactly one request; a 404 is not retried. **A terminal text failure now OUTRANKS the voice-download message** in `_ambLearnStatusText`: warming otherwise masked it, so a dead layer looked like a busy one. Verified by swallowing the request entirely (never respond, never fail): 2 attempts, then "Wikipedia didn't answer (timed out) — tap 📄 New article to try again" in both the popover and the card, where before it hung indefinitely.
- **ASKING FOR A SECOND ARTICLE HUNG ON "Fetching an article…" FOREVER, AND IT WAS NEVER A NETWORK BUG — the pump decides whether to fetch from the ENTRY, and `_ambLearnLoadNow` was only resetting `st`.** With `ent.lines` still holding article #1 and the source key unchanged (Random → Random is the same key), the pump skipped its fetch branch entirely, so the `st.phase = 'fetch'` that Load had just set was never cleared by anybody: the status read "Fetching an article…" permanently and NO request was made — which is exactly what a stalled network looks like, and is why the timeout work (correct, and verified separately) did nothing for it. `_ambLearnLoadNow` now invalidates the held article — but ONLY when there is one: the chooser's `load()` calls `_ambRenderExtras` first and the card's hook starts the pump, so invalidating unconditionally threw away an in-flight article and fetched a second (measured: 2 requests → 4 for a single press, back to 2 once scoped). Invalidation bumps `ent.gen`, which the pump checks everywhere it checks the source key (`stale()`) so a pump already in flight bails and re-kicks in its `finally`; `ent.buf` is deliberately kept, being audio keyed by voice+line. Plus a belt-and-braces line: a `'fetch'` phase may never outlive having text. **The lesson: "no status updates" means the status is TRUE and nothing is moving it — look for who owns the state the status reads, not at the network.** Diagnose with a request log — zero requests is the tell that separates this from a genuine stall.
- **LEARN READS FROM THE STANFORD ENCYCLOPEDIA AND PROJECT GUTENBERG TOO — through the voice server, because neither host sends CORS headers.** Verified by curl: `plato.stanford.edu` and `gutenberg.org` both answer 200 with NO access-control-allow-origin, so a browser can never read them directly (only `gutendex.com` metadata allows it). The server's `GET /source?kind=sep|gutenberg&term=…` fetches and strips them, JSONP-capable like everything else; the client tries a plain fetch first and falls back to the script transport, the same two-step the voice takes. Hosts are ALLOWLISTED there on purpose — an open URL proxy is an abuse vector. In the chooser they are two buttons under the search box, which doubles as their term (type "free will", tap Philosophy). TWO things this exposed: a SEP entry is 85-95 LINES, so the render-ahead cap now applies whenever a line costs a NETWORK round trip (`_AMB_WK || _ambTtsJsonpMode`), not only on WebKit — otherwise a fetched entry fires ninety-odd render requests before a word is spoken; and a JSONP callback must NOT be deleted on timeout — a slow reply arriving afterwards then executes against nothing and throws a ReferenceError into the page (seen with Gutenberg), so `finish()` leaves an inert stub and sweeps it two minutes later.
- **THE LEARN CHOOSER SEARCHES FIRST, THEN YOU PICK.** Typing a title and hoping it is the exact page name failed silently on near-misses, so `_ambLearnSearch` hits `https://<host>/w/rest.php/v1/search/title` (CORS-clean on every Wikimedia host offered, and it returns a short description per hit, which is what makes a result list readable) and the modal shows the hits and CHOOSING IS LOADING — tapping a result loads it, and there is no Load button, because a two-step select-then-confirm only adds a click and a state to get wrong. Random loads a random article the same way. Enter SEARCHES (the only thing it could sensibly do once loading happens by choosing). Verified end to end: choosing the second hit for "xylophone" requested `69_Love_Songs` and that title is what reached the header, the card and `L.article`.
- **MODAL TEXT MUST WRAP: `.ambient-hint` is `white-space: nowrap`.** That is right in a card row (auto-sized column) and wrong inside `.step-div-modal`, which is `width: min(360px, 92vw)` — the hint just runs out the side, which is what "the text overflows the popover" was. Both spoken modals now set `white-space: normal` on hints and the status line plus `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, because the longest strings are not ours to write (an article title, a status line naming one). The results list is `max-height: 34vh; overflow-y: auto` so eight hits cannot push the buttons off screen. Any new dialog reusing `.ambient-hint` needs the same override.
- **A NEW LEARN LAYER ASKS BEFORE IT FETCHES — and "waiting to be asked" is a WeakSet, never a field.** `_ambLearnSourceModal` offers Library / Article / Random / Load on add; the pump returns early while `_ambLearnPickPending.has(L)`. It must NOT be a layer field: `persistWorkspace` serialises underscore fields too, so a persisted "not chosen yet" flag would make every SAVED project stop fetching on load. Cancel means "not now" — the layer stays idle until an explicit release (play, or any of the card's own Random/Search/Load, both of which delete it from the set). **TWO ordering bugs, both measured:** (1) `_ambRenderExtras` wires the card and the card's hook kicks the pump, so marking the layer pending AFTER that render let a random article fetch start **840 ms before the user was asked** — mark it first; (2) that stray fetch then held the pump's `running` latch, so the real Load returned immediately and, when the stale fetch resolved against a changed key, the pump bailed with nobody to restart it and the layer stranded on "Fetching an article…" forever. The pump now RE-KICKS itself whenever it exits on a changed key — that is the general fix for any setting changed mid-fetch (voice, term, text), not just this flow. Also: only a `wiki-random` source accumulates further articles; a NAMED article is what the user asked for, and appending random ones behind it is the layer overriding a specific choice (a short article trips the 3-line lookahead immediately, so it happened at once).
- **ONE PUMP renders every spoken layer now — fetched articles included — and the tick NEVER synthesises.** `_ambSeelPrerender` (still the old name) does three jobs: FETCH an article when there is nothing left to say, RENDER lines IN ORDER so playback can start the moment line 0 exists and the rest arrive while it speaks, and stop at `_AMB_SEEL_CACHE_SEC` (300 s) of audio. Fetched lines ACCUMULATE — `ent.lines` grows across articles and a new one is only fetched when the play cursor is within `_AMB_SPOKEN_LOOKAHEAD` (3) lines of the end — so a layer builds a library instead of discarding each article. At the cap it sets `ent.full`, truncates to the rendered prefix and loops what it has; `_ambSpokenWantMore` returns false from then on, which is what "stop loading new articles" means. Because the cache is the engine WeakMap, everything rendered SURVIVES STOP and a fresh press restarts at line 0 (`st.ci` lives in seqState, which the play reset clears). A line is rendered at most once per voice — the map is keyed voice+text — so a repeated article costs nothing. **THE GATE THAT BIT:** the card-render hook kicked the pump only when `_ambSpokenLocalText(inst) !== null`, so a fetched Learn layer stayed idle until the first press and the "getting ready" popover sat on "Voice ready" with nothing behind it. It must fire for every speaking layer. Verified end to end: popover closes at 10.6 s when line 1 is audio, playback then reports `Speaking 3/12 — <article> · writing 6/12` (a second article fetched and rendering ahead), and a stop→play replays the identical opening lines. NOT exercised end-to-end: the 300 s cap itself (that is ~5 minutes of synthesis to reach) — its helpers are unit-tested and the truncation branch is read-only-verified.
- **LOCAL TEXT vs FETCHED is the axis that decides whether a spoken layer can pre-render.** `_ambSpokenLocalText(L)` returns Sir Eel's generated text and Learn's PASTED text (null for a fetched article), and everything downstream branches on it: `_ambSeelPrerender` renders the whole thing before the press, the tick replays from the cache instead of synthesising, and `_ambLearnLoadNow` renders all of it rather than one line ahead. The VOICE is part of the cache key (`_ambSpokenSrcKey`) and of the per-line map key — the same words in another voice are different audio, and the eight KittenTTS voices are style vectors against ONE model, so switching costs a re-render but never a reload. **RACE that cost a debugging pass:** the Paste Load button fires immediately after the textarea's own `change`, so two calls arrive for the same text; the second cleared `st.sentences` while the first was mid-render, and the finished render then reported "Voice ready — press play" against 0 lines. Any early-out in `_ambSeelPrerender` must therefore RESTORE `st.sentences` from `ent.lines`, not just return. Same class: `_ambLearnWarmUp` used to write the status with `st = null`, so every card re-render clobbered "Ready — …, N lines" — it now reports against the layer's real state.
- **LINES, NOT SENTENCES: `_ambSpokenLines` splits a long sentence at clause boundaries before synthesis.** A line costs ~0.9 s + about its own spoken length, and the FIRST line's cost is pure silence — nothing is playing yet to hide it. A 25-word encyclopedia opener is ~15 s of speech; split at commas/semicolons/conjunctions it starts speaking in about half that. Splitting NEVER happens mid-clause, and a clause that is still enormous is left alone rather than hard-cut at a word count (a butchered phrase sounds worse than a long one); pieces shorter than the limit are re-joined so the reading does not stutter into 2-word fragments.
- **COOP/COEP was MEASURED and deliberately NOT adopted (2026-07-31).** Cross-origin isolation does work here — a bare page with `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` + `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: credentialless` reports `crossOriginIsolated: true`, gets `SharedArrayBuffer`, and synthesises **~1.35x faster** (ratio 0.74→0.57 and 0.70→0.51 on the same two lines). That is a real gain but a modest one, and the cost is page-wide: COEP blocks every cross-origin subresource that does not opt in, and COOP severs `window.opener`, which is how Google Identity Services' OAuth popup talks back. Trading Drive sign-in for 35% off one optional layer's synthesis is not a good trade. Revisit only if the app stops depending on third-party auth in that page.
- **SIR EEL'S VOCABULARY IS ~5900 WORDS (was 399, 15x) AND EVERY ADDITION WAS MACHINE-CHECKED against the generator's own rules before landing.** The banks cannot be extended by taste alone: `_seelS` spells plurals and 3rd-person as sibilant→+es, consonant+y→+ies, else +s, and `_seelArt` reads the FIRST LETTER, so anything the rules would spell wrong is a grammar bug in someone's ear. The validator that gated this (scratch, reproducible) rejects: irregular plurals (cactus, ox, salesman, criterion…), `-o`→`-oes` (cargo, volcano, tornado), `-f/-fe`→`-ves` (wolf, shelf, knife), article traps where the letter disagrees with the sound (hour, university, unique, once), and irregular 3rd persons (be/have/do/go). It dropped 45 of ~5800 drafted words. A SECOND pass removed 80 mass and pair-only nouns that had leaked into `count` — the tell was "many hockeys" in generated output, since a count noun must survive pluralisation AND "many". Verified over 1200 generated sentences: 0 "a" before a vowel, 0 "an" before a consonant, 0 double spaces, 3831 distinct words in use. Closed classes CANNOT scale — prep 51, sub 20, conj 8 — English has no more of them; the variety comes from the open classes multiplying.
- **SPOKEN LAYERS (Learn, Sir Eel) are ONE engine with different text sources — adding another source is a branch, not a layer.** They share `_ambTickLearnLayer` (queue two lines ahead, speak, gap), `_ambLearnSynth` (the worker), `_ambLearnPlay` (buffer → the layer's own chain, so speech gets the layer's FX and is captured) and the status line; what differs is one branch in `_ambLearnAhead` that produces `{title, text}`. Three things must stay in step for a new source: `_ambSpokenType`/`_ambSpokenKey`/`_ambSpokenPrefix` (the engine key is `<type>:<id>` and the card ids are `ambient-<type>-<id>-…`, so a bare `'learn:'+id` anywhere is a bug), `_ambSpokenSrcKey` (what invalidates a queued reading — Sir Eel compares the TEXT, since an edit can keep the same length), and the tick's `ex.type === 'learn' || ex.type === 'sireel'` dispatch. Sir Eel's generator (`_ambSeelText`) rolls on `Math.random` at UI time and never touches `_ambRand`, so note generation is untouched and the invariant harness stays green; its `text` default is EMPTY in `_ambDefaultLayer` (which the harness builds from directly) and the opening text is rolled in `_ambAddExtra`, the same split as `_ambEuclidStochasticInit`.
- **A `new Worker(url)` is a cached asset like any other and needs the `?v=DEPLOYVER` stamp — but the URL lives in JS, so nothing stamps it unless that JS file is added to deploy.sh's list AND force-put.** `js/bloops/learn-voice.worker.js` is loaded from `17-ambient.js`, so 17-ambient.js now carries the token and is in both the sed loop and the explicit `put -O` block (a stamp-only change is the same byte length, and the `mirror --ignore-time` compares by SIZE — the long-standing frozen-stamp trap). Without this a phone keeps running the previously cached worker after a deploy, which for the Learn layer meant the old 60 MB model that crashes the tab. Same rule for any future worklet/worker/wasm URL built in JS.
